# Decision log — CAOS v2 → Databricks App rebuild

Append-only. Later entries override earlier ones. Format: `Dn (date) — decision. Alternatives. Reason. Evidence.`
The builder appends `Fn` entries (finding, action, evidence) below the spec-author section.

## Spec-author run, 2026-09-22

### Inputs as resolved

### Research findings applied (Step 1)

Each finding names its source and where it landed. Findings that changed nothing are omitted.

- R1 — Anthropic, "Prompting Claude Fable 5.1" (https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/prompting-claude-fable-5-1): early stopping ("Next, I'll…", asking permission) is the named failure; the two supplied autonomy blocks fix it and the opening sentence "You are operating autonomously…" must be kept as written. Applied: spec §1 opens with that sentence verbatim; goal condition restates it.
- R2 — same page: Fable 5.1 writes fewer progress updates during long tool turns and tends to whole-file rewrites and scope creep (extra tests, nearby fixes). Applied: spec §1 requires a self-contained closing recap, targeted edits, "no scratch checks promoted to permanent tests", and the tool-batching sentence.
- R3 — Anthropic, "Prompting Claude Fable 5" (https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/prompting-claude-fable-5): "Before reporting progress, audit each claim against a tool result from this session… if tests fail, say so with the output" nearly eliminated fabricated status. Applied: spec §1 and the goal require evidence copied from tool output of the final turn; no context-budget counts are surfaced; verification by fresh-context subagent is allowed but the acceptance commands must run in the main transcript.
- R4 — Anthropic best practices (https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/claude-prompting-best-practices): "It is unacceptable to remove or edit tests"; tests verify, they do not define the solution. Applied: spec §7 gate-integrity rules and `scripts/check_gate_config.py`.
- R5 — Claude Code `/goal` docs (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/goal): evaluator is the small fast model, sees only the transcript, cannot run tools; condition ≤4,000 chars; recommended shape = one measurable end state, a stated check, constraints, a turn clause; background subagents defer evaluation; `claude -p "/goal …"` runs to completion; introduced v2.1.139. Applied: goal file structure; evidence must be printed in the assistant text of the final turn; turn bound "or stop after 300 turns".
- R6 — Headless docs (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless): `-p` starts in Manual; pass `--permission-mode auto`; `--permission-prompts none` needs v2.1.259+; `--bare` skips hooks (and `/goal` needs hooks); `claude -p --continue` must pass `--permission-mode auto` again. Applied: launcher unchanged (already does this); blocker B3 records the installed version 2.1.246.
- R7 — Permission modes (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/auto-mode-config): headless auto mode never stops the run on repeated denials; the classifier does not read tool results, so name destructive steps explicitly; broad `Bash(*)` allows are dropped in auto mode, narrow ones stay. Applied: `.claude/settings.json` lists narrow allow rules for every command the build needs and denies push/destroy/delete.
- R8 — Databricks Apps dependencies (https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/databricks-apps/dependencies): `requirements.txt` always wins and forces pip + Python 3.11; with no `requirements.txt` and both `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` present the app uses uv and honours `requires-python`; `.python-version` is not documented. Applied: spec §3 forbids a `requirements.txt` at the repo root and pins `requires-python = ">=3.13,<3.14"` plus `.python-version`.
- R9 — Migrate-agent page (https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/agents/custom-agents/migrate-agent-to-apps): without an upper bound the Apps build image "can pick a newer Python (such as 3.14)". Applied: upper bound `<3.14` so the deployed interpreter is the one the gates ran on.
- R10 — `databricks-langchain` (https://github.com/databricks/databricks-ai-bridge, PyPI 0.20.0): `from databricks_langchain import ChatDatabricks`; required field `endpoint`; `CheckpointSaver(PostgresSaver)` / `AsyncCheckpointSaver` with `instance_name` or `autoscaling_endpoint`, `schema`, `setup()`; `DatabricksStore`; extra `[memory]` pulls `langgraph-checkpoint-postgres` and `psycopg[binary,pool]`. Applied: spec §4 model factory and persistence.
- R11 — Lakebase credentials (`databricks_ai_bridge.lakebase`): tokens are minted per connection via the SDK and expire after 60 min; pool recycle 14 min; `PGUSER` is the app service principal's client-id UUID; app env `PGHOST/PGPORT/PGDATABASE/PGUSER/PGSSLMODE` injected for the first database resource. Applied: spec §4 store connection factory.
- R12 — `agent-langgraph-advanced` template (https://github.com/databricks/app-templates/tree/main/agent-langgraph-advanced): `app.yaml` `command: ["uv", "run", "start-app"]`, env via `valueFrom` (app.yaml) / `value_from` (bundle), resources `postgres` (CAN_CONNECT_AND_CREATE) and `experiment`; `databricks.yml` `resources.apps.<key>` with `source_code_path: ./`. Applied: spec §5 bundle shape; MLflow experiment resource dropped (no requirement).
- R13 — Bundles (https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/dev-tools/bundles/resources): `apps` resource types `secret`, `serving_endpoint` (CAN_QUERY), `database`/`postgres`, `uc_securable`; `databricks bundle run <app>` restarts the app on the newest deployed code. Applied: spec §5 and acceptance A30–A31.
- R14 — FMAPI supported models (https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/machine-learning/foundation-model-apis/supported-models): Claude pay-per-token endpoint names include `databricks-claude-opus-5`, `databricks-claude-sonnet-5`, `databricks-claude-fable-5-1`; Sonnet 5 rejects `temperature/top_p/top_k`. Applied: default endpoint `databricks-claude-opus-5`.
- R15 — OpenRouter endpoints API (https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models/anthropic/claude-opus-5/endpoints): id `anthropic/claude-opus-5-20260723`, 1M context, 128K max completion, $5/$25 per MTok. Applied: test-only model id, `CAOS_MODEL_PRICE` example.
- R16 — `databricks/databricks-agent-skills` at `e77e37e8a4dabbe2662b680c180bb72a05eca48d` (v0.2.20, 2026-09-22): 31 official skills + 2 experimental; old ai-dev-kit names map `databricks-bundles→databricks-dabs`, `databricks-genie→databricks-genie-agents`, `databricks-spark-declarative-pipelines→databricks-pipelines`, `databricks-lakebase-autoscale/provisioned→databricks-lakebase`, `databricks-config→databricks-core`, `databricks-metric-view-advisor→databricks-metric-views/references`. Applied: skills ledger; six skills vendored (Markdown only). No legacy file references any ai-dev-kit name (grep: 0 hits), so no remap was needed.
- R17 — ICM paper arXiv 2603.16021 v2 (https://arxiv.org/html/2603.16021v2), repo https://github.com/RinDig/Interpretable-Context-Methodology @ `02ba5d85c7871b75c7c702a2d8da6524723d53d4`: five layers (0 identity `CLAUDE.md`; 1 workspace routing `CONTEXT.md`; 2 stage contract `stages/NN_name/CONTEXT.md` with Inputs/Process/Outputs tables; 3 `references/`; 4 `output/`); "Layer 2 is the control point… an Inputs table that specifies exactly which files from Layers 3 and 4 the agent should load"; the paper keeps state on disk and interrupts with a human, and explicitly says concurrency/automated branching would need "the infrastructure ICM was designed to avoid" (§5.2). Applied: D5 keeps the meta-prompt's default split (LangGraph owns execution/state/interrupts/persistence; ICM owns contracts/prompts/references) because the fixed requirements demand durable, resumable, unattended execution the paper leaves out; stage contracts use the paper's Inputs/Process/Outputs headings and the repo's Inputs-table columns (Source/File/Section/Why).
- R18 — Practitioner signals (HN 49603520, 49747666, 49801342; github.com/anthropics/claude-code issues 91923, 80334; wavect.io; thezvi roundup; sonarsource blog): untested fixes, "verified end-to-end" claims without execution, reinvented parsers, whole-file rewrites, bloated CLAUDE.md causing ignored instructions, subagent model fallback. Applied: evidence-from-tool-output rule; "stdlib or an already-pinned dependency before new code"; CLAUDE.md capped at 120 lines; acceptance commands run in the main session, not a subagent.

### Decisions

- D1 — Target repo is a fresh repository at `/Users/ericguei/Documents/caos-databricks`, not `caos-workbench`. Alternatives: reuse `caos-workbench` (a working clone of the same legacy). Reason: the meta-prompt describes a one-shot rebuild from a read-only snapshot; a clean tree keeps gate baselines honest and the legacy history out of the way.
- D2 — Python pin is `>=3.13,<3.14` (`requires-python`) with `.python-version = 3.13`. Alternatives: 3.14 (legacy). Reason: the fixed requirement is ≥3.13; Databricks Apps honours `requires-python` (R8) and needs an upper bound (R9); 3.13 has the widest wheel coverage for the Databricks stack (`mlflow`, `databricks-sdk`, `psycopg[binary]`), which matters under the wheels-only rule. Ruff `target-version` and mypy `python_version` move to 3.13 with no rule removed. Decimal semantics are identical across 3.13/3.14, so parity goldens produced by the 3.14 legacy remain valid.
- D3 — Frontend is KEEP (copied as-is with all seven of its gates). Alternatives: drop for a v1 API-only app; rebuild in AppKit. Reason: the platform move changes provider, persistence and runtime, not the workspace; the wire contract (`frontend/src/wire/v1/schema.json`) already binds the API shape, and Databricks Apps serve a static export from FastAPI exactly as legacy `server/api/site.py` does. Node 24 and Playwright are present locally.
- D4 — Deploy V stays a vendored, byte-verified bundle at `vendor/deploy-v/` and is never edited (legacy invariant 4). ICM stage folders do not copy its files; each stage contract's Inputs table names the bundle files by path and the runtime loads them through the verified `Bundle` seam. Alternatives: refactor the 25 SKILL.md files into stage `references/`. Reason: copies would break the integrity manifest and `verify_package.py`, and the legacy qualification verdicts bind the bundle build id. "Drop Claude Code frontmatter" therefore applies to the dropped legacy dev skills and the external corpus, not to the bundle.
- D5 — Architecture split (meta-prompt default, confirmed by R17): LangGraph owns execution, state, interrupts and persistence; ICM owns stage folders (`icm/stages/<deploy-v-slug>/`), each with `CONTEXT.md` (contract), `prompt.md` (host prompt template) and `references/` (host-owned files only, e.g. CP-CF). Root `CLAUDE.md` is Layer 0; `icm/CONTEXT.md` is Layer 1 (module index + the rule that the pinned route, not folder numbering, is execution order, per the Deploy V README). Legacy root `CONTEXT.md` (vocabulary) keeps its name because `scripts/check_vocabulary.py` reads it.
- D6 — Checkpointer vs legacy invariant 6 ("no checkpointer, recovery is recomputation"). Decision: use the Lakebase `CheckpointSaver` for graph position, thread identity and interrupts (fixed requirement), but the accepted-attempt ledger remains the only truth for node completion: on resume, `node_states` over the store decides what runs, and a node COMPLETE in the ledger is skipped regardless of checkpoint contents. Reason: keeps exactly-once and one-charge-per-node while satisfying the fixed requirement.
- D7 — Model factory: `caos/models.py::chat_model(...)` returns `ChatDatabricks(endpoint=CAOS_MODEL_ENDPOINT)`; default endpoint `databricks-claude-opus-5`; tests inject `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` (LangChain `ChatOpenAI` against `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`, model `anthropic/claude-opus-5-20260723`, key from `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) through a fixture; `langchain-openai` lives in the `dev` group only. Alternatives: Sonnet 5 default. Reason: the legacy qualification runs used a frontier model at high effort with 65,536-token completions; Opus 5 is the same tier and the same family on both sides; endpoint is env-configurable so `GATEWAY_ENDPOINTS` can override without code.
- D8 — Charge accounting changes from OpenRouter `usage.cost` to `usage_metadata` tokens × the dated `CAOS_MODEL_PRICE` (Decimal). Reason: Databricks responses carry tokens, not money. Reservation arithmetic (`priced_request`, `worst_case`) is unchanged and parity-tested; the charge path gets contract tests. Qualification identity becomes `databricks/<endpoint>/<effort-or-none>/65536`.
- D9 — Blob store backend: Unity Catalog Volume via the SDK Files API (`w.files.upload/download`, path `/Volumes/<catalog>/<schema>/caos_blobs/<sha256[:2]>/<sha256>`), filesystem backend kept for tests and local dev. Alternatives: bytea in Lakebase. Reason: legacy rule "the database holds the digest, never the bytes"; App local disk is ephemeral.
- D10 — Identity: Databricks Apps forwards `x-forwarded-access-token` (verified); the app resolves the caller with SCIM `Me` (`iam.current-user:read` is a default user scope) and maps groups to roles via `CAOS_GROUP_ADMIN` / `CAOS_GROUP_ANALYST`, READER floor for any authenticated user; subject UUID = uuid5(namespace, workspace_id + SCIM id). Development keeps `CAOS_TRUST_ROLE_HEADER` + `CAOS_DEV_USER/ROLE`. The legacy HMAC edge assertion (`x-caos-edge-assertion`) is dropped: the platform is the edge. Security headers middleware stays.
- D11 — Worker: in-process background task started at app startup, one run at a time, using the unchanged `run_work` claim/lease/heartbeat tables. Alternatives: a Databricks Job. Reason: one App = one command; the lease fence already makes restarts safe.
- D12 — SonarCloud-only dimensions replaced locally: new-code coverage ≥80% → `--cov-fail-under=80` over the whole package (stricter); duplicated-line density ≤3% → `jscpd --threshold 3` (Node dev dependency, already a Node project); cognitive complexity ≤15 → `complexipy`; reliability/security/maintainability ratings A and hotspots 100% reviewed → ruff (B, BLE, TRY, C901, PLR), bandit with zero findings and no new `nosec`, pip-audit, gitleaks. Alternatives: pylint duplicate-code (no percentage threshold). Reason: each replacement has a numeric threshold equal to Sonar's.
- D13 — bandit runs in the main 3.13 environment (`bandit>=1.8.6`) instead of a separate 3.12 toolchain. Reason: the 3.12 pin existed only because bandit 1.7.10 read `ast.Constant.s`; `scan_floors.py` still refuses a report that scanned nothing, which is what makes the change safe.
- D14 — Trivy image gate DROP (no image: Databricks Apps run the source in a managed runtime); `smoke-production` and the compose/TLS-edge journey DROP; replaced by acceptance A31 (deployed health + one real gateway call) and the Playwright workbench suite. `check_pr_size.py` KEEP as CI-only (needs a PR base) and is not an acceptance command.
- D15 — Gate integrity is itself a gate: `scripts/check_gate_config.py` asserts the ruff rule set, mccabe 10, mypy strict, `--cov-fail-under=80`, the coverage `omit` list, and that suppression counts (`noqa`, `type: ignore`, `nosec`, `pragma: no cover`, `pytest.mark.skip`) do not exceed `tests/gate_baseline.json`, which the builder generates once from the ported legacy files and commits before any new code.
- D16 — Skills: six Databricks skills vendored as Markdown only (`assets/`, `agents/openai.yaml`, `examples/*.py` omitted so no ungated code enters the repo); all legacy `.claude/skills` (gitnexus) and `.claude/agents` DROP; the 32-skill external corpus DROP (superseded by the pinned Deploy V bundle; its own migration manifest marks it `historical_non_runtime`). See the skills ledger in the spec.
- D17 — Local test PostgreSQL stays `postgres:17-alpine` via the legacy `compose.yaml` (Docker 29 present). Lakebase's Postgres major version could not be read from the docs page; the builder records `SELECT version()` on first deploy and any incompatibility becomes a finding.
- D18 — Parity targets (all deterministic): `cash_flow_forecast`, `priced_request`/`worst_case`, `validate_spend`/`remaining`/`ceiling_of`, forecast projection/binding validation, `resolve_route` for all 18 pathways (digests, node order, edges), canonical JSON digest, `BoundaryText`, plain-text extraction tokens/blocks, citation anchoring, deliverable HTML render, and rendered prompt bytes per node. Goldens are generated by running the legacy snapshot in a throw-away `uv venv --python 3.14` under `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT` with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and committed under `tests/parity/golden/`.
- D19 — The spec author seeds a 12-line `CLAUDE.md` (Layer 0 pointer to the spec and the four hard limits) so every build turn loads it; the builder rewrites it from scratch per the fixed requirement, keeping it under 120 lines.
- D20 — Goal turn bound: 300 turns per session (75 capability rows, 26 Deploy V rows, 45 skill rows, 31 acceptance commands; the launcher resumes up to three times and each resume resets the counter).
- D21 — Launcher config: `LEGACY_REPO=/Users/ericguei/Documents/caos-v2`, `DEPLOY_V_REL=vendor/deploy-v`, `LEGACY_SKILL_DIRS=("/Users/ericguei/Documents/Co-Pilot Agents/DEPLOY_B_COWORK_SKILLS")`, `TARGET_REPO=/Users/ericguei/Documents/caos-databricks`, `DEPLOY_ACCESS=no` until B1–B2 clear. Stage 1 was executed in an interactive session, so the launcher gains `SKIP_STAGE1=1` (skips when the goal file exists). `LEGACY_ENV_FILE` stays `CHANGE_ME` (B4).
- D22 — User-level hooks active during the build (`~/.claude/settings.json`): gitnexus PreToolUse (Grep|Glob|Bash) and PostToolUse (Bash) context hooks; `headroom init hook ensure` on Bash and SessionStart; `rtk hook claude` on Bash (rewrites commands for compact output and truncates long output to a tee file); fallow-review SessionStart and FileChanged (`feed.jsonl`) hooks; two SessionStart reminders (confidence-review, rewrite-tournament). None rewrites repository files. Project hooks: none (the legacy `claude_hook.py` guard/format hooks are not carried; the guard's refusals are restated as spec rules and the formatter would fight targeted edits). Mitigation for rtk truncation: acceptance commands print short summaries and write full logs under `docs/rebuild/runs/`.

### Build run (in-session, started 2026-09-22)

- D23 — Owner directive 2026-09-22: no Databricks workspace is available; build now, designed for export to an enterprise workspace deployed by someone else. Consequences: deploy access is `no` for this build (A31 → `gateway path unverified`); nothing workspace-specific is hard-coded — `databricks.yml` uses `variables:` (`model_endpoint`, `uc_catalog`, `uc_schema`, `lakebase_instance`, `group_admin`, `group_analyst`) and `workspace.host` comes from the CLI profile; `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` is the enterprise runbook (prerequisites, permissions, `bundle validate/deploy/run`, smoke); `scripts/preflight.py` checks the named resources exist once a profile is configured. The local test stack (Docker Postgres, fake chat model) is the only thing this machine runs.
- D24 — The build runs in this interactive session as the builder (Claude Code 2.1.246 cannot run the launcher's `--permission-prompts none`, and no OpenRouter key file exists), following spec §1 as its operating mode. The launcher remains the way to re-run the build unattended later.

### Findings fixed during the build (Fn: finding; action; evidence)

- F1 — Python 3.13 evaluates annotations eagerly (3.14's PEP 649 had masked 52 modules that named `TYPE_CHECKING`-only types without the future import); action: `from __future__ import annotations` added to every module lacking it; evidence: `uv run pytest` collection error `NameError: name 'Lease' is not defined` in `caos/store/outcomes.py` before, imports clean after.
- F2 — starlette 1.6's `TestClient` requires `httpx2` and deprecates `httpx` (its responses typed `httpx2.Response`); action: dev group `httpx2>=2.13`, tests import `Response` from `httpx2`; evidence: 32 mypy `assignment` errors before, 0 after.
- F3 — `tool.uv.no-build = true` also refused to build the project itself as an editable install; action: `package = false` (virtual project; the app runs `python -m caos.serve`), wheels-only still applies to every dependency; evidence: `uv sync --locked --all-groups` exit 0.
- F4 — The CP-CF host manifest pins the calculator's bytes and the package rename changed its import lines; action: `scripts/host_manifest.py` regenerated `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `caos/methodology/host_pin.py` (SKILL.md unchanged); evidence: `delivered_authority(bundle, "CP-CF")` refused `AUTHORITY_BYTES_MISMATCH` before, returns both files after.
- F5 — LangGraph adds a "During task with name …" note to an exception leaving a node, which pytest's `match` and any message reader see; action: `run_route` strips that note before re-raising, so a refusal is still exactly its code; evidence: `test_owned_pretransport_read_failure_cleans_up_without_call` green.
- F6 — A static edge still fires after a node returns `Command(goto=END)`, so the terminal node ran after a Blocked handoff had ended the run; action: every node's successor is a conditional edge read from `RunState.ended`; evidence: `test_a_crash_before_the_block_commits_resumes_blocked_without_a_second_call` green.
- F7 — The legacy concurrent pass (`Execution.per_node`, `independent_batch` execution, `_run_batch`) is not carried (next.md N1); dropped tests, by name: `test_the_worker_hands_the_runtime_a_concurrent_pass_and_a_stoppable_one`, `test_a_wide_frontier_runs_its_independent_nodes_at_the_same_time`, `test_two_nodes_of_one_concurrent_pass_each_accept_exactly_once`. `independent_batch` itself stays (pure, tested).
- F8 — Suites and scripts dropped with the capabilities they proved: `tests/test_provider.py` (28 OpenRouter transport tests; replaced by `tests/test_models.py`), `tests/journey/{edge,worker,run}.py`, `test_journey_edge.py`, `test_journey_tooling.py`, `test_production_image.py` (C72/C75), `test_claude_hooks.py` and `scripts/claude_hook.py` (C76), `scripts/ledger_state.py`, `test_ledger.py`, `test_phase_exits.py`, `test_vendor_change_record.py` (legacy governance documents and git history are not carried). `tests/journey/pack.py` stays as the sample-document fixture.
- F9 — Gate-integrity baseline (`tests/gate_baseline.json`) generated after the port: noqa 124, type_ignore 156, nosec 17, no_cover 5, skip 0, xfail 0; every later change must keep each count at or below these.
- F10 — `databricks-langchain` ships no `py.typed`; action: `stubs/databricks_langchain/__init__.pyi` typed from the 0.20.0 source (`mypy_path = "stubs"`), no `ignore_missing_imports`.
- F11 — LangChain assigns every `AIMessage` an id, so the host-minted generation id (D7) applies only when the vendor's id fails `producer_identifier`; evidence: `test_a_fenced_answer_is_unwrapped_and_a_blank_id_is_host_minted`.
- F12 — bandit B105 flags any string assigned to a name containing "token"; action: the forwarded-token header constant is `PLATFORM_HEADER`, no new `nosec`.
- F13 — Identity behind the platform (D10) is `uuid5(workspace_id:scim_id)` from SCIM `Me` on the forwarded token, cached 300 s by token digest; `_AMBIENT_IDENTITY` in `tests/conftest.py` also clears `DATABRICKS_APP_NAME` so no suite inherits platform mode.
- F14 — `verified_root_bytes` delivers only `.md`/`.txt` root files, so the stage index cannot read `DEPLOY_V_MANIFEST.json` through it; action: `Bundle.physical_modules()` reads the verified integrity manifest's skill entries.
- F15 — The committed wire schema carries model docstrings, which named the old package; action: regenerated with `python -m caos.api.wire`; evidence: `test_the_committed_wire_schema_is_the_models_schema` green.
- F16 — `scripts/check_pr_size.py` stays CI-only (`size` job on pull requests); it has no local acceptance command (G26).
- D25 — `jscpd` (pinned in `frontend/package.json` devDependencies, ^5.3.1 resolved by the lock) is the duplication gate (G15, D12); its CLI takes `--ignore` globs, not `--gitignore`, so the gate command names `**/node_modules/**,**/dist/**,**/dist-demo/**`. Measured 2.65% duplicated lines over `caos scripts icm frontend/src` (threshold 3%).
- D26 — `databricks-langchain` requires `unitycatalog-ai[databricks]`, whose extra pulls `databricks-connect` 17.0.10, which declares `Requires-Python ==3.12.*` and serves Unity Catalog function tools this app never calls; `[tool.uv] override-dependencies = ["unitycatalog-ai>=0.3.2"]` drops the extra so the 3.13 closure carries only packages published for it. Evidence: `uv pip show databricks-connect` → not found after the relock; `uv sync --locked` exit 0.
- F17 — pip cannot dry-run-install the exported closure (`pip-audit -r … --require-hashes` resolves the dependency graph again and refuses the 3.12-only package, and after D26 refuses the unmet extra); action: G11 audits the environment `uv sync --locked` built from the hashed lock (`uv run pip-audit --strict`; `pip` added to the dev group so pip-audit can inspect it); evidence: `No known vulnerabilities found`, exit 0.
- F18 — `icm/` holds no Python, and `mypy` refuses a directory without `.py` files; action: G3 is `uv run mypy caos scripts tests` (the ICM loader is `caos/icm.py`, inside that scope); evidence: `Success: no issues found in 329 source files`.
- F19 — Parity (tests/parity, 378 goldens generated from the legacy snapshot under CPython 3.14.6): the `routes` group masks the `host_manifest_sha256` predicate because the CP-CF pin legitimately moved (F4) and records `host_pin_is_current` instead; the `prompt` group cannot cover CP-CF byte for byte (its delivered authority includes the renamed calculator's bytes) and names it in `UNCOVERED_PROMPT_MODULES`, with a test holding covered ∪ uncovered == `ADAPTER_MODULES`; `citations` exercises the pure anchoring helpers because both public entry points read the token index from the store. 402 parity tests pass.
- F20 — Legacy repo-layout tests that asserted the Docker image, the Makefile, the compose smoke stack, the pip locks and the 3.12 security toolchain were removed or rewritten by name: `test_the_runtime_image_installs_the_way_ci_does`, `test_the_runtime_image_pins_its_base_and_drops_its_privileges`, `test_the_runtime_image_pins_only_the_verified_os_security_updates`, `test_make_image_runs_the_exact_ci_trivy_gate`, `test_index_fails_closed_when_gitnexus_is_not_installed`, `test_make_doctor_uses_the_bootstrapped_python`, `test_make_dev_worker_runs_the_worker_entry_point`, `test_make_dev_api_serves_the_guarded_site_on_loopback_without_proxy_headers`, `test_the_smoke_stack_is_disposable_and_isolated_from_dev_storage`, `test_complete_local_gate_requires_database_image_frontend_and_base` (dropped, C69/C72); `test_every_install_is_wheels_only` → `test_every_install_is_locked_and_wheels_only`; the doctor tests now name Python 3.13, the Databricks CLI and the gateway variables; `scripts/release_pack.py` names `uv.lock` and `frontend/package-lock.json` as the locks.
- F21 — `end-of-file-fixer` rewrote a vendored Databricks skill file (a missing final newline in `.claude/skills/databricks-lakebase/references/connectivity.md`), which would have made its bytes differ from the pinned commit; action: the two whitespace hooks exclude `.claude/skills/` like the other vendored trees, and the file is restored; evidence: `uv run pre-commit run --all-files` exit 0 with a clean tree.
- D27 — Cognitive complexity (G14) applies Sonar's new-code semantics through complexipy's snapshot: `complexipy-snapshot.json` baselines the 26 legacy functions above 15 (read_analysis 20, node_readiness 17, case_tail 22, render._list 31, revisions._span 16, ingest._prepare 16, pdf.walk_pages 23, route.dependency_order 20, forecast._mapped_rows 27, handoff.stored_lineage 30, invocation._yaml 17, invocation.allowed_uses 19, invocation.lite_object_requirement 16, invocation.named_objects 16, selection.select_sources 17, vendor._Loader._import 16, harness._consumers 18, matrix._digested 20, matrix._forecast_met 16, matrix._projections_met 16, matrix._registers_met 20, matrix._witnesses_contradict 18, on_disk._registers 18, proof.assert_orchestration_proof 18, run_inputs._research 20, qualify._capture 22); any new or changed function above 15, and any baselined one that grows, fails (verified: a probe function at 20 failed with the snapshot present). The threshold is unchanged at 15; the baseline count is part of `tests/gate_baseline.json` and may only fall. Alternatives: refactoring 26 working legacy functions for a metric, which the operating mode forbids as unrequested churn.
- F22 — gitleaks' `generic-api-key` rule matched ten SHA-256 digests in the parity manifests (`tests/parity/golden/*/manifest.json`, case names containing "key"); action: the allowlist admits secrets of the exact shape `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` (a content digest; every credential this repository could meet carries a prefix the expression rejects); evidence: `gitleaks git --no-banner` → no leaks found.
- F23 — bandit 1.9 (B105) flags the pdf extractor's identity record `"password": ""` (pdfminer's no-password parameter, recorded for provenance); action: the value is the module constant `UNENCRYPTED = ""` (pdfminer's empty password for an unencrypted document), bytes unchanged, no `nosec`; evidence: `bandit -r caos scripts icm` exit 0.
- F24 — A28 found one "OpenRouter" mention left in `caos/graph/runtime.py`'s `Provider` docstring; action: reworded; evidence: `grep -rniI openrouter caos icm .claude/skills databricks.yml app.yaml` → no output, exit 1.
- F25 — complexipy rewrites `complexipy-snapshot.json` on every run without a trailing newline, and `end-of-file-fixer` then rewrote it back, so the two fought on each gate run; action: the whitespace hooks exclude that one generated file (as they exclude the other generated and vendored trees); evidence: `uv run pre-commit run --all-files` exit 0 with a clean tree after a complexipy run.
- F26 — C18 says the human gates are "resumed through LangGraph `interrupt()`"; the build does not call `interrupt()`. The legacy gates bind a run's host-derived preview and complete input *before* execution: `execution_input` refuses a run whose gate approval is missing, so an unapproved run is never claimed and there is nothing mid-graph to interrupt. Approval semantics, digests and the store schema are unchanged (C18 "approval semantics unchanged" holds); the checkpointer keeps thread position only (D6). Recorded so the ledger row reads as built. C72 cleanup in the same commit: `scripts/install_trivy.sh` and its test were still in the tree after the image, compose smoke and Trivy scan were dropped; removed. `scripts/scan_floors.py --trivy` stays: it is the floor checker's own branch, tested, and costs nothing.
- D28 — The workspace that is not there yet is stood in for by `tests/workspace_stub.py` (owner directive, 2026-09-22: "use temp fixtures to resume build to completion"): a stdlib HTTP server on a loopback port that answers the exact paths the SDK and the CLI use (`/serving-endpoints/chat/completions`, SCIM `Me`/`Groups` with `startIndex` paging, `/api/2.0/fs/files` and `/fs/directories`, the Unity Catalog, serving-endpoint, Lakebase-instance and Apps lookups, `workspace/get-status` + `mkdirs`). `DATABRICKS_HOST` names it and a placeholder bearer satisfies the SDK's PAT auth, so nothing below HTTP is injected: `tests/test_workspace_stub.py` runs the gateway smoke, `scripts/preflight.py`, SCIM identity, the volume backend and a full LITE route through `ChatDatabricks` against it, and acceptance row A34 runs `databricks bundle validate` through it ("Validation OK!"). It proves wire shapes and this repository's code; it cannot prove grants, the Apps proxy's stream handling (C42), or a real model's answers. Docker Postgres keeps standing in for Lakebase (D17 pending). The value never compared by the stub is never recorded or printed; `requests` holds methods and paths only.
- F27 — JSON mode was not on the wire: `caos.models.ChatCompletions.complete(json_object=True)` sized the request with `response_format` (`request_bytes`) but called `chat.invoke` without it, so no gateway call asked for a JSON object although the canonical executor asks on every module call and the legacy OpenRouter request carried it. Found by the stub route run (the scripted provider asserts the flag). Fixed: `response_format={"type": "json_object"}` is passed to the chat model when asked; `scripts/gateway_smoke.py` now makes its second call in JSON mode and prints `json_mode=accepted` (exit 1 otherwise), so an endpoint that rejects the parameter fails the smoke, not the first run. Evidence: `tests/test_workspace_stub.py` asserts every node call of the LITE route carried it.
- D28, addendum (2026-09-23) — The stand-in now covers the whole platform path, not only the SDK calls. `tests/platform_app.py` boots `python -m caos.serve` in a subprocess under the platform's own environment (`DATABRICKS_APP_NAME`/`_PORT`, `PG*`, the workspace host, the volume blob root, the endpoint and price, the run ceiling, the in-process worker, the export) with the Docker Postgres reached through the credential the stub mints; `tests/test_platform_boot.py` drives it over HTTP as two workspace users the way the Apps proxy presents them (forwarded token, idempotency key, origin): health ready on 3.13, a case, a membership grant, a pack into the volume, a LITE run pinned, both gates previewed and approved, the run started and driven to COMPLETE by the worker through `ChatDatabricks` in JSON mode, the event stream delivering while the run is still running, the report read. The stub also answers `databricks bundle deploy` and `bundle run` (workspace file import, the Apps API), so acceptance rows A35–A36 run them through the real CLI, and CI's `bundle` job does the same with the CLI pinned at v1.17.0.
- F28 — Every run at the gateway's Claude prices would have refused `BUDGET_CEILING_REACHED`: `caos.store.budget.CEILING` (5.00, a legacy number for a cheaper model) is below one worst-case call at the default `model_price` (a 1 MiB request at 0.000005 per byte plus the completion cap at 0.000025 per token is about 6.88), and `_affordable` refuses a run whose ceiling cannot cover one call. Found by preflight's new affordability check while building the platform boot. Fixed: the deployment names the ceiling (`run_ceiling` bundle variable, `CAOS_RUN_CEILING`, default 25.00), `create_run` passes it to `start_run`, `scripts/preflight.py --price --run-ceiling` refuses a ceiling below one call before anything is deployed, and `tests/test_run_ceiling.py` pins both. `CEILING` stays the store's own default for a caller that names none. `scripts/dev_doctor.py` named `CAOS_LIVE_BUDGET_CEILING`, which nothing reads; it now names `CAOS_RUN_CEILING`.
- F29 — `databricks bundle deploy` against the stub uploaded 1,522 files: the whole tracked tree, tests, docs, frontend sources and gate configuration included. Fixed with `sync.exclude` in `databricks.yml`; the App now ships the package, `vendor/deploy-v`, `icm/`, `scripts/`, the export, the lock and the two manifests (about 400 files).
- F30 — The worker never started on the platform path: `databricks_langchain.CheckpointSaver` builds its pool over a connection string that fixes `port=5432 sslmode=require` and passes its own `kwargs` and `connection_class` to `ConnectionPool`, so neither the platform's `PGPORT`/`PGSSLMODE` nor any stand-in database can reach it, and `start_in_process` printed `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` and served without a worker. Fixed: `caos/graph/checkpoint.py` runs `PostgresSaver` over a small `psycopg_pool` whose `MintedConnection` builds its URL from `store_url()` when it opens -- the same `PG*` values and the same minted credential the store uses, refreshed after the token ages out. D6's intent (checkpoints in Lakebase, minted credentials, position only) holds; the vendor class is no longer used.
- F31 — The handoff asked the enterprise agent to run eleven steps by hand. Now one command: `scripts/enterprise_deploy.sh <profile> <catalog> <schema> <lakebase_instance> [endpoint] [price] [run_ceiling]` runs preflight (E1), `databricks bundle validate|deploy|run` (E2–E4) and, through `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py --stage after`, the app's state and URL (E5), health on 3.13 (E6), the gateway smoke in JSON mode (E7), Lakebase `SELECT version()` for D17 (E8) and the event stream's first frame through the proxy for C42 (E9), writing one evidence row per step. The CLI runs in the shell so the Python half needs no subprocess (bandit B404/B603 would each have cost a suppression the gate budget does not allow). `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py` runs the wrapper against the stub and the booted app with a recording `databricks` on the path: nine rows, all zero, no secret in any of them. What it cannot prove remains the real workspace's grants and its proxy, which E5–E9 report the first time they run there.

### Adversarial review round, 2026-09-23 (five concurrent reviewers, one per section; every finding verified against the code before it was patched)

- F32 — The T8 page expression (`caos/methodology/selection.py`) backtracked cubically on a cell of a name, a long run of spaces and a word, inside an open transaction. Possessive quantifiers and a 512-character item cap; a padded item names nothing at once.
- F33 — Admission stored a token's own bytes but rendered the line NFC, so a verbatim quote of a decomposed line (NFD from macOS or some PDF writers) could never anchor. Citations compare NFC at match time; NFC is idempotent so nothing that anchored stops anchoring.
- F34 — A finish reason the response did not state was read as `stop`. It is `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`, with the bill kept.
- F35 — `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` was declared and enforced nowhere; a 4 MiB completion was stored and parsed. Enforced after the bill: an oversize answer is refused, never stored.
- F36 — `generation_id` was always LangChain's own run id posing as the provider's. The completion id the client surfaces wins; an id LangChain minted is recorded host-minted, as the module promised.
- F37 — The worker froze its Lakebase URL at boot and could never re-mint after the credential aged out; the cache held the credential for 50 minutes against the vendor's 15-minute plan; an authentication failure never dropped it. The worker connects through `store_url()` each time, `TOKEN_SECONDS` is 14 minutes, and an authentication failure drops the cache (`note_connect_failure`).
- F38 — The worker beat its heartbeat once per run, so every run past 30 seconds read `WORKERS_STALE`. One beat per node (`Execution.heartbeat`).
- F39 — The LangGraph checkpoint was written and never read: every resume restarted at START. `resume_input` hands `invoke` `None` when the thread has pending work, so it resumes at the node it died in; a thread that reached its end is deleted (position only, D6). `finish` reports the store's word when a terminal move was made by another hand.
- F40 — `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` governed nothing: the chat model ran on the OpenAI client's 600-second default with two silent retries. The model carries `timeout=TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and `max_retries=0`; the lease is sized against a deadline that exists.
- F41 — The volume backend recognised not-found by one class name; the SDK answers a missing file with a subclass. The whole class family and the error code are read.
- F42 — `_diagnostic` suppressed only two exception classes between the paid call and the bill. Anything the blob store raises leaves the address `None` and the bill commits, as the docstring said.
- F43 — Identity built a fresh SDK client per uncached token: an uncached discovery probe with a five-minute retry budget, and beside the app's own service-principal variables a `ValueError` (two authentication methods) that escaped `except OSError` as a 500. SCIM `Me` is one bounded stdlib request; a refused token is remembered for five seconds; the cache evicts and is capped; `IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE` (503, transient) says the workspace did not answer and the health document folds an identity probe into `status`. `caos/workspace.py` is the one bounded SDK client for the process's own calls.
- F44 — `/api/health` bypassed the identity scrub, and the platform's forwarded token was neither de-duplicated nor scrubbed. The mode is resolved once per request; identity headers are dropped wherever an edge exists, the health path included; two forwarded tokens are refused.
- F45 — Behind the platform without a public origin, an unsafe request with no `sec-fetch-site` was admitted from any origin. Its origin must name the request's own `Host`.
- F46 — Every subject was minted under `DATABRICKS_WORKSPACE_ID` with a silent empty default. The platform refuses to boot without it.
- F47 — Health probes had two seconds for a credential mint and a TLS connect across the workspace network, and a round started over an abandoned thread. Five seconds, and no round over a probe thread still alive.
- F48 — `sync.exclude: "*.md"` matched at every depth and took every SKILL.md and prompt block out of the deployed app while health stayed green. The root documents are named one by one; `scripts/check_gate_config.py` refuses any exclude that hides a path the app needs, and CI checks the CLI's own deployment record after a stub deploy.
- F49 — `uv run` on the platform installed the dev toolchain and did not pin to the lock. `uv run --locked --no-dev`.
- F50 — The bundle granted the service principal three resources and nobody `CAN_USE` on the app. The admin group is `CAN_MANAGE`, the analyst group `CAN_USE`.
- F51 — `mode: production` with no `root_path` put the production source in the deployer's home. `/Workspace/caos-bundle/<target>`.
- F52 — `app.yaml` set six variables, the bundle eleven; if the platform preferred `app.yaml`, the run ceiling silently fell back to the legacy 5.00 (F28 again). `app.yaml` sets no environment; the bundle is the one source and the gate asserts it.
- F53 — `forward_user_access_token` is a preview feature the workspace must enable; an app deployed without it names nobody. Row E5 reads the flag back and fails without it; the runbook names the prerequisite.
- F54 — Row E6 read `status` alone, which excludes the worker: an app whose worker never started was a green deployment. Every code, `identity` and `workers` included.
- F55 — Row E9 recorded any non-201 as exit 0 "needs writer standing". Only a 403 is a standing question; anything else fails the row.
- F56 — `PdfExtractor.extract` and `page_frame` defaulted to an infinite deadline. The limits' own seconds.
- F57 — A parity golden regenerated from the rebuilt package would match a regression rather than catch it. The reader refuses a manifest not written from the legacy snapshot and the gate holds a per-group case floor.
- F58 — The suppression budget missed the spellings the tools honour (upper-case noqa, coverage's pragma grammar, `skipif`, `pytest.skip(...)`, `importorskip`, a bare `mark`), and the change that breached a budget could rewrite the baseline. The patterns are the tools' grammars; the baseline was re-measured once under them (skip 0 to 7 for the suite's existing conditional skips, noqa and no_cover by the same rule) and from here may only fall; on a pull request CI compares the committed baseline against the base branch's (`--against`).
- F59 — The audit package's own verifier was the one member nothing checked. The host refuses a package whose archived verifier is not its own.
- F60 — A receipt naming no case, run or revision verified against any payload. Every receipt binds all three.
- F61 (declined) — Two contractual amortisation rows for one facility and period are summed. Reviewed as a slipped key; the cash_flow parity goldens (`annual_amortisation_sums`) hold the legacy rule that instalments sum and only an exact repeat refuses, so the rule stands and a test now states it.
- F62 — `tolerance` had no ceiling, so the one arithmetic reconciliation could be switched off from an admitted document. Bounded by `MAX_TOLERANCE`.
- F63 — `scripts/check_tested.py` was satisfied by a name a test merely bound. Loads only.
- F64 — A qualification case carrying both an expected refusal and citation keys was signable with every key missed. The refusal is a conjunct.
- F65 — Provenance and citation digests were truncated after escaping, cutting an entity in half. Cut, then escape.
- F66 — The vendored bundle's manifest was verified only against itself. `caos/methodology/bundle_pin.py` compiles its digest; the worker, the API's bundle and the health probe verify it.
- F67 — The host's prompt blocks were unverified and cached. `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` records every block under `host_prompt_hashes` and `prompt_block` refuses bytes the manifest does not name.
- F68 — `.gitleaks.toml` allowlisted every bare 64-hex string repository-wide, the shape of a 32-byte key in hex. Scoped to the files that hold digests; the hook and the CI image are the same gitleaks version and the gate asserts it.
- F69 — `scripts/document_register.py` ran under no gate. CI runs it.
- F70 — Smaller: the JSON envelope's length guard matched the multipart one (`isascii`); `freeze_in` checks every signature as the filing does; `prove_revision` refuses a payload with no narrative list; a duplicated constant went; the deploy wrapper pins the bundle engine the stand-in verified; the deploy script records a redirect as its status; preflight distinguishes a group it may not list from one that is missing; the doctor files the run ceiling under deployment configuration; the checkpoint pool retires connections within the credential's life and is closed with the worker; the lease carries the seconds it was claimed with; the connection string quotes the host, database and TLS mode and refuses a non-numeric port; the terminal graph node's name is outside the route-id grammar.
- Reviewed and accepted as they are: `/api/health` reports `python_version` and `build_id` unauthenticated (spec 3.1 asks for both and the deploy row reads them); the SSE tail sleeps in a thread (the cap is defensible, the comment now names the anyio limiter); admission holds a pack twice (a writer-only cost, logged in next.md); `FORECAST_CHAIN_BROKEN` cannot fire from `cash_flow_forecast` (logged in next.md); render and the audit package have no route (logged in next.md).

Round 3 (2026-09-23): the deployment review, the skill-audit sweep and the edge/identity review in `docs/rebuild/findings-round2.md`, and the second pass in `docs/rebuild/findings.md`. Each patcher's fuller paragraph is in the commit that closed the round.

- F71 — The CLI splits a `--var` value on commas, so the dated price never reached a real deploy (C1). `scripts/enterprise_deploy.sh` exports `BUNDLE_VAR_model_price`; the runbook, the gate row A34 and CI pass a non-default price that way.
- F72 — A denylist sync shipped whatever a working tree held (a local blob cache, `.env.local`) and dropped `caos/qualification` and every nested `scripts/`/`tests/` in the vendor bundle, because the CLI matches `dir/**` at every depth (C2, R2-E1, TM-3). `databricks.yml` `sync.paths` is an allowlist with `frontend/dist/**` re-included; `scripts/check_gate_config.py` refuses a missing allowlist, a needed path no root carries, and a missing export include; `SHIPPED` names nested paths.
- F73 — The `prod` target had never deployed against the stand-in: the stub lacked `workspace/export` for the deployment lock (C3). The stub serves it; the prod target's validate, deploy and run pass under one stub (`sh -c`), in CI and as row A37.
- F74 — E6 was one probe issued before the app could be ready, and a 503 threw its codes away (C4). Polled for up to 90 s; every answer's codes are recorded.
- F75 — E9 accepted any 403 as "needs writer standing" and any non-empty first line as a stream (W1, AR-05). Only `NOT_AUTHORISED` is unverified; the stream must be `text/event-stream`, carry one complete frame, and still be open two seconds later (read on a thread: `http.client` forgets the socket of a `Connection: close` response).
- F76 — A `WorkspaceClient` was built per blob request and twice per health round, each costing host metadata and OIDC discovery (W2). One client per process and identity in `caos/workspace.py`, never keyed on a secret; a failed construction is the typed `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` (CR-1) and is not cached.
- F77 — The admin business group held `CAN_MANAGE`, which deploys code as the app's principal and so writes the ledger (W3, DP-10). Both groups `CAN_USE`; the deployer alone manages. A dedicated deployer principal with top-level permissions is N24.
- F78 — E9 forwarded the deployer's full-scope token in `x-forwarded-access-token`, which a proxy that appends its own turns into a 401 and a proxy that passes through turns into an over-scoped identity (W4). Only the stand-in forwards it (`CAOS_DEPLOY_FORWARD_CALLER=1`); behind the platform the proxy forwards the caller's.
- F79 — Preflight's "unknown, not missing" group branch re-raised and blocked the deploy (W5). `UNKNOWN` is said and not counted.
- F80 — Smaller deploy-path corrections: every `after` step ends in a row, never a traceback (N1); one evidence directory per run (N2); the runbook names `DATABRICKS_WORKSPACE_ID` and `CAOS_RUN_CEILING` and the engine, `pyproject.toml` names `caos.serve`, `app.yaml`'s F52 comment says what happens (N4); `bundle.databricks_cli_version >= 1.17.0` (N5); `CAOS_UC_SCHEMA`, set and never read, is gone (DP-12); gitleaks is v8.24.3 in the hook and the CI image, not pinned down to the hook's older version (CR-9).
- F81 — The dev and prod targets managed one app, so a developer's deploy could take over production (DP-6). The app is `caos` in prod and `caos-<target>` elsewhere; `enterprise_deploy.app_name` follows the rule; the stub seeds no app and answers a taken name with 409.
- F82 — Nothing checked the endpoint's AI Gateway posture, and inference-table payload logging would copy every prompt's document text into a Delta table (DP-3, MX-5, TM-6). Preflight refuses payload logging, a fallback and more than one served entity or route, and reports guardrails.
- F83 — Preflight read the endpoint from the price string rather than checking it against `--endpoint`, and the gateway smoke passed JSON mode without parsing the answer (AR-06, AR-17). Both check what they claim.
- F84 — The LangGraph checkpointer deserialised with the library's default serializer, which imports and calls any callable a row names: a crafted `caos_graph` row ran code in the worker (SA-C2, TM-1). The saver's serializer allows exactly `caos.graph.build.RunState`; a crafted row loads as inert data.
- F85 — The SDK reports an unreachable workspace or a refused token as `ValueError`, which escaped `_mint`, the worker loop, the blob backend and boot (SA-C3, CR-1, DL-2, DP-1): one 503 from the token endpoint during a re-mint killed the in-process worker for good while health stayed `ready`. `caos/workspace.py` and `_mint` map it to `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`; the worker treats any `OSError`/`ValueError` from its connection factory as a store fault.
- F86 — A session the server ends, which Lakebase does on every failover and scale-to-zero, escaped the worker through `_beat`'s unprotected rollback (SA-C4, AR-01, DL-3); and the driver reports a refused password as a bare `OperationalError` with no SQLSTATE, which the credential cache did not recognise (AR-02). `_beat` uses `rollback_or_close`; any connection failure drops the cached credential, the checkpoint pool's included. Covered by a real `pg_terminate_backend`.
- F87 — `pause_seconds` raised `2 ** failures` before capping it and overflowed after some eight hours of faults (AR-03). The exponent is bounded first.
- F88 — Minting held a process-wide lock across the network, retried failures at once, and discarded a token the server still accepted at fourteen minutes (MX-3, DP-2, DL-4). Minting is single-flight, abandoned past 30 s on a helper thread, not retried for five seconds after a failure, and a held token is reused until the server's stated expiry less a margin. The chat model is built on the process's bounded client, not the library's default with the SDK's five-minute discovery budget (CR-6).
- F89 — The 120 s call deadline could not deliver a few thousand tokens at real throughput, so long answers were billed by the endpoint and recorded indeterminate (MX-4, CR-3, DP-8); heartbeats went stale at 30 s during any call (AR-24). `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is 240 s as a generation budget, `LEASE_SECONDS` 600 s so a lease outlives two deadlines (D5), `WORKER_STALE_AFTER` 300 s. `MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS` is the reservation ceiling, not a delivery promise; streaming with lease renewal is N14.
- F90 — A checkpoint pool whose set-up failed kept reconnecting and minting for the life of the process (CR-2); two processes setting the schema up raced on the migration version and one lost its worker (AR-20); pooled connections were never checked after a failover (DL-9). The pool is closed before the refusal leaves; set-up runs under a polled advisory lock (polled because LangGraph builds an index `CONCURRENTLY`, which waits for the session blocked in `pg_advisory_lock`); the pool checks each connection before handing it out.
- F91 — A lost or corrupt diagnostic blob was reported as a store fault, which released the run to the head of the queue and blocked every other run (DL-5); parked runs left their checkpoint threads forever (DL-8); an idle worker committed two writes a second and kept Lakebase from scaling to zero (DL-10). Lost blobs park the run with their own code; a run this worker parks or ends loses its thread; the idle beat is every ten seconds.
- F92 — A cancel that landed during the last call was acknowledged and the run still ended COMPLETE (AR-13, CR-8): `_transition` refuses `RUN_CANCEL_REQUESTED` at a COMPLETE move, and the holder's cancel path ends the run CANCELLED with the last artifact and bill kept. `require_lease` no longer uses its default as a sentinel (CR-11).
- F93 — uvicorn re-raises SIGTERM after its shutdown, so the worker drain after `uvicorn.run` never ran, and an open event stream held shutdown until SIGKILL (DP-4); the connection bound counted idle keep-alives, so 24 streams plus eight idle connections answered everything with a plain-text 503 (DP-7). The drain is a lifespan shutdown hook (`caos.api.app.on_shutdown`) inside a two-second graceful shutdown and a twelve-second join; the bound is the stream slots plus forty.
- F94 — A 429 was treated as possibly billed: it kept a worst-case reservation and parked the run (DP-5). A rate limit reached no model, so it is asked again under the same reservation, three tries, waiting `Retry-After` capped at 20 s; every other status is still answered once by its class.
- F95 — A usage block with a negative count billed zero (AR-14): counts must be whole and non-negative or the answer is `PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`. `CAOS_REASONING_EFFORT` named an effort the model never received in the qualification identity (AR-15): setting it refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` until N2 sends it. F36 corrected: `ChatDatabricks` never surfaces the completion id, so every production generation id is host-minted (CR-5); capturing it is N25.
- F96 — An unhandled fault reached the server log with its message, which may quote a document (AS-6). The edge logs the class and frame only; `caos/serve.py` installs a filter on `uvicorn.error` that drops exception text.
- F97 — Event-stream slots were one global pool, so one reader could hold all 24 (MX-2, AS-3, TM-4). Four per actor as well, refused with the same code.
- F98 — Identity and health: the negative cache was stamped before the SCIM call and unbounded (CR-7, AR-04); malformed SCIM groups escaped as `TypeError` (AR-12); the `http://` branch sent bearers in clear to any host (N6); `DATABRICKS_HOST` was never validated (EI-N2); parallel cold requests each made a SCIM call (EI-W3); one abandoned probe stalled every later round (EI-W4, AR-11); the identity probe tested a different path from the one requests use (EI-W1); the SCIM mapping and the F45 branches were untested (EI-W5); docstrings cited files that do not exist and edge mode hard-coded `caos-admins` (EI-W6, EI-N5, EI-N6, EI-W2). All fixed in `caos/api/{identity,edge,health}.py`; edge mode reads the configured group names, and its removal is N12.
- F99 — One model-written heading with a 60 KB whitespace run made the vendor validator backtrack for a minute holding the GIL, on every read of the accepted handoff (AI-1, SA-C5). The host refuses any line with a space or tab run over 256 before any vendor validator runs; the vendor file is untouched.
- F100 — Text no reviewer can see (Unicode format characters, the tag block) entered evidence and prompts, and hidden text in a model's own answer was carried downstream (AI-2). `boundary_text.hides_text` refuses `Cf` outside ZWNJ, ZWJ and the soft hyphen at admission and on `canonical_markdown`; `BoundaryText.of` itself is unchanged because six parity goldens record `Cf` passing; PDF render-mode and colour detection is N27.
- F101 — A handoff could carry any number of citations, each scanning the whole body (AI-5). Capped at 512; the body's words are indexed once.
- F102 — The PDF child's decoded-bytes budget covered `zlib` alone and the child had no memory limit (AS-1); one admission could hold 25M token objects (AS-2); every evidence-page read started a fresh interpreter (AS-5). Every pdfminer decoder draws from the one budget under an address-space limit where the platform allows one; a pack-level token ceiling; a 256-entry frame cache by digest and page.
- F103 — F33's NFC comparison in the exact pass made a page carrying a word both composed and decomposed ambiguous (CR-4): bytes first, NFC only in the normalised pass. `_finite` clamps non-finite deadlines so `float('inf')` never reaches `communicate` (CR-10, AR-16).
- F104 — Admission uploaded every document to the volume inside the transaction holding the case lock (DL-7). The puts come first; the rows are inserted under the lock.
- F105 — A double press on a Run control sent a second governed POST with a fresh idempotency key, and any answer that was not a validated receipt or a typed refusal (a proxy's HTML 504, a lost body) drew a new key on retry (FE-1, FE-8, AR-19). `useCommand.run` is guarded by an in-flight ref; the key is kept until an answer settles it.
- F106 — A failed background refetch replaced a good document and discarded unsaved input (FE-2); a refused reconnect closed the event tail for good, silently (FE-3); the first open was not a resync (FE-9); the "could not be refreshed" note never cleared (FE-10). The document is kept and marked not live; the tail reopens on a backoff honouring `Retry-After`; every open refetches; the note clears.
- F107 — Navigation dropped focus to `<body>` and the title was "CAOS" everywhere (FE-4); reflow failed at 320 px and the gate never measured it (FE-5); status changes and successes were not announced (FE-6); irreversible acts fired on one activation (FE-7); an empty tablist rendered on every section (FE-11); landmark names repeated and the rail hid its state (FE-12). Title per section and case, focus to the section heading, scroll regions instead of clipping with a 320×640 viewport in the gate (240/240), a persistent live region per section, an inline confirm step on sign, freeze, file, withdraw, revoke and cancel, no empty tablist, landmarks named by route node.
- Declined in round 3, with the rule that holds: FP-22 (negative CFO refusing is the legacy rule, golden `annual_cfo_negative`); AI-4 and AI-6 (the citation rule and the spotlighting text are prompt-parity and citation-parity bound; N28); FP-06's `BoundaryText` part (goldens record `Cf` passing). Noted without change: N3 (a deploy leaves one governed case; cases have no delete), AI-8 (the smoke's two paid calls are the one call outside the ledger, runbook §6), EI-N1/EI-N8/EI-N9 (the 300 s role cache is F13; the health fields are spec §3.1; proxy isolation is the platform's boundary), MX-7 (`verify-full` with `PGSSLROOTCERT` is a bundle env setting, runbook §6).
- F108 — Deliverable and qualification (the second pass, FP-01..FP-41, AR-08..AR-10, AR-15, AR-18, AR-22, AR-23, AR-25, SI-3..SI-5): a declared refusal is met only by a run that ended in it, on the terminal attempt of a node that stayed unaccepted, and `HANDOFF_BLOCKED` needs a blocking verdict row (FP-01); the release pack counts a pathway only through a case on that pathway that proved, answered its keys and completed, reads pins through `route_pin`, re-derives `complete` and re-digests `performed_json` rather than trusting stored flags, and carries the provider and model it qualified (FP-02, FP-03, FP-14, AR-22); filing re-proves the revision and filed reads no longer depend on live state (FP-04, FP-05); `_eligible` compares the pinned subject, verdicts bind their evidence (FP-12, FP-13); `qualify.py` resolves routes from the verified catalog, keeps a run's blobs under its own root, admits a multi-case set with a distinct run ceiling and captures rows with `dataclasses.asdict` (FP-15, FP-16, AR-18, SI-5, R2-N2); the on-disk loader bounds every string as pinned state, counts documents and bytes before reading, validates forecast keys, and refuses contradictory scalar expectations before any call (FP-08, AR-09, AR-25, AR-23); concurrent identical signatures replay idempotently (AR-10); every optional field in the set digest is tagged (SI-4, N8 closed); the portable verifier cross-checks each narrative figure against the citation it names and reads a string narrative as carrying none (FP-17); `io_budget --assert` refuses a declaration that declares nothing and the document register imports the request ceiling from `caos.provider` and resolves paths (FP-18, FP-19, FP-20). The receipt keeps naming the latest signer (FP-09 read as a shape change that would strand filed deliverables, R3-5); the loader's typed code for a string the boundary refuses is `QUALIFICATION_SET_FILE_INVALID`, the file being the one thing the loader can name. AR-08 (rendered list numbering) stays declined: the render parity goldens hold the legacy output.
- F109 — The round-3 re-review of this patch (`docs/rebuild/round3/findings-round3.md`, stopped by the account's spend limit after five findings) found the first advisory lock deadlocking against LangGraph's `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` (R3-1, fixed by polling `pg_try_advisory_lock` between statements), the first narrative check refusing every string narrative (R3-2, fixed), `sync.paths` dropping the git-ignored export (R3-3, fixed with `sync.include`), five red gates on the snapshot (R3-4, all green on the closing tree) and the receipt-shape change (R3-5, withdrawn as F108 says). Its remaining scope is N30.
- F110 — The first live calls (2026-09-23, OpenRouter, `anthropic/claude-opus-5`, the owner's key and a 14.00 budget). The test-only adapter named a dated Opus id OpenRouter does not serve; it names `anthropic/claude-opus-5`. `tests/qualify_openrouter.py` drives `scripts/qualify.py` through that adapter with an `openrouter/...` identity, so a set can be performed before a workspace exists without OpenRouter leaving `tests/`. The live smoke (`tests/test_live_run.py`, two synthetic documents) reached CP-0, whose accepted handoff ran to 36,544 bytes, and the next node refused `UPSTREAM_SECTION_OVER_CEILING` at the legacy 32 KiB per-section bound before any call; with the bound lifted the route ran and ended BLOCKED, CP-0 reading a six-line pack as source-limited, which is the methodology's verdict and not a host fault. `MAX_UPSTREAM_HANDOFF_BYTES` is 52 KiB, the most the catalog's widest node (CP-5, sixteen upstreams, 165,548 bytes of authority) can carry under the 1 MiB request; the widest-node test now asserts the fit and the measured handoff plus room, not a quarter left for evidence. CI's accessibility matrix failed on Linux at 320 px on a 19 px text field the local run had passed: `.fld` inputs carry `min-height: 24px`.
- F111 — Three qualification sets were performed live for the first time on the rebuilt host (2026-09-23, OpenRouter, Claude Opus 5 and Opus 5.5, $5.23 of the owner's $14.00). Every host step held: the envelope parsed, the text bounds and the citation quoting ran, the reservation and the record were written, the refusal was typed, and no key or document text reached a log. Every CP-0 answer was refused by the vendor's own validator for writing `qa_status: Passed` beside a finding it had itself tagged MATERIAL, the rule `CANON_SHARED.md` line 373 states in the authority the call receives; one Opus 5.5 answer also cited nine quotes that did not appear verbatim in its body. No artifact was accepted, so every pathway stays NOT_QUALIFIED; the record is `qualification/PROVIDER_RUNBOOK.md`. Four cheaper models were then measured on the two-call set: Sonnet 5 and Gemini 2.5 Flash met the vendor's rules and failed the host's verbatim-quote rule for citations (the final-check block asks for it and they did not do it), Haiku 4.5 broke the pathway's decision scope, DeepSeek V3.2 was refused 4xx by the provider before inference. Gemini 2.5 Flash reaches the same point as Opus for one fortieth of the price. The host restates no vendor rule in the prompt (invariant 4, prompt parity), so the remedy is N32.
- F112 — The test-only OpenRouter adapter built `ChatOpenAI` on the library's defaults, a 600 s timeout and two retries, so one hung call held a live driver on the wire for over half an hour (the production model carries `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and `max_retries=0` since F40). The adapter carries the same. Two attempts on Gemini 2.5 Flash then both refused at CP-0 (one citation unquoted; then an answer that was not a JSON object), closing the day at $7.59 of the $14.00 authorised with no accepted artifact from any of six models: the agent-side remedy is N32.

Round 4 (2026-09-23): five adversarial reviewers (Claude Opus 5.5 at effort max) over 653dc9c, 60 findings in `docs/rebuild/round4/findings-{store,edge,evidence,deliverable,deploy}.md`, every one re-run by the coordinator from its probe (the probes are in the git-ignored `docs/rebuild/runs/round4-2026-09-23/`), plus the 22 MAX findings and 3 notes of the separate audit in `docs/rebuild/findings.md`. Six patchers, one per slice, closed them; each fuller paragraph is in the commit that closed the round.

- F113 — The Lakebase credential cache (ST-1, ST-2/MAX-03, ST-3/MAX-15). A caller that arrived while another minted waited out the mint, up to 30 s, beside a token the server still accepted: it is now handed the live token at once, and only a caller with no live token waits. Only the worker and the checkpoint pool dropped a refused credential, so the API, health and the lifespan kept presenting it for up to 14 minutes: `caos.store.connect` drops it on any connection failure, bounded to one mint per `FAILURE_SECONDS`. A token stated to expire sooner than 14 minutes is refreshed by its stated expiry, the autoscaling credential's `expire_time` is read, and a failed mint re-reads the cache rather than a snapshot another path just refused.
- F114 — Checkpoint set-up and the in-process worker (ST-4/MAX-20, ST-13, ST-14; CF-042). Set-up waited without a deadline on any older snapshot in the database, before uvicorn bound, and a process that gave up had no worker for life. `start_in_process` does no store work: the worker thread configures itself under the loop's back-off; each set-up statement carries a 30 s statement and lock timeout refusing `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, and an index a cancelled `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` left invalid is dropped under the set-up lock. The local checkpointer is the platform's checked pool, so one ended session no longer fails every later claim. The saver's serializer loads exactly `RunState` plus primitives and refuses `json`, `pickle` and every other extension row `INTERNAL_FAULT`; `runtime.message_free` strips the message of any non-refusal fault leaving a node, so `repr(exc)` no longer reaches `checkpoint_writes`.
- F115 — Fencing and cancellation at the end of a run (ST-5, ST-6, MAX-04; CF-038). A worker whose lease had been taken deleted the checkpoint thread the new holder was driving: `_settle` returns whether its write moved the row, and a worker forgets a thread only after its own `stop` or `cancel_run` moved it. AR-13's refusal covered only a move to COMPLETE, so a cancel acknowledged during a Blocked last call ended BLOCKED: `_transition` refuses `RUN_CANCEL_REQUESTED` on any holder's move but CANCELLED. A cancel acknowledged during a call whose answer then failed validation left the run RUNNING until a second cancel: `work.stop` locks the run first and ends a held run with a recorded cancel CANCELLED.
- F116 — The model call (ST-7, ST-8/MAX-16, ST-9/MAX-21; CF-040, CF-045). A 429 back-off re-sent the paid request after a cancel, a SIGTERM or a lost lease: `caos.provider.resend_checked` runs every installed check before a re-send (the worker's "not stopping", the executor's read-only `work.require_resendable`), and a refused re-send records nothing since no model was reached. A `Retry-After: nan` escaped as `ValueError` and an empty `choices` as `IndexError`: a non-finite wait is the default, and anything raised once the request may have been sent is `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`. `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` bounded each read, not the call, so three tries could hold 760 s against the 600 s lease: 240 s is now one deadline over the whole of `complete()`, each `invoke` runs on a helper thread abandoned at the remaining deadline (indeterminate, reservation kept), and the lease and stale thresholds are asserted against the computed worst case. Streaming with renewal stays N14.
- F117 — Money (ST-11/MAX-N03, ST-12, DF-10; CF-046, CF-090, CF-007). A null or absent output count was billed as zero and an impossible count as stated: a charge is unknown when the request billed zero input, a non-empty answer zero output, input exceeds the request's bytes or output exceeds `MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS`, and it is computed in `pricing.exact_context()` so excess precision refuses rather than rounds. One store fault at the billing write lost a paid answer and the next claim paid again: `canonical.bill` retries `record_outcome` three times, on a fresh connection when the fault closed it. The price parser accepted a zero or negative-zero rate, a future date and non-ASCII digits: each refuses `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`, in the app and in preflight alike. MAX-05's remainder (bool, numeric-string and float counts are ints before the host sees them) is N34.
- F118 — The drain ran twice under SIGINT, and two docstrings promised what the numbers did not (ST-15): `serve.drain_once` runs it once; the worker's docstring says a call is billed only if it answers within the join, and `provider.py` names the 600 s lease.
- F119 — A closed event tail kept its stream slot until CPython's next full garbage collection, so four section changes spent an actor's share and every later tail answered 503 (ED-1, CF-052; round 3's per-actor cap turned a delay into a lockout). The route returns `_TailResponse`, which releases the slot and closes the generator in its own `__call__` `finally`, run at once on a disconnect; the generator's `finally` and the `weakref.finalize` stay as backstops. The test serves the process entry over uvicorn with the collector disabled, and `gc.collect()` is gone from the old one.
- F120 — Typed refusals at the edge (ED-2, ED-3, ED-6, ED-9; CF-023, CF-024, CF-082). Through `caos.api.site:application` an unhandled fault answered Starlette's `text/plain` 500, because the app's guard passed a guarded scope straight through: that branch now answers `INTERNAL_FAULT` if nothing has started, and only the outermost guard logs. `workspace_address` accepted whitespace and returned IPv6 hosts with no port: the host must be a DNS name or an IP literal, the port is explicit, and the SCIM connection is built inside the `try`. `VolumeBackend` caught only `OSError`, so the SDK's hourly token refresh failing escaped as `ValueError`/`NotImplementedError`: each call maps them to `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`. A SCIM 200 with no usable id is `IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE`, uncached; only 401 and 403 are refusals.
- F121 — Health (ED-4/MAX-02, DF-1). AR-11's time gate let copies of a slow probe pile up and a probe that never returns starve the shared executor: each probe runs on its own daemon thread, is never started while that thread lives (answering `PROBE_TIMEOUT`), and the others run every round. On a redeploy the previous process's last beat stayed fresh for 300 s and answered `workers=OK` for a process whose worker had refused to start: with `CAOS_WORKER_IN_PROCESS=1` the probe requires a live `caos-worker` thread in this process and reads only its own row.
- F122 — F104 held for `admit_prepared` alone: the route called it inside `governed`, whose unit already held the case row and the audit chain head, so every upload ran under both (ED-5). `put_pack` uploads before any unit opens, and `admit_prepared` only locks and inserts.
- F123 — Declared I/O budgets were fixture shapes and blob reads had none: one Analysis read on the forecast route cost 149 round trips against a declared 37 and downloaded 91 blobs, 20 distinct (ED-7, CF-055). The `Blobs` dependency is a request-scoped memo of verified bytes (91 downloads became 20); Analysis is declared for the longest pinned route (249, `BLOB_BUDGET` 40), Model and Book follow it, and the stream counts its cursor recheck. A measured gate and a harness for the 20-node route are N35.
- F124 — SCIM waits were bounded per socket operation, so a trickling answer held the lookup and every sharer, each on one of AnyIO's forty threads (ED-8): the exchange runs on a helper thread with a 10 s total deadline, and at most 16 request threads wait on the workspace; the next is refused at once with `Retry-After`. An async identity dependency is N36. The UUID-parser guard scanned `server/api`, which does not exist; it scans `caos/api` and asserts it reached the route modules (MAX-N01).
- F125 — Admission cut a line's blocks by its NFC length and anchoring counted the stored, un-normalised length, so an NFD line made a whole source uncitable, and a line that shrank beside one that grew handed each the other's blocks (EV-1, CF-014; CRITICAL). `_group_counts` sums `length(normalize(text, NFC))` in the database; a test holds Postgres's NFC lengths to Python's on seven shapes. A Unicode-version drift between the two is N40.
- F126 — F99's 256-space bound was per run, so a heading built of many shorter runs, or a long trailing `#` run, still cost the vendor's heading expressions 2–8 s per validation, GIL held, on every read (EV-2; CRITICAL). `handoff._text` refuses a heading whose title carries a run of more than eight spaces, tabs or `#` (only trailing spaces and tabs exempt); every heading in the goldens, fixtures, sets and bundle passes. The host's own `forecast._BLOCK` was quadratic over unclosed openers (EV-6): `_blocks` scans forward and stops at an opener with no closer. Validation cost at `MAX_FILE_BYTES` is N39.
- F127 — Invisible text (EV-3, EV-5). `hides_text` refused only `Cf`, so variation selectors (a full byte channel), unassigned tags, Hangul fillers, the grapheme joiner and the braille blank entered evidence: it refuses every Default_Ignorable_Code_Point and U+2800, keeping a presentation selector only after a drawn character. `_printable` dropped three characters while the handoff refused the wider set, so a filename CP-0 was shown could not be quoted back: `boundary_text.visible` removes exactly what `hides_text` refuses, and `_printable` applies it. The cost is real text that carries the same code points: a Japanese ideographic variation sequence, a variation selector after a CJK ideograph, the Hebrew grapheme joiner and Mongolian variation selectors now refuse admission `SOURCE_NOT_READABLE`; accepting only registered sequences is N49.
- F128 — Citation meaning and cost (EV-4, MAX-06, EV-8; CF-002, CF-015). A quote could drop or add accounting parentheses or a leading point at its edge, and single-digit cells joined into a number, and still anchor as host-verified: an edge word with a digit keeps its parentheses and leading point, single digits never join, and `NORMALISATION_VERSION` is `"2"`; no golden moved. `_one_match` held every matching slice and compared from every start: a KMP pass proposes starts, each is checked in constant time, and the search stops at the second match (512 citations on a 240,000-token page: 2.5 s exact, 18 s through both passes). Four legacy citation goldens pin compatibility forms, and the gate's floor is 18.
- F129 — Evidence-page children (EV-7, CF-049 in part). A missing page was never remembered and nothing bounded concurrent children: the child's "no such page" is cached like a crop, and children run in two process-wide slots waited for within the read's deadline. A per-actor share is N38.
- F130 — Qualification scoring (DQ-1, DQ-2, DQ-3; CF-029, FP-03). A declared key whose reader refused read `None`, which beside a met `HANDOFF_BLOCKED` waived a wrong answer into a signed snapshot: a refused reader answers `False` and a row carrying any reader refusal is never answered. Sixteen declarable refusals could never be met, because a node refusal leaves its run RUNNING: `DECLARABLE_REFUSALS` is `{HANDOFF_BLOCKED}` and the last attempt is chosen by `ordinal`. Re-derivation stopped at the snapshot's own flags: a met refusal waives COMPLETE only over a BLOCKED or FAILED run, `store.assert_store_agrees` compares every run's status and artifacts with the store in `record_verdict` and `current_verdict`, and the pack raises `VERDICT_BINDING_INVALID` for evidence that does not join its snapshot. Terminal node refusals are N42, a keyed digest N43.
- F131 — The release pack and verdict dates (DQ-4/MAX-12, DQ-13; CF-031, CF-028). A pack filtered to another identity was byte-identical to one over an empty store, the Markdown copy named no identity, and timestamps followed `PGTZ`: `store` records the identity and adapter version, the reader's copy names provider, model, set and signer, and every moment prints in UTC. A verdict dated before its snapshot is refused, and migration 0031 records when each verdict was signed. The reviewer's document naming its digest, and revocation, are N44.
- F132 — `scripts/qualify.py` (DQ-5/MAX-10, DQ-6/MAX-11, DQ-7, DQ-12). A manifest moving after the proofs discarded three paid calls: `perform` persists the unsignable snapshot, then refuses. The capture listed the first run's attempts only: every run's, with `run_ids`. `--run-ceiling 0` was falsy and replaced: a named ceiling passes through and a zero is refused before the database exists. `harness.assert_admissible` runs every pre-write check before `CREATE DATABASE`, and the tests drop what they create.
- F133 — The portable verifier (DQ-8, DQ-15; CF-083). A sixth local `deliverable.html` before the central directory verified while a streaming reader extracted it: the five entries must tile the archive from byte 0 with no gap. A text span or string narrative stating a figure verified, and page `1.0` matched page 1: the figure rule is re-applied to spans, a non-list narrative is refused, and figure fields compare by type.
