- F299 — The checkpointer pool had no socket bounds and no statement bound (review 1: W4): `store.SOCKET_BOUNDS` is shared with `connect()`, and pooled connections start with a 30 s statement timeout.

- F300 — A stopped or store-faulted pass released a cancelled run without forgetting its thread (review 1: N1): both branches forget after releasing.

- F301 — A `lock_timeout` (55P03) during `apply_schema` was final drift (review 1: N2): it is `STORE_UNAVAILABLE` and retried.

- F302 — Every boot needed CREATE on the database (review 1: N3): a schema is created only when absent, so a least-privilege role boots on schemas it owns.

- F303 — `connect()` replaced the operator's `options` from the connection string or `PGOPTIONS` (review 1: N4): `store.startup_options` puts the operator's first and ours last.

- F304 — `store_connection` answered every `psycopg.Error` with 503 and logged nothing (review 1: N5): only `store.interrupted` faults are `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`; the rest are `INTERNAL_FAULT` with the edge's class-and-frame line.

- F305 — CP-2G's driver rows and CP-CF's request were compared without reversing the vendor's sign convention (outflows negative), so a dividend could never match and an acquisition was booked as an inflow (review 2: C2; invariant 7): each mapped row declares a negated conversion, compared in Decimal with `copy_negate`, and CP-CF's brief and the forecast extension state the convention; three model-owner prompt goldens regenerated from the legacy snapshot with the text mirrored. Tests at the mapping and `test_cp_cf_maps_each_cp2g_sign_to_the_calculator_end_to_end`.

- F306 — `driver_value` negated with unary minus, which rounds in the ambient context (review 2: N14): it uses `copy_negate`.

- F307 — D44's tolerance was shared between debt and cash, so a large debt let a cash residual far past its own size reconcile (review 2: N10): each residual is held to one part in a thousand of its own opening balance, still capped by the stated tolerance and `MAX_TOLERANCE`.

- F308 — A 429 resend could start with seconds left and be abandoned while billed (review 2: N11): `MIN_RESEND_SECONDS` (half the call deadline, 120 s) is checked before the wait and after the resend fence.

- F309 — F209's one-selector rule could be bypassed through ZWJ, ZWNJ and soft hyphen, and VS1–VS14 after any letter left 4 hidden bits a character (review 2: W5): shaping characters are not drawn bases and their runs are only Unicode's defined sequences; VS1–VS14 only after CJK ideographs and small registered sets; VS15/VS16 after any drawn character; U+034F only between combining marks. A Latin letter now carries under 3.5 bits; Japanese IVS names still admit.

- F334 — `bills_at` compared an unpriced provider by model name only (review 2: N15; invariant 8): it fails closed, `price` is a member of both provider protocols (which found `worker._Stoppable` typed `object`), and the suite's fakes state their run price.

- F335 — Integration of the fix batches: the store fixes' straggler double and worker tests state their run price (F334); the demo's CP-1B, CP-2B and CP-2G handoffs carry their tables in their markdown, so what is served derives from it.

- F336 — F190 turned the static export's directory redirect into a cross-origin open redirect: uvicorn decodes `/%2Fevil.example%2F..` to `//evil.example/..`, Starlette normalised it to the export root and answered `307 Location: //evil.example/../` with the query carried, and FastAPI's trailing-slash redirect echoed the client's `Host` (review 3: C1). Neither redirects any more: a directory without its slash under the export and an API path with a trailing slash (`redirect_slashes=False`) answer 404 `ENDPOINT_NOT_FOUND`. Tested over a real uvicorn socket in dev and platform mode.

- F337 — The render read served a frozen, unfiled revision, a withdrawn source's quotes included, after Report and Committee refused it, because it checked only for a publication row (review 3: W2): Committee's proof is one function, `reports.proven_revision`, and the render and package reads run it in the same read unit; the package runs `proven_filing`, which answers `DELIVERABLE_NOT_FOUND` for anything not filed.

- F338 — The package download packed the current renderer, so a revision filed under an earlier one failed its own verifier (review 3: W1): filing builds the audit package once, stores it by digest and records `package_sha256` on the receipt row (migration `0041`); the download serves those bytes only when the archive's receipt equals the filing's proven receipt, else 500 `ARTIFACT_RECORD_MISMATCH`; a filing that predates 0041 answers 404 `DELIVERABLE_PACKAGE_NOT_STORED` (no deployment predates it); a payload the renderer refuses refuses the filing.

- F339 — A writer's new draft over a frozen revision blocked that revision's filing (review 3: W5): a frozen, unfiled head takes no new draft (`DELIVERABLE_ALREADY_FROZEN`, 400) and Report stops offering the save; once filed, a new draft supersedes it and the filed record, receipt and package stay as they are.

- F340 — A leftover `CAOS_EDGE_TOKEN` booted silently in dev mode, where a loopback client's `x-caos-role` header was trusted (review 3: W6): boot refuses `EDGE_CONFIG_INVALID` in both modes while it is set, naming nothing secret; it is the only variable F188 retired.

- F341 — The admission slot was held while the upload streamed in, with no deadline, and each waiter held a store connection and an in-flight place (review 3: W3; review 2: N12): the pack is received whole and spooled before the slot is taken (documents read into memory only under it), the standing check's connection closes before the upload, the edge bounds a request body's arrival (10 s plus the declared length at 128 KiB/s, else `REQUEST_INVALID`), and `serve.py`'s comment says what uvicorn's keep-alive timer covers. Tests in `tests/test_admission_slots.py`.

- F342 — A pack more than one document over CF-075's ceiling answered 400 `REQUEST_INVALID` (review 3: N2): the parser refuses the part past the ceiling `SOURCE_TOO_LARGE` as it ends.

- F343 — A caller's own research-brief mistakes (NFD text, an empty `completion_test`, an impossible `as_of_date`, an unknown consumer, a brief on a LITE route) answered 500 `RUN_INPUT_INVALID`, an operator fault (review 3: W4): a refused pin re-checks the brief alone against the pinned route and answers 400 `RESEARCH_BRIEF_INVALID` when the brief is the cause; brief text is NFC-normalised at the boundary; the pin form's question id starts `RQ-1` (its prose fields are the caller's to write, and an untouched form gets the 400). A pin that succeeds pays no extra read.

- F344 — The research brief's wire bounds could not be sent under the 16 KiB command body limit (review 3: N1): the pin command's limit is 144 KiB, room for the store's 64 KiB brief escaped twice over.

- F345 — `metrics.fcf_to_debt` was labelled a multiple (review 3: N3): it is a `RATIO` in Model; the margin stays an unscaled ratio in Model and a percent in Book, each labelled (R24-12).

- F346 — A publication row that disagreed with its revision answered 400 `DELIVERABLE_PAYLOAD_INVALID`, a caller fault, and `verdict_reviewer_id`'s docstring said six bindings (review 3: N4): the shared proof answers 500 `ARTIFACT_RECORD_MISMATCH` for Committee and both downloads; the docstring says seven.

- F347 — The boot check `site._complete` (DF-4) verified only the files `index.html` names, so an export missing a lazily loaded section view (F317) booted `ready` (N92): Vite writes its build manifest (`.vite/manifest.json`) and boot requires every file each entry names (`file`, `css`, `assets`), read once under `MANIFEST_CAP` (1 MiB) and failing closed on a manifest missing, unreadable, oversized, malformed, without an `index.html` entry, or naming a path outside the root or through a dot directory; `_Export` serves no dot path, so the manifest, which lists source paths, is never served. Tests `test_an_export_missing_a_lazy_section_view_refuses_boot`, `test_a_missing_or_malformed_manifest_is_not_a_complete_export`, `test_a_manifest_past_its_cap_is_not_read`, `test_the_production_build_is_a_complete_export_to_its_manifest`, `test_the_stand_in_export_is_complete_to_the_boot_check`.

- F348 — `cash_flow._tolerance` was fixed from the chain's first openings, so after a deleveraging year a later residual far past its own balances reconciled (review 5: W-A, N75): each period caps the stated tolerance per residual by the debt and cash it opened with. Test `test_each_period_is_held_to_its_own_opening_balances`.

- F349 — Save, sign and freeze never rendered, so a payload the renderer refuses was refused only at filing after two approvals, and F339 then closed the frozen head to every draft (review 5: W-C, N77): `revisions.renderable` runs at save and freeze with the render's own code, and a frozen head that no longer re-proves or renders takes a superseding draft (availability says so). Test `test_a_payload_the_renderer_refuses_is_refused_before_it_can_wedge_a_run`.

- F350 — The Report offered Sign to the revision's author, whom `sign_opinion_in` refuses (review 5: N-2, N78): `FilingFacts.actor_saved` shows it refused `APPROVER_NOT_INDEPENDENT`. Test `test_the_narratives_author_is_not_offered_the_signature`.

- F351 — A filed revision's render re-rendered its payload with the deployed renderer (review 5: W-B, N76): it serves the page stored in its package once the package carries the filing's proven receipt (render budget 20 IO, 3 blobs). Test `test_a_filed_revisions_page_is_the_one_stored_at_filing`.

- F352 — The filed-receipt and publication chain checks answered 400 `DELIVERABLE_PAYLOAD_INVALID` for the server's own records (review 5: N-1, N74): they answer 500 `ARTIFACT_RECORD_MISMATCH`.

- D50 (2026-09-24; review 4: W2, W7) — PyYAML is a direct dev dependency (`pyyaml==6.0.3`, already locked through langchain-core and bandit): the gate checker and the stand-in read `ci.yml`, the pre-commit config and `databricks.yml` structurally, since text windows let a moved `if:`, a quoted key, a `defaults.run` shell or an `exit 0` switch a gate off unnoticed.

- F353 — The documented rollback (check out the previous commit, run the one command) boots `STORE_SCHEMA_DRIFT` across a migration, and a commit from before F219 boots on an empty store in `public` (review 4: C1): `scripts/rollback_check.py <older>` refuses an older commit whose migrations or store schema differ from the release's, and `DEPLOYMENT.md` §6 and `ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` run it first; otherwise roll forward, or restore into a new database (the `docs/MIGRATIONS.md` backup or a point-in-time Lakebase branch), proven by E6 and E8. Tests in `tests/test_rollback_check.py`.

- F354 — E10 counted any two progress frames as proof, so a gateway that refused the app's own call (403, 404, 429) or a park passed (review 4: W1): E10 requires an answered call and names the outcome code otherwise.

- F355 — `check_gate_config` read `ci.yml` and the pre-commit config in text windows, so a moved or quoted `if`, `defaults.run`, `exit 0`, a trap, a heredoc, `working-directory`, a races step without its environment, and pre-commit's `exclude`, `default_stages`, `types` or a forked repo all passed while switching gates off; the gitleaks gate was checked by any mention; F171's `${{` check read raw text (review 4: W2, W3, N3): both files are read as YAML and every required step is pinned, the gitleaks command included.

- F356 — A bare or aliased `skip` and a `live_provider` marker were never counted (review 4: W4): both are counted, `live_provider` as its own kind at its current count.

- F357 — `--against` ran the change's own checker, so a change could loosen a pattern and a suppression with it (review 4: W5): it also holds both trees to the base revision's own checker, loaded with its own `scripts/` siblings.

- F358 — `io_budget --assert` passed when its record, or a measured module's entry in it, was missing (review 4: W6): either is refused.

- F359 — A `workspace.host` in `databricks.yml`, an attached `-pNAME` or `--profile=`, or inherited Databricks auth variables took the stand-in off loopback (review 4: W7, N1): the stand-in and `check_gate_config` refuse host and auth keys and every attached profile form, and clear the inherited variables.

- F360 — E2 did not hold the group names F276 moved onto `BUNDLE_VAR_`, and accepted any Postgres branch or database path (review 4: W8, N2): E2 holds the groups, commas included, and the bound branch and database to what was given.

- F361 — The vocabulary gate missed star captures in match patterns and digit-suffixed names (review 4: N4): both are read.

- F362 — F245's single source had gaps: literal group defaults in `preflight.py`, an inherited `MODEL_ENDPOINT`, `MODEL_PRICE` or `RUN_CEILING` silently winning, and an inline comment read into a default (review 4: N5): `databricks.yml` is the one source and the environment sets only what the header lists.

- F363 — A bill landing after a pass read the ledger, or a call still on the wire when another worker took over the lapsed run, let a second paid attempt start at the node (review 1 residual; invariant 6): `start_attempt` refuses `ATTEMPT_UNSETTLED` (503), with nothing written, while an earlier attempt is owed a verdict (the `replay_billed` or `unexplained_charge` shapes) or its call is held by another session (`call_hold`, a session advisory lock from the pre-call check to the bill, counted for at most `CALL_HOLD_SECONDS`, two leases); the worker gives the run back and backs off. Four harness tests start their attempts before billing. Tests `test_a_bill_landing_after_a_pass_read_the_ledger_is_replayed_not_paid_twice`, `test_a_call_in_flight_past_its_lease_keeps_its_node_from_a_second_call`, `test_a_held_call_keeps_its_node_only_while_a_live_session_holds_it`, `test_a_charged_answer_holds_its_node_exactly_when_the_next_pass_settles_it`.

- F364 — The health store probe did not check who owns the store's schemas (review 1 residual, F297): it refuses a schema another role owns; the runbook says database CREATE is needed for the first boot only.

- F365 — Text in a Separation's `/None` colorant, or a DeviceN whose colorants are all `/None`, paints nothing (ISO 32000-1 §8.6.6.4) and was admitted unmarked (N27): it is marked `colorant_none`, decided by the colour each render mode paints with; `PdfExtractor` v8 (an existing fixture's mark moved; earlier rows verify as recorded); migration `0042`; `PageLine.hidden` holds up to six reasons. Tests `test_text_in_a_colorant_that_paints_nothing_is_marked`, `test_a_spot_ink_is_still_read_by_its_tint`.

- F366 — The approver's page read showed a pre-v7 token's stored line breaks (F290's remainder): `read_page` shows each line through `extract.one_line`, as the prompt does; stored tokens and digests are unchanged. Test `test_the_page_read_shows_a_line_stored_with_a_break_as_one_line`.

- F367 — The every-figure-is-cited rule's limit was undocumented against invariant 11 (review 5: N-3, N79): `_is_figure` states that it sees numeric characters, not numbers spelled in words, and that the signers' review holds prose quantities; the portable verifier applies the same rule.

- F368 — `NarrativeFigure` carried no rectangles and no withdrawal, so a saved figure's drawer drew no highlight and, on an unavailable page, could only guess its source "may have been withdrawn" (N93, F332): a figure carries its citation's `rects` from the saved record and its source's live `withdrawn_at` (`cited_source_ids` reads it in the query it already ran, no new IO), as a `CitationView` does; the drawer states the withdrawal it is served and the guess is gone. A hidden-text mark stays the page's to show (F315), as for every citation. Tests `test_report_reads_the_exact_saved_revision`, `test_cited_source_ids_resolves_the_run_pin_and_refuses_an_uncited_document`, and `evidence-page.test.tsx`.

- F369 — Two names from the fix batches tripped bandit's B105 (a `pass`/`token` name holding a constant), so the bandit floor failed on the merged tip: `pdf.TOKEN_LINE_BREAKS` is `GLYPH_LINE_BREAKS` (the identity key it writes is unchanged) and `check_gate_config._local` builds its hook body from pairs; no suppression.

### Design critique plan, 2026-09-23 (the `/impeccable critique` of `frontend/src`, 16/40, and the owner's plan built on it; branch `impeccable/plan`)

- D32 (2026-09-23) — The Analysis read serves each accepted handoff's `<!-- table-id: -->`-tagged tables as typed data, derived at request time from the Markdown it already verified, by the bundle's own reader: `caos.methodology.tables.handoff_tables` calls `cp_tables.parse_tables` from the verified contract (`VendorContract.cp_tables`, the module the completeness checker imports), in document order, each cell its text as that reader splits it. Nothing is stored; the Markdown stays the authority and the tables are model-authored as it is (§46.3); no store or blob read is added. A cell has a value only where the bundle's `parse_figure` reads a figure (its null vocabulary is null, `5,2` is no value), and the value is that spelling re-read with `Decimal`, never the vendor's float (invariant 7), in plain notation (`^-?[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$`); a figure past 64 characters has no value and keeps its text. Bounds: 64 tables, 32 columns, 2,000 rows, 4,096 characters a cell, 256 a table id; past one, or where the reader raises, the handoff serves no table and `tables_unavailable_reason` `TABLES_TOO_LARGE` or `TABLES_MALFORMED`. Alternatives: store tables at acceptance (a record change and a second authority); parse Markdown in the browser (the frontend never does); a host-only figure grammar (the bundle is the authority on what a figure is, invariant 4); the vendor's float (invariant 7). Evidence: `tests/test_handoff_tables.py`, `tests/test_model_section.py::test_analysis_serves_the_tagged_tables_each_owner_wrote`, `tests/test_wire_contract.py::test_a_table_cell_is_its_text_and_a_plain_decimal_string_or_null`.
- D33 (2026-09-23; superseded by D61) — Charts are drawn by React as SVG and d3 is maths only: `d3-scale` 4.0.2 and `d3-shape` 3.2.0 (and their `@types`), exact-pinned; no `d3-selection`, no full `d3`, nothing that writes `innerHTML`. Alternatives: `@antv/g2` rejected, because its `@antv/component` tooltip assigns `innerHTML`, which the production CSP (`require-trusted-types-for 'script'; trusted-types 'none'`) blocks, and its `chart-visualization` skill posts chart data to antv-studio.alipay.com; hand-written scales considered, but nice ticks and band arithmetic are where off-by-one and float-residue bugs live. Printed figures never come from d3: they are the API's exact decimal strings, and bridge and stack sums are exact BigInt counts; floats only place marks. Cost: about 10 kB gzip for d3, about 18 kB for the five primitives. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/chart-maths.test.ts`, `frontend/tests/unit/charts.test.tsx`.
- F151 — Committee and Report could not be reached from inside the workspace (critique P0): "Open case" linked Analysis only, Report needs `?run`, Committee `?run&revision`, and `?revision` was set only by a Report save. The rail now carries the displayed run into Report and Committee and a frozen or filed revision into Committee (`committeeRevisionOf`); the Report and Committee reads list the run's revisions newest first with how far each has gone (`RevisionSummary`, `REVISIONS_MAX` 64, one more round trip on every path that finds a revision, `IO_BUDGET` 46/20/53); Report lists them with Open and Committee links; a case section missing its run or revision is the new `choose` state, not "Unavailable or not permitted.". Evidence: `tests/test_governed_write_routes.py::test_report_lists_the_runs_revisions_so_committee_has_a_front_door`, `frontend/tests/unit/selection.test.tsx`.
- F152 — Evidence was unreadable at the moment of verification (critique P0): the page text layer inherited the terminal's light text on paper (1.14:1) and model prose was an unwrapped `<pre>` clipped by its panel. `.pagerender` sets `--paper-ink`; `pre.model-text` wraps at 72ch in the body face; prose past 20,000 characters is shown on request. Offline and refused regions offer TRY AGAIN; the verbless "Clears when a case you are a member of…" reads "Clears when the case exists and you are a member of it." Evidence: `selection.test.tsx` (retry), `analysis.test.tsx` (long prose).
- F153 — Type below any reading floor, invisible fields and non-tabular numbers (critique P1): the scale is raised to floors of 10px labels, 11px tabular mono data and 12px body (DESIGN.md, owner-approved), every `.fld` field and the narrative draft draw a 3:1 edge (`--field-edge`), Book and Model figure columns are mono, tabular and right-aligned, and figures read rounded (`displayDecimal`, on the digits, never a float) with the exact decimal kept in the passport and the cell's title. Evidence: `helpers.test.ts::test_displayDecimal_rounds_on_the_digits_and_groups_thousands`, `book.test.tsx`.
- F154 — The four bands were hollow (critique P1): `composeChrome` now composes every cell from the section's own body facts (run status and what it waits on, open gates, the conclusion module's committee status, weak modules, citations and withdrawals, revision state), a cell with nothing to say is null and not drawn, the ribbon names the issuer, the h1 is a real heading, the verdict wash is flat, and a severity beside its own word is announced once (`SeverityMark decorative`). Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/compose.test.tsx`.
- F155 — Refusals were written for implementers, in critical red (critique P1): a refusal reads as one plain sentence (`refusalText`), its code and clearance kept in the control's detail (`refusalDetail`, `ActionReason.reasonTitle`); refused controls are quiet, red is kept for outcomes; rationale paragraphs are closed help disclosures; Upload says a shared withdraw refusal once; Directory's case access is a closed line where the reader administers nothing; a gate preview's failure no longer claims a write may have happened; command success sentences drop "Reading it back"; a confirm step's dismiss reads "Go back". Evidence: `helpers.test.ts`, `states.test.tsx`, `upload.test.tsx`, `confirm.test.tsx`.
- F156 — Analysis was nine expanded cards of raw text (critique; owner-requested redesign on the legacy three-pane desk): the modules are the section's tabs with their QA state as shape and hue (`?tab=` in the address, a native select on a phone), the body is an evidence rail (what the run rests on, then the module's citations), the module (name, status, figures from its tables, prose), and a right column about the selected thing (a pressed mark, else the module's provenance); the section opens on its conclusion (`conclusionOf`, never the CP-CF calculator). Model draws the host's forecast as solid lines where a case has two periods. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`, `model.test.tsx`.
- F157 — Run's right column was a nine-panel second menu and its route hid the frontier past the panel edge (critique): the run's acts moved beside the route in the order they happen, the diagram opens scrolled to where the work is (once per route), its scrollbar stays visible, nodes are sized for the new floors, and "Gates 2 of 2" became "n of m released". Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/route-graph.test.tsx`, `run.test.tsx`.
- F158 — Four pre-v1 fixtures (`report.json`, `committee.json`, `states/committee.filed.json`, `states/committee.reader.json`) were never served by the v1 middleware or rendered `WIRE_SHAPE_INVALID`; deleted.
- D35 (2026-09-23) — The workspace's UI layer is shadcn/ui's Nova style on Base UI, copied into `frontend/src/components/ui` by the shadcn CLI (4.21.0, `base-nova`) and owned from there (owner decision): `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0 for the primitives (button, menu, tooltip, dialog as the sheet, tabs, separator, input), `class-variance-authority` 0.7.1 and `cn` 0.4.0 (shadcn-ui's compiled class merging, in place of clsx and tailwind-merge) for variants, `lucide-react` 1.47.0 for icons, all exact-pinned. The CLI is a tool, not a dependency: `shadcn` and `tw-animate-css` came with `init` and were dropped, the eight Base UI state variants and `no-scrollbar` are vendored into `tokens.css`, and popups animate with Base UI's `data-starting-style`/`data-ending-style` transitions instead of keyframes. Alternatives: Radix primitives (shadcn's default), not chosen; Sonner for toasts, rejected because it inserts its stylesheet as an inline `<style>` element at load, which the production CSP's `style-src 'self'` blocks, so command outcomes stay inline beside the control that sent them; next-themes, rejected because its no-flash script is inline (`script-src 'self'`) and the CSS reads the system theme itself. The copies are edited where the workspace needs it: the sidebar keeps its open state in localStorage, not a cookie the API would receive; `SidebarInset` is a div so the header stays a banner; a button presses at `scale(0.97)`, and a refused (`aria-disabled`) one is dashed and quiet, never `disabled`. Cost: the JS bundle went from 450 kB to 707 kB (230 kB gzip). Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/chrome.test.tsx`, `frontend/tests/unit/theme.test.tsx`, the a11y matrix.
- D36 (2026-09-23) — Type is Geist and Geist Mono, self-hosted (owner decision): `@fontsource-variable/geist` and `@fontsource-variable/geist-mono` 5.3.0 (OFL-1.1) put the woff2 files in the build, so `font-src 'self'` holds and no request goes to a font host. Replaces DESIGN.md's "No web font" and its system stacks. The scale is shadcn's: 14px body, 13px figures and secondary text, 12px labels; chart ticks 11px and values 12px (`TICK_SIZE`, `VALUE_SIZE`); figures stay mono and tabular. Alternatives: system stacks (the previous rule), which the owner found prototype-like; Inter, a default; Vercel's `geist` package, which is shaped for Next.js. Cost: 29 kB (latin) and 23 kB (mono latin) of woff2 on first load. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/charts.test.tsx`, `frontend/tests/unit/chart-maths.test.ts`.
- D37 (2026-09-23) — Light and dark themes, the system's by default, and the shadcn app shell in place of the four bands (owner decision). A collapsible sidebar holds the nine sections with their icons, the case switcher (the directory's own read, asked for when it opens), the served role and the two refused foot controls, and is a sheet on a phone. A header holds the Case › Section trail with the section as the h1, the document's warnings, the run's state, at most three actions and the theme menu. A summary card opens the body: the verdict with its severity mark and what blocks it, revision and approval, the headline figure, then change, impact, next step and evidence. The dark variant is written once and matches both `.dark` and a system dark with no pick, so a dark reader is dark before any script runs; only a pick is stored (`src/app/theme.ts`). Every colour was measured: text 4.5:1 on each surface it sits on, tinted status chips included; fields and chart hues 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11); the chart ramp through the dataviz validator per theme; and the a11y matrix scans both themes (`SCHEMES`). Motion follows Emil Kowalski's rules: a strong custom ease-out, under 300 ms, a press scale, transitions rather than keyframes, and reduced motion keeping fades only. Supersedes DESIGN.md's dark-only terminal and its four bands; `ribbon`, `brief` and `verdict` remain the composed data they were. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/chrome.test.tsx`, `compose.test.tsx`, `tests/workbench/chrome.spec.ts`, `scripts/a11y-axe.mjs`.
- F159 (CF-036) — A RESTRICTED node was drawn and announced as WARNING, against CONTEXT.md ("not degraded, partial or warning"). It now has its own mark (a ring in the success hue), word and accessible name: `Severity` gains `RESTRICTED`, used for node states only. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/run.test.tsx`, `chrome.test.tsx::test_severity_renders_shape_and_hue`.
- F160 (CF-058) — A Create run answer that landed after the analyst had left wrote its run into the address and navigated them back to Run. The address is now corrected only while the form is still mounted. Evidence: `run.test.tsx` ("a Create run answer that lands after the analyst has left moves nothing"), which fails without the guard.
- F161 (CF-059) — Directory's Revoke and Grant, like every other `.rb` control outside `.confirm`, were 19 px targets. Every governed control is now the shadcn Button, at least 28 px (`sm`). Evidence: the a11y matrix's target-size probe.

### R24 frontend halves, 2026-09-24 (owner go, relayed by the audits session; branch `wp/r24-frontend` from `backlog/r24`)

- F310 — N4's frontend half (backend F221): Committee offered neither the rendered page nor the audit package the host now serves. Its case panel links `render_url` ("Open the rendered paper") and, once filed, `package_url` as a download; a frozen revision shows the package control refused with `PACKAGE_NOT_FILED`, not hidden. Evidence: `frontend/tests/unit/committee.test.tsx` ("offers the rendered paper, and the package once filed").
- F311 — CF-044's frontend half (backend F186): a parked run read "Running" in the register, the runs list and the Directory summary, though no worker drives it. `isParked` (a RUNNING run with a `stop_code`) marks it "Parked" with its stop code in the case register and the runs list, and the Directory summary counts parked runs apart from running ones and turns WARNING. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx` ("isParked: …"), `directory.test.tsx`, `run.test.tsx` ("a parked run … (CF-044)").
- F312 — N60's frontend half (backend F259): a margin read `0.20`. A Book cell now reads in its column's `unit` — percent (`20.0%`, the point moved on the digits, never through a float), multiple (`3.46x`), count, currency or none — and the passport names the row's currency and scale only for a currency column; the table's "Units" column is "Currency". Evidence: `book.test.tsx` ("a cell reads in the unit its column declares").
- F313 — N61's frontend half (backend F260): `sections/analysis/modules.ts`, a frontend map mirroring the bundle's stage slugs, is deleted; module names are the wire's `module_name` in Analysis (tabs, heading, pending nodes, the next step) and Run (the selected node, the diagram's tooltip). `conclusionOf` and `handoffSeverity` moved to `sections/analysis/tone.ts`. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx` ("a module is named by the bundle catalog's name on the wire").
- F314 — N48's frontend half (backend F195): Start and Retry were refused after a reload until a preview was read again. They now fall back to the run read's own `input_fingerprint` when this session has read none; one read in this session still comes first, so a start after a preview asserts the input that preview showed and a re-pin by someone else since is refused rather than silently sent. With neither, the refusal says the input is not pinned. The demo run fixtures, which pinned a subject but served no fingerprint (a state the host never serves), now carry one. The cursor frame's `retry:` needs no client code: `EventSource` applies it itself. Evidence: `run.test.tsx` ("start and retry send the run read's own fingerprint after a reload (N48)").
- F315 — N27's frontend half (backend F208): hidden PDF text was marked for the model but not for the approver. The source drawer outlines each line the rendered page does not show (a dashed `--paper-hidden` outline on a wash, 5.7:1 on the paper), names why in its title and to a screen reader, and says under the page how many lines are hidden and why. A reason the host adds later reads as its own words, so a new one is shown, never dropped. Evidence: `evidence-page.test.tsx` ("a line the rendered page does not show is marked…").
- F316 — N59's frontend half (backend F258): a narrative figure in Report and Committee was bracketed text. It is now its quote and a chip that opens the source drawer at its page (`evidence/Narrative.tsx`, one renderer for both). The figure carries no rectangle and no file name, so the drawer claims neither: it says nothing is highlighted and names the document by its digest. Evidence: `evidence-page.test.tsx` ("a committee figure's chip opens its source page, claiming no rectangle"), `committee.test.tsx`, `report.test.tsx`, and in three browsers `tests/workbench/navigation.spec.ts`, which opens the chip on the demo's page for the figure (`fixtures/pages/v1/…a1.7.json`, one line hidden) and finds that line marked.

### Design items, 2026-09-24 (owner: resolve every remaining local item, relayed by the audits session)

- F317 — N65: every section view shipped in the entry bundle, so a first load fetched all nine. Each view is now its own lazily loaded module (`app/views.tsx`), and the workspace asks for it when it asks for the document (`preloadView`), so the two load together rather than one after the other. While a view is still loading, the region shows the ordinary loading surface, which the a11y matrix already waits out; a view that fails to load meets the section boundary. First load is the shell plus one section: 190 kB gzip plus the section's 0.1–9 kB, from 230 kB. The reachability gate (`scripts/check-tested.mjs`) now follows a literal `import("…")` as the edge it is, and still reaches nothing through a computed one. Checked under the production CSP with Trusted Types in a browser: the section's module and the next section's on navigation load with no violation. Evidence: `tests/unit/workspace-lazy.test.tsx` (loading until the view lands, a failed load meets the boundary, the view requested on mount), `reachability.test.ts` ("a literal dynamic import reaches…"). Not done: splitting the libraries into their own cached bundles (N91).
- F318 — N64: the evidence drawer, the source drawer and the metric passport each ran their own modal hook (`ds/use-modal-a11y.ts`: a hand-rolled focus trap, Escape stack and scroll lock). They are now one `evidence/Overlay.tsx` on Base UI's Dialog, which owns the trap, Escape and outside press for the topmost of nested overlays, the scroll lock and the inert page; the hook is deleted. What it keeps from IA_SPEC.md 7: focus returns to the opener the caller passes (`finalFocus`), never to wherever focus sat before the click, and not at all to an opener that has left the page, where SourceDrawer's heading fallback still places it (FE-4). The overlay closes itself and tells its owner only when the close has finished (`onOpenChangeComplete`): unmounted mid-close, Base UI returned focus to the previous element, the defect `test_dialog_opener_is_explicit` caught. That also gives the three an exit transition they never had (reduced motion keeps the fade). `aria-modal` is stated on the popup, since Base UI makes the page inert without saying so. Checked in a browser with real keys: a drawer opened from the passport closes first, focus returns to the chip inside the passport and then to the Book cell, Tab stays inside, and the lock is released. Evidence: `hooks.test.tsx` (Escape and the close button close it, nested Escape closes the topmost only, focus returns to each opener, `test_focus_is_not_handed_to_an_opener_that_has_left_the_page`), `evidence.test.tsx::test_dialog_opener_is_explicit`, `evidence-page.test.tsx`.
- F319 — The metric passport's value column was `1fr`, at least its longest word, so a 64-digit snapshot digest pushed it past the dialog's edge (659 px of content in 576 px) and cut every field's right side. The column is `minmax(0, 1fr)` and a code value breaks anywhere. Found while checking F318 in a browser; measured with no horizontal scroll after the fix.
- F320 — N90: the narrative editor held raw `{{figure:CP-0#1}}` tokens, readable only through the preview under it. A figure is now a footnote marker, `[1]`, in the same native text area (undo, paste and a screen reader keep working), and the draft lists each marker it uses with the citation it names (`DraftFigures`). The number is the figure's place in the draft's own list, not in the picker's, so a refetch that reorders the picker cannot re-point a marker; a citation inserted twice keeps its number (`withFigure`); a bracketed number the draft does not list stays prose, which the server refuses as a digit, as before. The Report fixtures' CP-1 record now carries the citation the Committee fixture's figure already named (page 7, "Coverage 2.1x"), so the demo offers the picker. Checked in a browser with real typing: the marker lands at the caret, focus returns to the draft, and the legend names the citation. Evidence: `report-figures.test.tsx` (`test_a_figure_marker_composes_the_span_the_save_command_validates`, "withFigure: …", the picker-to-save test reading `[1]`).
- F321 — N56: CP-1B, CP-2G and CP-2B's tagged tables drew no figure, and the demo route carried none of the three. `sections/analysis/figures.tsx` now draws each from the bundle's exact columns (`REF_CP-1B_STEPS.md`, `REF_CP-2G_STEPS.md`, `REF_CP-2B_STEPS.md`): `comparatorChanges`, each metric's change on its reference period as a diverging bar in percent (the one unit a revenue and a debt change share; the bundle writes `current / prior - 1`, read as a percent on its digits by `hundredfold`, now shared with the Book in `ds/format.ts`), an uncalculable change a gap carrying its status, one basis named in the title and several on each bar; `addbackValidation`, CP-1B's difference from CP-1 per add-back for the latest period, with what the module ruled counted in the summary; `forecastDrivers`, one line figure a division, base against downside by fiscal year, a slot unused throughout drawing nothing; and CP-2B's catalysts as a ranked, dated list with each source, not a chart, an event not being a magnitude. The demo Analysis fixture gains the three handoffs (additions only; existing content byte-for-byte unchanged, checked by parse), their add-back ids and periods taken from CP-1's own tables, and the a11y matrix now opens CP-1, CP-1B, CP-2B and CP-2G's tabs (720 scans), so a module's figures are measured at last. Evidence: `figures.test.tsx` (the four), `helpers.test.ts` (`hundredfold`).
- F322 — Every horizontal chart cut its widest category label by its last letter ("Restructuri…"): the label column is sized to that label's own estimated width, and `fitText` floored 13 × 6.82 ÷ 6.82 as 12.999…. It now allows for the rounding. Found checking F321 in a browser. Evidence: `chart-maths.test.ts`, which fails without it.
- F323 — At 320 px (WCAG 1.4.10) a module's figure grid pushed the page 16 px sideways in every engine and theme: `minmax(320px, 1fr)` is a track that cannot shrink below the screen. `minmax(min(320px, 100%), 1fr)`. CP-1's charts had it too; the matrix had never opened a module with figures until F321's routes.
- F324 — N62: a filed deliverable read like any other page. Committee now sets a filed revision as paper (`Paper`, `.paper`), ink on cream in either theme and in filed output only: a masthead, the case title with its revision and run, a "Filed" stamp in the paper's green (a ruled box turned a few degrees, never animated), the narrative with its figure chips in the paper's blue, who signed, froze and filed, and a filed line with the payload and filed-event digests, each short on the page and whole in its title. The paper replaces the case and narrative panels for a filed revision only; the links to the rendered paper and the package sit under it, and the full receipt and the artifacts stay below as the record. A frozen revision keeps its panels. On a phone the stamp joins the flow (no overflow at 320 px). Every colour is a paper token already measured (cite, stamp, meta and note on the cream). Evidence: `committee.test.tsx` ("a filed revision is paper…"); the a11y matrix scans the filed Committee route in both themes.

### Adversarial review of F310–F324, 2026-09-24 (owner: "run adversarial review on this work"; three independent reviewers — saboteur, new hire, security auditor — each finding confirmed by probe or test before it was patched)

- F325 — CRITICAL, two reviewers. N56's percent figures scaled every rate by 100, but the model may write `8%`, which the host's reader keeps as points (`caos/methodology/tables.py` strips the sign), so the comparator and division growth drew "+800%" and a real `0.5%` a plausible 50%. `percentDatum` now moves the point only for a bare fraction, and the division summary reads through it. F321's "the bundle writes `current / prior - 1`" was a helper's convention, not the table's. Evidence: `figures.test.tsx` ("a rate written with a percent sign is not scaled again"), which fails on the old code.
- F326 — CRITICAL, all three reviewers. F320's `[n]` markers meant whatever the draft's own list said, so a pasted footnote `[1]`, or `[01]`, became a citation the author never placed and slipped past the server's digit check. A marker now names its citation in the text, `[CP-0 #2]` (route node and the citation counted from one): it survives a paste, cannot be re-pointed, and a bare `[1]` stays prose for the server to refuse. The draft-local list is gone. A save clears the draft only if it is still what was sent, so words typed during the save survive, and inserting a marker over itself no longer leaves the caret to jump on the next key. Evidence: `report-figures.test.tsx`.
- F327 — WARNING, security. The Analysis figures did a `find` or `filter` per mark over model-authored tables of up to 2,000 rows: measured 5.5 s at 1,000 add-backs, 266 s and 4 million marks at 2,000 facilities, repeated on every mark pressed. Rows are now indexed once (`groupBy`), a figure past 2,000 marks is stated not drawn, a module past 24 figures says how many more there are, and the figure list is memoised per document. Evidence: `figures.test.tsx` ("a table built to be expensive is stated, not drawn") — 2,000 facilities in well under a second.
- F328 — CRITICAL, two reviewers, and W7 of the API review. A lazy section view that failed to load once stayed broken until a full reload (React keeps a failed lazy's error), under a message that blamed the document. `retryingView` keeps one component per section and swaps in a fresh lazy view only when the section boundary asks (`ViewNotLoaded.retry`), never on React's own re-render after the failure, which would ask again in a loop for a file that is gone. The boundary tells a failed load (`VIEW_NOT_LOADED`, "Try again") from a render failure, and a second failure in a row offers "Reload the page", since a file missing after a deploy means a stale page. The loading fallback now sits inside the section's tabpanel, so no tab names a panel that is not there. Evidence: `workspace-lazy.test.tsx` (the real `retryingView`: one failure then success, and a file that stays gone asking twice then offering the reload), `workspace-boundary.test.tsx`.
- F329 — CRITICAL, two reviewers. The add-back validation figure took the register's last-listed period as the latest, so a BLOCK in the newer quarter could hide behind an older one listed after it. It now draws every period the register validated, each bar named with its period, and counts across all of them. The comparator names a period where one metric is compared twice on one basis; a case the bundle does not name sorts after base and downside, not before; a catalyst ranked 0 is a rank. Evidence: `figures.test.tsx`, each failing on the old code.
- F330 — WARNING. A parked run read "Running" on its own Run section, where the Directory sends the reader to retry it: the header, verdict, next step and status now say "Parked" with the stop code. An unpinned input's Start and Retry were refused `COMMAND_EXPECTATION_STALE`, whose remedy is a re-read; it is the host's `RUN_INPUT_NOT_PINNED`. The gate panels are keyed on both the run read's fingerprint and the session's, so a re-pin elsewhere clears a stale preview too, and the docstrings that said the run read never serves a fingerprint are corrected. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx`, `run.test.tsx`.
- F331 — WARNING, all three reviewers. F315 said a hidden-text reason the host adds later "is shown, never dropped"; the wire's list is closed, so such a page is refused whole. The wording map is now typed on that list, so the compiler asks for a new reason's words when the wire names it, and the claim is corrected.
- F332 — WARNING, all three reviewers (withdrawal), security (digest). The source drawer showed a page of whatever document answered for the case, run, source and page; it now also requires the page's document digest to be the citation's, else `WIRE_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` and no text. A saved narrative figure carries no withdrawal of its own, so its unavailable page says the source may have been withdrawn since the revision was saved (the wire change is N93). Each opening of a drawer or the passport is keyed, and a close clears only its own opening, so one opened while the last is still leaving is not closed with it. Evidence: `evidence-page.test.tsx`.
- F333 — NOTE, security. Committee linked whatever `render_url` and `package_url` said; it now links only this revision's own reads at the exact paths the host builds, and refuses any other (`LINK_NOT_OWN`) — another origin, another case or revision, a script. The render link reads "Open the rendered page", so "paper" means only the filed sheet, and the narrative marker sits on the paper's narrative, not the whole sheet. Evidence: `committee.test.tsx`.

### Model Markdown formatted, one module layout, shadcn charts, 2026-09-24 (owner: "raw markdowns are visually unappealing"; the module-output map and two samples approved: "Approved proceed with building. No agents.")

- D60 (2026-09-24) — The browser draws the model's Markdown -- an Analysis handoff's `model_analysis`, a Report or Committee artifact's saved Markdown -- as the host renderer's closed element set (`caos/deliverable/render.py`, `ELEMENTS`), built as React elements. `frontend/src/ds/markdown.ts` reads `render.py`'s grammar rule for rule (heading, list with its written ordinals and four levels, table that refuses a row that misfits its header, fence with both labels, comment kept as written, `_inline`'s flanking and literal code spans) and returns data; `ds/ModelMarkdown.tsx` draws it; nothing writes `innerHTML`, so `trusted-types 'none'` holds whatever the model wrote. A text the host would refuse, or one past 500,000 characters, is shown as written with the reason. D32's "the frontend never parses Markdown" stands for data: no figure is read from the Markdown -- charts and key figures come only from the host's typed tables; a figure column's alignment and a status word's badge are display. The Analysis module is drawn in five places every module shares, because every module writes one skeleton (`vendor/deploy-v/CANON_SHARED.md`): the header's host facts; the model's opening view beside its key figures and caveats; the figures; the reader-facing sections; one card of tabs, Appendix · Audit · As written. A register is placed by the shape its columns give it (`sections/analysis/parts.ts`, from the 22 output profiles' 306 registers): evidence chains read as items, dated registers down time, audit-shaped ones (sources, gaps, conflicts, QA, readiness) in the Audit tab in one order in every module; CP-0's and CP-5's conclusions, audit-shaped, lead. What the run rests on and the module's host-verified citations were a rail beside the module; they are audit material (owner: "uses too much space ... should not be using as much space"), so a count in the module's header opens them in the evidence drawer and the Audit tab lists them first. A Report or Committee artifact reads formatted, its exact signed text a tab away. Alternatives: react-markdown or remark (a dependency, and a general grammar that would draw what the deliverable refuses); the host's rendered HTML injected (Trusted Types refuse it); the raw text (the owner's complaint). Evidence: `tests/unit/markdown.test.tsx` (the grammar, hostile markup as text, the split and the shapes), `analysis.test.tsx`, `report.test.tsx`, `committee.test.tsx`.
- D61 (2026-09-24) — Charts are drawn by Recharts, exact-pinned at 3.10.1, which replaces D33's `d3-scale` and `d3-shape` (removed with their `@types`): shadcn/ui's chart primitive (owner: "Replace D3 with shadcn and shadcnblocks charts"). shadcn's own `chart.tsx` is not vendored: its `ChartStyle` sets colours through `<style dangerouslySetInnerHTML>`, which `style-src 'self'` and `trusted-types 'none'` refuse; Recharts itself writes no `innerHTML` and measures text through the CSSOM. Recharts draws the axes, the grid and the marks -- `<Bar shape>` and `<Line shape>` in this app's provenance shapes, solid for the host and outlined and hatched for the model -- and what it has no word for (an unavailable value's "n/a", a label left out rather than clipped, a stack's total, a bridge's joins) is drawn by the chart module from the same geometry. Each mark reports its box to the frame, which keeps a native button over it (Tab reaches one, the arrow keys the rest, at least 24px), the readout and the table twin. The ticks stay this app's: d3-array's nice-number algorithm, ported (ISC), gives Recharts an explicit domain and ticks, so an axis reads as it did. The frame is laid out as shadcn's chart card: title and summary, the chart, then its legend and the Table toggle. Cost: the lazily loaded chart file grows from 37 kB to 333 kB (101 kB gzip). Alternatives: keep d3 (the owner's request); vendor `chart.tsx` and patch `ChartStyle` (a fork of a file whose one job is the refused style element); visx or ECharts (a second dialect beside shadcn's). Evidence: `tests/unit/charts.test.tsx`, `chart-maths.test.ts` (unchanged guarantees: exact labels, residuals, shares summing to 100.0, keyboard marks, 24px targets, the table twin, no axe violation).

- D62 (2026-09-24) — The Analysis lead reads a module's opening in the canon's own order (CANON_SHARED, reading order: a conclusion-first view, then up to three decision drivers), from real Deploy V outputs (an older Copilot sample run, kept local) and the owner's approval of a mock: the view's first sentence is the page's first sentence, set large, with the rest of its paragraph beneath; drivers written as a bold-led list are key points, each claim a line and its support shown three lines at a time; a section headed as the module's argument against itself (the canon requires one and names no heading) sits beside the view, above key figures and caveats; and a bracketed module reference (`[CP-1B B2/B5]`) is a link to that module's tab and, for a register, opens it in the appendix. All of it is the model's text split or placed, never rewritten, and no figure is read from it (D32); a paragraph with no sentence of readable length, a list not every item of which opens in bold, and a bracket that is not wholly references (a file name, "external: …") stay as written. References are links in Analysis only: filed output keeps the host renderer's element set. Not taken: key points from the Audit Summary's bullets (audit material stays in the Audit tab); the mock's leverage-by-basis chart, which needs the register served typed (N94). Evidence: `markdown.test.tsx`, `analysis.test.tsx`.

### Review of D60 and D61 before landing, 2026-09-24 (inline, three personas -- saboteur, new hire, security auditor; no agents, at the owner's word; each finding reproduced before it was fixed)

- F370 — CRITICAL. Every mark's report re-rendered the chart frame, the frame handed Recharts a new chart, and Recharts laid it out again, which reported again: "Maximum update depth exceeded" on the first render of any chart. A mark now reports only when its box, name or readout moves (`Reported`, `useEffectEvent` over its values), and the frame draws each chart once per width (`useMemo`), so its own state reaches the chart through context. Evidence: `charts.test.tsx`, every chart.
- F371 — WARNING. A register whose shape is `table` carried it as a class and met Tailwind's `.table` utility (`display: table`), so on a 320px phone CP-2G's forecast drivers ran 1,617px wide past its card (the a11y matrix's layout check). The shape is a `data-shape` attribute, never a class, and a case-by-period table reads as Financials. Evidence: `npm run a11y`, 720 scans, 0 layout failures.
- F372 — WARNING. The tabs card had no h3, so its groups stepped from the module's h2 to h4, and a lead register's title from h3 to h5 (axe `heading-order`). Groups are h3, and a register's title is h4 wherever it sits.
- F373 — WARNING. Caveats counted the appendix's gap and conflict registers only; a table under the module's own `## Gaps & Conflicts` went uncounted, so the caveat could say nothing where the module wrote gaps. Both are counted.
- F374 — WARNING. A text with an H2 that is not one of the six (the demo's `## Normalised financials`) lost its opening: none was read as the analysis. Without an `## Analysis`, every section that is not an audit one is read as the analysis, its heading kept. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx`, `markdown.test.tsx`.
- F375 — NOTE. Recharts drew a gridline at the plot's top edge, off the ticks, and wrapped each tick label in a `tspan`; the grid is drawn at this module's tick positions and each tick is one `text`. Parity with `render.py` was checked on a ten-sample corpus (lists, literal ordinals, fences, comments, tables, unpaired delimiters, a refusal): element counts equal, refusal included. Security: Recharts' files and the built chart chunk carry no `eval`, `new Function`, `innerHTML` or style-attribute write.

### Critique of the Analysis section, 2026-09-24 (/impeccable critique, 25/40; all five issues, at the owner's word; inline, no agents)

- F376 — WARNING. The Appendix tab, open by default, drew every register whole: CP-1's card ran 4,749px and the page 6,843px, and a canonical module carries 15 to 31 registers. It is an index: a register a line (id, name, row count), opened in place one at a time, its first eight rows and then "Show all N rows". CP-1's page is 2,239px. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx`.
- F377 — WARNING. The shape rules read columns, so `cp1.adjusted_ebitda_bridge` went to Definitions (its `source_definition` column) and `cp1.operating_kpi_schedule` to Other, breaking "the same place in every module". Every table the bundle tags is placed by its id, under its schema reference's name (`STABLE_TABLES`, 22 ids); `markdown.test.tsx` walks `vendor/deploy-v` and fails on a tagged id with no entry, or one that would land unsorted. The last group is called Unsorted.
- F378 — WARNING. The module began 518px down a 900px screen: the summary with its brief, then thirteen module tabs in three rows. With a view open the summary is one line, its brief on request; past eight views the tabs are one row of codes that stays under the header, each name on hover and to a screen reader. The module now begins at about 266px. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx`.
- F379 — NOTE. Column heads were the model's identifiers (`period_id`, `YOY_SAME_QUARTER`); an identifier head reads in words, the identifier on hover and exact in As written (`plainHead`, sharing `plainName` with the figures' labels). The documents count in the header now looks like the button it is.
- F380 — NOTE. The lead's prose measured about 94 characters a line and notes about 161. Prose is held at 60ch (Geist's "0" is wide: 64 characters measured) and the section's notes at 50ch (about 72).
- F381 — NOTE. What repeated: a provenance key under each of six charts (twelve lines), the host's calculation statement after 4,700px of tables, CP-1B's change chart beside key figures printing the same six changes, a caption narrating the design ("the same three tabs in every module"), and a lone "QA passed" caveat beside the header's Passed tag. The key and the calculation statement are said once, in the Figures header (`ProvenanceKeyed`); the change chart is drawn only past six comparisons; the caption is gone; a passing QA is not a caveat, and a view with nothing beside it takes the width. The charts' index comment no longer names d3.
- F382 — NOTE. Found in passing: the maturity ladder's legend read "Not stated not_stated" -- `replace` took the first underscore only. Every underscore goes, and a status stated twice is said once. Evidence: `figures.test.tsx`.
- F383 — NOTE. A module with no appendix register opened on an empty tab. It opens on Audit; asked for, the Appendix says it is empty. The tab is never `disabled`: a refused control stays reachable (the lint rule, IA_SPEC.md 2).
- F384 — NOTE. Self-review of F376–F383 before landing: an opened register's panel id was built from its title, and a title with a space split `aria-controls` (a list of ids) in two; it is built from the register's position. A module whose tables draw nothing (a comparator the key figures already print) kept an empty Figures heading; it shows only the host's calculation statement. Evidence: `figures.test.tsx`.
- F385 — WARNING. Found by rendering real Deploy V outputs (an older Copilot sample run, transcribed from the owner's screenshots into a local fixture that is never committed): deployed modules do not write the canon's headings. They head the audit summary "Module Result" and QA "QA & Governance", put the appendix under its own H2, label registers with a bold line (`**T1 — …**`) or a bare id line above the table, and use one-letter ids (`A1`, `B8`, `R10`). Every register therefore read as an untitled "Table" under Unsorted, and the audit sections as reader text. The parser now reads each of these the way it reads the canon's; periods across the columns read as Financials, and a register titled a walk, bridge, delta, debt, tranche or liquidity schedule reads by its title. A document's H1 title is left to As written. Evidence: `markdown.test.tsx`.
- F386 — NOTE. Same rendering: a caveat carrying long limitation identifiers ran past its card, eight flags filled four lines of the module header, and an item's title ran into its state word ("…$3.15bn)High" — adjacent text nodes are one flex item). The header says how many limitations; each is its own caveat, in words, its identifier on hover; caveat text wraps anywhere; an item's title and state words are separate items.
- F387 — WARNING. Self-review of D62 before landing: a hand-typed address with a broken escape (`#register-%E0%A4%A`) made `decodeURIComponent` throw inside the render, taking the section down; the register is read by `registerOfHash`, which answers no register instead. And the contrary-view rule matched "bull case" and "bear case", which would have lifted CP-6's debate -- its analysis, not an argument against it -- into the side card; the rule is contrary, counter-argument and counter-case only. Evidence: `markdown.test.tsx`.
- F388 — WARNING. A second Deploy V sample pack (a newer run, kept local) read against the module layout: ten of its fourteen modules wrote the appendix heading under `## Evidence Trace` or `## Source Registry`, so the parser saw no registers at all; an appendix heading inside any section now sends the rest of that section to the appendix, and a named section written before `## Analysis` (CP-3's Recommendation) leads. Three smaller misreadings from the same pack: a metric compared over two periods printed two identical key-figure labels, now told apart by the period, and a change the host's reader could not type ("(5.1%) reported") printed n/a beside it, now the served text as written; a sustainability-linked ratchet table filed under Market by its "Expected Spread Effect" column, now a trigger; and validation warnings printed as raw identifiers, now in words where they are identifiers and as written where they are prose. Evidence: `markdown.test.tsx`, `analysis.test.tsx`.
- F389 — The base's `frontend` job was red from 61d820b on: `ds/ModelMarkdown.tsx` imported React's `Fragment` by name, which the TypeScript vocabulary gate reads as the CONTEXT.md synonym of block. The name is React's and cannot change, so it is used where it is needed (`React.Fragment`) and never declared as an import specifier, which is what the gate examines; no alias, no exemption. Evidence: `npm --prefix frontend run lint` (vocabulary: 0 findings).
- F390 — Brief 6.1 (docs/rebuild/2026-09-25-design-brief.md): an id or a digest read three ways -- elided in Directory, whole at 64 characters in Model, Report and Committee, and wrapped over two lines in the Run card's caption. `ds/Digest.tsx` is the one treatment: `shortDigest`'s 8…4 on the page, the whole value in its title and to a screen reader, and, where the browser has the async clipboard, the chip itself is the control that copies the whole value and says "Copied" beside itself for 1.2 s, or "Not copied" when the browser refuses the write (no toast, D35). A 24px icon beside the value was tried first and the a11y matrix refused it twice: at 1024px, scrolled under the sticky header, 4px of the header's theme button left it 20px wide (WCAG 2.5.8); at 320px, Committee's receipt column could not hold `sha256:` and the value on one line and the control ran past the viewport. The chip keeps its size wherever it scrolls, and it wraps after `sha256:`; the document also stops focus scrolling 3.5rem down (`scroll-padding-top`, the header's height, WCAG 2.4.11). The paper quotes its digests with `copy={false}` in its own type size, filed output offering no control; Committee's own `Short` is folded into it. Applied to the forecast, the Report and Committee artifact cards, Report's saved-report card, Committee's filing and receipt ids, and the Run card; a `kv` row now sits on its value's baseline, so a label beside a chip does not ride above it. Committee stays read only: the workbench's check that its only buttons are figure chips and view tabs names the copy chip (`data-digest-copy`) too, which changes nothing. Evidence: `digest.test.tsx`, `model.test.tsx`, `report.test.tsx`, `committee.test.tsx`, `tests/workbench/navigation.spec.ts`, the a11y matrix.
- F391 — Brief 6.3: Run's Work card drew Start, Retry and Cancel refused for one reason and printed that reason three times. `drawnRefusal` names what a control resolves to (its own refusal, else `ACTION_UNPLACED`, else live), `sharedRefusal` names the one a group shares (two or more, all refused, the same words), and `SharedRefusal` says it once while each control keeps it as its accessible description (`reasonDisplay="hidden"`). The Upload pack's existing "said once above the pack" rule now uses the same component. Evidence: `helpers.test.ts`, `run.test.tsx` (`test_work_controls_refused_for_one_reason_say_it_once`).
- F392 — Brief 5 and 6.7-6.8, copy: Upload's verdict read "1 withdrawn source stay cited where they were used" and now agrees in number; Set versions read a bare number and "1 sources", now "Version n" with singular counts; the Runs card's caption was a note to the builder ("displayed and latest are named separately") and now counts the runs; Analysis's "Route complete" card carried a badge reading 0 and no longer draws one; and the route canvas headed CP-CF's column "Stage 100", the host's `MODEL_STAGE` (`caos/graph/route.py`), which now reads "Extension" (`stageLabel`). Evidence: `compose.test.tsx`, `route-graph.test.tsx`.
- F393 — Brief 6.4, the chart card's rhythm: a row of Analysis figures was one height, but each card's Table toggle sat wherever its legend ended and its plot started wherever its summary did, so the row read as six loose cards. Each card is now a column whose readout, legend and Table toggle sit at its foot, and whose summary takes two lines (clamped, whole in its `title`), so the plots start level. A horizontal chart's category name was cut to one line ("Share-based compe…"); `fitLines` breaks it between words onto up to three lines, the row grows to hold them, and only what three lines cannot hold is cut. The host's calculation note sat beside the figures' key because `.analysis .note`'s 50ch cap let it fit there; it is the key's caption on its own line. Evidence: `chart-maths.test.ts`, `charts.test.tsx` ("wraps a long category name"), the a11y matrix.
- F394 — Brief 6.5 in part: a register wider than its card scrolled its first column away, so a row lost its name. The case register, the source pack and the Book (`pin-first`) hold that column at the left from 48rem up, on the card's own ground and re-tinted when the row is hovered or selected; below 48rem it scrolls with the rest, where a held column would leave too little room. The Book printed each credit's snapshot whole (64 characters, 490px of held column); it is a `Digest` (F390). Evidence: the a11y matrix and workbench at 1024 and 320.
- F395 — Brief 6.6, the route canvas: edges were straight segments, so an edge spanning a column (CP-4 to CP-7) ran diagonally through the stage between; `routeOf` draws each one square, out of its source's right side, down the gutter after it, along the gap above its target's row, and into its target from the gutter before it, so no edge crosses a node. The rail named CP-0 while the canvas opened scrolled to CP-6 wearing an amber ring for its gate: Run's selection now opens on `focusOf`'s node (where the work is), the node the canvas scrolls to, in the one ring a node wears, blue; a gate's amber is on its edge and its diamond only, the diamond in the gutter before the node it holds, its name said rather than printed across both nodes. An unpinned route drew a canvas of dot grid and a full key for edges that were not there; it is the empty pattern (mark, title, sentence). The key is one line: each edge type as its code, its meaning in its title, and the soft edges' shared rule said once. Evidence: `route-graph.test.tsx`, `run.test.tsx` (`test_the_rail_and_the_canvas_open_on_where_the_work_is`, `test_route_not_pinned_is_rendered_as_a_preview`), `tests/workbench/run.spec.ts`, the a11y matrix.
- F396 — Brief 6.2, a section's state said once: a partial document said "Partial" four times -- the header chip, the verdict, the Evidence cell's notes, and a dashed region alert repeating those notes -- and Upload's withdrawn count twice. The region alert is gone: the summary, drawn over every document, carries the verdict and the notes. The header's warning chips (the only two kinds, "Partial" and "n withdrawn", both said by the verdict) give way while the summary is on screen and carry the warning once it scrolls away: the summary marks the page's root (`data-summary-in-view`) through an IntersectionObserver below the 3.5rem header, and an unlayered rule hides `.header-chip` from 48rem up -- inside the components layer the badge's own display utility won. A phone always shows them, and without an observer the root is never marked and they stay. The run's state badge stays: the summary says the section's conclusion, not the run's state. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx` ("the summary marks the page while it is in view"), `tests/workbench/states.spec.ts`, the a11y matrix.
- F397 — Brief 5, Book and Directory: a Book credit with no period here printed "Not served" in every cell of its row; it is said once beside the credit's name, each empty cell a quiet dash that still says "Not served" to a screen reader, and a single missing cell in a served row keeps its own chip. Directory's latest run stacked its state and two mono lines into a 78px row; they share one line where the column allows. Case access read "your standing: approver" beside a register that says "Approver"; it reads "Your standing · Approver". Evidence: `book.test.tsx`, `directory.test.tsx`.
- F398 — Brief 6.11, filing as a sequence: Report's save, sign, freeze and file were four equal buttons in a row, Save always the primary. They are an ordered list of four steps (`filingSteps`), each under a rule in the success hue once done and ink while current, with its number or a check and the word "Done"; done is only what the document says -- saved once a revision exists, signed and frozen once it is frozen (the Report carries no signatures, and a frozen revision was signed), filed once filed -- and the current step is the first not done that the document offers, its control the section's one primary. Every control stays on the page with its refusal, and the confirm step, the idempotency key and the re-read are unchanged. Evidence: `report.test.tsx` ("filing reads as its sequence, with one current step").
- F399 — Brief 6.10: Upload's file input was the one native control on the page ("Choose Files / No file chosen", the browser's words and casing). It is still an `<input type="file">`, focused and labelled "Documents to admit", visually hidden; a "Choose documents" label drawn as the outline button opens it and wears its focus ring, and the names chosen are chips beside it, or "No documents chosen". Drawing it found that `buttonVariants` used alone on a label or link left the base `border-transparent` over the outline's border, so Committee's two links had no edge either: both are merged with `cn` as the Button merges them. Evidence: `upload.test.tsx`, the a11y matrix.
- F400 — Brief 6.9, one label style per card: Model's forecast card, and Report's and Committee's artifact cards, read their limitations and validation warnings as bold-led sentences under the card (`NoteList`, "Limitations." then the flags), a second label style beside the definition list above them. They are rows of that list (`NoteRows`): a label, its values joined, "none" when there are none, in the card's one label style. Drawing them at 320px found two things a loose note never met: a value broke only between words, so the hostile fixture's long warning token set the value column's width and pushed the digest chips past the card, and a label never wrapped, so "Validation warnings" held its full width beside a chip that needed the room. A note's value breaks inside a token where one cannot fit (`wrap`), and a label breaks between its words only when its value needs the room; every list that fitted before lays out as it did. Evidence: `model.test.tsx`, `report.test.tsx` (each note a `dd` after its `dt`, inside the artifact's `dl.kv`), the a11y matrix at 320px.
- F401 — Brief 6.1, the ids left over: Upload's pack and its set versions printed twelve characters of a digest in a bare `code`, the evidence and source drawers "sha256" and twelve characters with an ellipsis, and Committee's saved (unfiled) card the whole case, run and revision ids and payload digest in running text -- three spellings beside the one `Digest` everywhere else. All are `Digest`: eight and four on the page, whole in its title and to a screen reader, one press from the clipboard where the browser offers it. Evidence: `upload.test.tsx` (the pack row's digest and the set's fingerprint), `committee.test.tsx`, the a11y matrix.
- F402 — Brief 5, Admin and the absent route: an unavailable region drew its mark, the word "Unavailable", and the title "Unavailable or not permitted." and stopped -- title and state saying the same thing, and nothing about what to do. The title stays the one neutral wording a private 404 shares with an absent route; beneath it one sentence says what the reader can do without saying which of the two it was ("Either nothing is here or you hold no standing on it; a case's owner grants standing.", `UNAVAILABLE_NEXT`), and the one action is the way out, "All cases" -- except on the Directory, which it would lead back to. Evidence: `states.test.tsx` ("an unavailable region says what to do and offers the way out").
- F403 — Brief 5, the chrome: the case switcher cut the issuer's title at the sidebar's width ("Carvana Co. (NYSE: …") over "Case 00000000". It reads the issuer's name, and beneath it the listing the title carries ("NYSE: CVNA") or else the case's short id (`switcherLines`); a parenthesis that is not a listing stays in the name, and the whole title is in the trail and the name's title. Evidence: `chrome.test.tsx` ("the case switcher names the issuer and its listing, never a cut title").
- F404 — Brief 5, the chrome: Ask and Sign out sat in the sidebar's foot of every page as dashed refused buttons whose reason only a pointer's title or a press reached. Refused controls take a third reason display, `tooltip`: the reason and its detail (the code and what clears it) open on hover and on keyboard focus, the sr-only description is unchanged, a press still flashes it for touch, and no title draws a second tooltip over the first. The foot's two use it, and lose the dashed edge (still muted, still `aria-disabled`, still there). The chrome's no-build-phase check reads a control's title, or its description where a tooltip carries the reason. Evidence: `chrome.test.tsx` ("the foot's refused controls are quiet and say why on focus"; "every refused control in the chrome names what clears it today, never a build phase").
- F405 — Brief 5 (Report) and 6.10, the narrative editor: Report's draft was a textarea, two sentences of rules under it ("Type prose only. Every figure goes in through the citation picker…"), then the citation select and Insert figure as a row of their own. The draft and its picker are one control (`.composer`): one edge, the draft's focus ring on the whole, the picker its foot under a hairline. The rule is a one-line caption under the label, still the draft's description ("Prose only: every figure goes in through the citation picker, as a marker."), and the refusal at save carries the rest: `NARRATIVE_FIGURE_UNREFERENCED` reads "The prose carries a figure. Type prose only; each figure goes in through the citation picker." Evidence: `report-figures.test.tsx` (the picker inside the draft's control, the description), `helpers.test.ts`.
- F406 — Brief 6.10, the Subject form: Run's four subject fields sat in an auto-fill grid that, at 1440, held three columns and put Analysis date alone on a row, and a label sat 6px over its field, off the 4px rhythm. A panel of exactly four fields lays them out as two pairs at any width that holds two (one column below that), and a label sits 8px over its field everywhere; a field's caption sits 4px under its label and 8px over the field. Evidence: `run.spec.ts` ("the subject's four fields are two pairs, never three and a stray", at 1440), the a11y matrix.
- F407 — Brief 6.13, the summary at 390px: the headline figure was a right-aligned block, so once it wrapped under the verdict on a phone "6/10" hung right-aligned over "modules complete", mid-card, between the verdict and the brief's cells; and a cell broke its observed date after a hyphen ("2026-09- / 09 14:33Z"). Wrapped under the verdict, the headline aligns with the verdict's words, and closes the row right-aligned from 40rem up as before; a brief cell keeps each date and stamp whole (`keepStamps`) and its other words wrap as they did. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx` ("a brief cell keeps each date and stamp whole…"), Run, Analysis and Directory drawn at 390 in dark.
- F408 — Brief 6.12, dark parity: a section's active view pill was a muted fill with the line variant's transparent border, which all but vanished on the dark page; it has a hairline edge in both themes. Committee's artifact card printed its scope in mono, where a value that is words reads in the body face as Report's does. Run's tinted selected row and the paper on a dark body were drawn in dark and hold. Evidence: `chrome.test.tsx`, `committee.test.tsx`, the a11y matrix in both schemes.
- F409 — Brief 5, Book and Analysis, documentation among the figures: Book's Basis card closed on a bold-led paragraph about which runs can fill it, and each period's table opened on a centred caption, the one centred text in the product, repeating the card's title. The demonstration's note is the card's caption at its foot (`.card-note`: caption size, under a hairline, no bold lead), and the table's caption names it to a screen reader only while its header carries "Credits compared" as the card's caption. On a module with no figures to head, the host's calculation statement was a loose line between the lead and the depth card; it is drawn as a caveat on the module (`.calc-caveat`), and under the Figures heading as before where there are figures. The Audit tab's filenames were bold sans, heavier than the headings above them; a filename is an id, mono and regular. Evidence: `book.test.tsx`, `figures.test.tsx`.
- F410 — Brief 5 (Upload) and 6.3, the pack's reason said twice: Admit's refusal line sat under its button and "Withdraw: Not offered on this page yet." under that, the same words one over the other. Upload reads both commands' refusals once; refused for one reason, the pack says it once ("Admit and withdraw: …"), and each control keeps it as its description. Two different reasons are still each said. Evidence: `upload.test.tsx` ("the pack's two commands refused for one reason say it once"; "a refused withdrawal renders its code and clearance and is not hidden").
- F411 — Two leftovers of the `Digest` rollout (F390, F401) and the paper: in a table row a chip wrapped its copy control onto a second line and doubled the row's height, and in a left-aligned cell it hung 6px right of the words above it, its offset written for a right-aligned list. A row keeps its digest and control on one line (the table scrolls), and a left-aligned cell's chip hangs into the left gutter. The paper's Filed stamp crossed the mast's rule; it sits beside the title. Evidence: Book, Upload and Committee drawn at 1280, the a11y matrix.

It is said once as the field's caption, in caption size: "Available means the command would answer, not that it will succeed: this demonstration refuses every command, so a press shows RESPONSE_INVALID." Evidence: `directory.test.tsx` ("test_the_demonstration_directory_offers_one_available_command").
- F413 — Brief 5, Committee: the paper's left edge met the cards above it and its right edge stopped at 46rem, a ragged column. The paper and the two links that open and download it are one fixed page width, centred under the summary (`.paper-desk`); the cards below keep the body's width.
