### Desktop scope and the design brief's open questions, 2026-09-25 (owner: "remove mobile phone use, it is not required, 100% PC use only. review the methodology and apply your recommendations to the remaining questions")

- D63 (2026-09-25) — Desktop only (owner decision). CAOS is used at a desk: a PC, a pointer, a keyboard, a window 1024 px wide or wider at 100% zoom. No screen is designed, polished or checked for a phone or a tablet; the brief's 6.13 (phone at 390 px) and every 390 px item are withdrawn, and N104's banner-at-390 item with them. What stays is reflow: 320×640 in the a11y matrix is a 1280 px desktop at 400% zoom (WCAG 1.4.10, `fixture-routes.mjs`), a desktop reader's need rather than a phone's, and a gate that may not be loosened (CLAUDE.md). The narrow-width behaviour that serves it -- the sidebar as a sheet below 768 px, a section's views as a native select below 640 px -- is driven by the window's width, not the device, and is kept to what reflow needs; `DESIGN.md` says so, and the comments that called it a phone layout now call it a zoomed width.
- D64 (2026-09-25) — Brief question 1, a reading width at ≥1440: cards stay the body's full width -- tables, charts, the route canvas and the Book need it, and cards of different widths leave the ragged column F413 removed from the paper -- but the summary's verdict, headline and four cells stop at a reading measure, 78rem, instead of spreading across a wide desk screen (the cells at most 17.5rem each, so their right edge meets the verdict row's). The Analysis lead already stops at 65ch. With D63 a 1920 px screen is the ordinary case, which is what decides it. Evidence: `chrome.spec.ts` ("on a wide desk screen the summary stops at its reading measure", at 1920).
- D65 (2026-09-25) — Brief question 2, "Committee Ready": quoted as the bundle spells it. It is one of the bundle's enumerated committee statuses -- "D2 COMMITTEE: Committee Ready | Draft Only | Requires More Work | Insufficient Information | Restricted | Blocked" (`CANON_SHARED.md`), which the validator refuses anything outside (`validate_handoff.py`, "committee_status is not a canonical enum value") and the host passes through verbatim -- so it is a code quoted as a code, as DESIGN.md already treats refusal codes and edge types, and so are the QA statuses beside it ("Passed", "Restricted"). The workspace sentence-cased it in three places (the summary's Approval cell, Report's and Committee's artifact captions), so Analysis printed "Committee Ready" in the module header and "Committee ready" in the summary above it; every print site now quotes the served value, and the summary prints its Approval and Revision cells as each section composes them (the section's own words already in sentence case). A decision scope is the bundle's `FULL` or `SCREENING_ONLY`, read in words, "full scope" and "screening only" (`scopeOf`); "full committee" was the demonstration fixture's invented `FULL_COMMITTEE`. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx`, `chrome.test.tsx` ("Approval Committee Ready").
- D66 (2026-09-25) — Brief question 5, the demo banner: the thin form. A line in the header's own colour, the words in `--warning` (text-safe at 4.5:1) beside the warning mark, so it says what the build is by shape as well as hue; the full-width warning wash, a yellow-olive band over every page in dark, is gone. It is on a demonstration build only, and it stays on every page, above the header, as the `complementary` "Demonstration mode". Evidence: `chrome.spec.ts`, the a11y matrix in both schemes.
- D67 (2026-09-25) — Brief question 3, the digest treatment: 8…4 with copy, as built (F390, F401, F411): the digest is an id, and one spelling across Directory, Upload, Report, Committee and the drawers is what lets a reader match two of them by eye; the whole value is one hover (its title), one screen-reader read and one press away. Upload's twelve characters were the outlier and are gone.
- D68 (2026-09-25) — Brief question 4, the header's state when the summary is in view: as built (F397). The header's warning chips repeat the verdict's warning, so they give way while the summary is on screen and return once it scrolls away; the run's execution badge stays, because the header is the only place execution is said (the summary draws persistence and approval, never execution).
- D69 (2026-09-25) — Brief question 6, Committee's two links: under the paper, as the paper's own, not header actions. A header action is a governed command the section's document judges (`chrome.actions`, at most three, one primary, each with its refusal); "Open the rendered page" and "Download the package" are the host's own reads of the filed revision, refused only as `PACKAGE_NOT_FILED` or `LINK_NOT_OWN`, and set among commands they would read as acts. They sit with what they open, in the paper's centred page width (F413).
- D70 (2026-09-25) — N100, the summary's grammar. The verdicts mixed two grammars: some were fragments with full stops ("4 cases.", "Filed.", "Accepted CP-CF projection.", "1 withdrawn source stays cited where it was used."), some headlines without ("In progress · CP-6 at its gate"). A verdict is a headline, not a sentence: one line, sentence case, no closing full stop. With a subject it is "State · detail" ("Frozen · awaiting committee", "Saved · not yet frozen", "Accepted · CP-CF projection", "Screening only · not committee clearance", "Filed · deliverable of record"); a count alone is the count and its noun ("4 cases", "3 credits", "5 sources admitted"), and draws no headline figure, since it is one; a warning leads with what is wrong ("1 run parked · waiting on a retry", "1 withdrawn source · still cited where it was used"). The four cells beneath are the sentences and keep their stops. The headline figure is hidden only where the verdict states it with what it counts ("4 cases" over a 4 labelled "cases"): it used to vanish on any equal number, so one case beside "1 run parked" lost its figure. Evidence: `compose.test.tsx`, DESIGN.md's summary rule.
- D71 (2026-09-25) — N101, the severity of a stated limitation, in the bundle's terms; amends F159, which kept `RESTRICTED` for node states only. The bundle never calls a limitation a warning: its word for a module that ran carrying a limitation forward is QA `Restricted`, the normalised form of every "with limitations" and "with gaps" (`CANON_SHARED.md` D1; CP-2G "with gaps or limitations→Restricted"), which meets the QA gate (`QA_GATE_MET`), and CP-CF's contract is to "carry every upstream restriction"; its warnings are a field of their own, `validation_warnings`. So a handoff or CP-CF's forecast reads: QA not passed (Failed, Blocked) critical; any validation warning, or a QA word the bundle does not declare, a warning; QA Restricted, a stated limitation flag or a screening-only scope (never Committee Ready) RESTRICTED's ring; else success (`handoffSeverity`, now read for Model's forecast too). Analysis's verdict wears the ring when every module to review is restricted, the warning when any is warned or failed. The accepted CP-CF node itself stays COMPLETE on the route; the ring is its handoff's reading, not a route state. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx` ("test_handoffSeverity_and_conclusionOf"), `compose.test.tsx` ("a limitation carried forward is RESTRICTED's ring, a validation warning a warning (D71)").
- D72 (2026-09-25) — N105, Run's node cards: a code and a state, with the placing edges said elsewhere -- the brief's first design, not a wider card, which on a desk screen of 1280 px would add a column's scroll to every route of more than six stages. The card's third line says why without the edges it names (`cardReasonOf`): "awaiting the gate", "accepted", "READY_WITH_LIMITATIONS" (the gate's verdict is the cause, and short), and where the edges are the whole reason it counts them, "waits on 2 edges", "runs without 1 edge". The edges are the canvas's own lines, listed in full in the Selected node card ("Edges in", beside the full reason), and on the card's tooltip, which is also its description, with the catalog's name. On the real host the line was rarely long -- it serves only a node's unmet edges (`reads/run.py`, none for a completed node) -- and the clamped "accepted · CONDITIONAL rn-cp-1 …" was the demonstration fixture's (N106). Evidence: `route-graph.test.tsx` ("a node card says its reason without the edges it names (D72)"), `run.test.tsx` ("test_node_states_are_the_bundles_four_with_reasons"), `layout.spec.ts` (the reason never clipped).
- F414 — The demonstration fixtures spoke a vocabulary the bundle does not have, which D65's verbatim quoting would have put on screen: QA `ACCEPTED`, committee statuses `READY`, `Ready` and `Screen Only`, decision scope `FULL_COMMITTEE`, and a scope of "Credit review". They carry the bundle's values: QA "Passed"; "Committee Ready" for a full-scope module that passed; CP-1C, QA Restricted, "Restricted" (the committee transition its QA takes); CP-CF "Draft Only", the one value its host contract gives a passed forecast (`icm/stages/cp-cf`, and `handoff.py` allows only Draft Only or Restricted); and scope `FULL`. Evidence: the unit suite and the a11y matrix over the corrected fixtures.
- F415 — Found reviewing the methodology for N105: the route canvas drew no edge and no QA gate from a real host. The host names a route node `RN-{profile}-{selection}-{stage}-{module}` and an edge's source by its module (`EdgeView(source="CP-5")`, `reads/run.py`); `edgesOf` took the source as a route node id and the canvas looked it up among them, found nothing and skipped every line. The demonstration fixture hid it by naming sources as route node ids (`rn-cp-5`). A source now resolves to its node either way -- a route holds each module once (`ROUTE_DUPLICATE_MODULE`). Evidence: `route-graph.test.tsx` ("an edge the host names by its module is drawn to that module's node": every edge a line, the gate `CP-5 → CP-6`, over host-form ids).
- F448 (2026-09-28) — Directory, Upload and Report accepted late command refreshes over newer documents, including live withdrawals. Their reads now share Run's sequence guard in `useDocumentRefetch`: a newer parent document, later read or unmount supersedes an earlier response. Evidence: `directory.test.tsx`, `upload.test.tsx`, `report.test.tsx` (delayed responses), `upload.test.tsx` (overlapping admission and withdrawal reads, completion after leaving), and the existing Run ordering tests.
- F449 (2026-09-28) — Successful admissions, case creations and standing grants cleared input entered while their requests were pending. Text clears only while it still matches the submitted value; a file picker clears only while its selection has not changed. Evidence: `upload.test.tsx` and `directory.test.tsx` (later selections, titles and member ids survive).
- F450 (2026-09-28) — F448 ordered command reads separately from Workspace reads, so an older Workspace response could cancel or overwrite a newer command refresh. Both now take tickets from one Workspace sequence, and a queued parent render carries its read version. Superseded Workspace reads still release their flight and drain a coalesced event; leaving a section invalidates only its local commands. Evidence: `workspace-live.test.tsx` (both request and response orders, failure warning, refresh after a discarded read, and Reload while a read is in flight), `document-refetch.test.tsx` (queued parent render after command success or failure).
- F451 (2026-09-28) — F449 compared only a grant's member id, erasing it when the reader edited Standing during submission. A grant now clears its recipient only if neither field was edited since submission, including an edit back to the original value. Evidence: `directory.test.tsx` (standing edits, later recipient, and unchanged form clears).
- D73 (2026-09-25) — N106, the route's edges on the wire, and the Run fixtures in the host's form (owner: "apply your recommendation for N106"). The host served only a node's unmet edges (`NodeView.waiting_on`, none for a completed node), so a canvas could draw only what was still waiting and a finished route had no line on it; the demonstration hid that by carrying invented edges on completed nodes. `RunView` gains `edges`, the pinned route's own typed edges, both ends by module and in the route's order, met or not (`RouteEdgeView{source, target, type}`, at most `ROUTE_EDGES_MAX` = 4096; empty while no route is pinned, as `nodes` is) -- filled from the route the read already holds, so it costs no store read. `waiting_on` keeps its meaning, a node's reason. The schema is regenerated and the key sets pinned (`test_wire_contract.py`, `test_api_routes.py`). The canvas draws the route's edges (`edgesOf(nodes, edges)`, both ends resolved to their nodes as F415 resolved the source), and the Selected node card lists every edge that places the node ("Edges in") apart from those it still waits on ("Waiting on"), so a completed node no longer reads "none". The Run fixtures (`run.json`, `states/run.*.json`, the stream's four frames) are the host's form: sources by module, `waiting_on` only the unmet (the QA gate kept where the node awaits it), a route-level `edges` list, and CP-1 → CP-4 REQUIRED where the fixture had a CONDITIONAL edge the host refuses (`ROUTE_EDGE_UNSUPPORTED`) and the catalog never carries. Evidence: `test_run_section.py` (the served edges equal the resolved route's, both ends on the route; none before a pin), `test_api_routes.py`, `test_wire_contract.py`, `wire-contract.test.ts`, `run.test.tsx` ("test_edges_are_the_route_s_own_met_or_not"; the Selected node card), `route-graph.test.tsx`.

### Final sweep, 2026-09-25 (`docs/rebuild/final-sweep/CONTRACT.md`, BASE `2a4f983`; run in a cloud container, the gates as a non-root user under Node 24 and uv 0.12.5, as CI runs them)

- F416 — Acceptance row A28 was red at BASE: `caos/qualification/store.py` named the test provider in a comment's example (`("openrouter:x", "m")`), though the provider exists only under `tests/` (CLAUDE.md), and no CI step runs A28, so nothing caught it. The example now names a neutral provider, and the suite holds A28's scope itself. Test `tests/test_gate_structure.py::test_openrouter_is_named_only_under_tests` (failed at BASE, naming the file).
- F417 — `tests/test_identity_platform.py::test_a_cached_caller_is_served_while_a_cold_burst_waits` (ED-8) was red at BASE, three runs in three, with the cached caller measured at 1.38–1.87 s against a bound of 0.75 s. The clock started before each request built its own `httpx.Client`, and building one reads the CA bundle into a TLS context: 0.07 s alone, 1.49 s while forty-five are built at once on this host (measured). So the test timed its own clients, not the server's answer to the cached caller. Every client is now built before any clock starts, each request still opens a connection of its own, and the bound (half the SCIM pause) is unchanged: 5.3 s runs, three in three green. No test is weakened; the server path it measures is the same.
- F418 — HIGH, the final sweep's review of what landed after review 5. `sections/analysis/parts.ts`'s `SENTENCE_END` matched the closers after a sentence's stop as `(?:\*\*|\*|["”’)])*`, and `**` as one alternative beside `*` let a run of stars pair in a Fibonacci number of ways before a failed match gave up: a module's lead paragraph ending in a stop and 44 stars took 15 s in `splitLede`, 60 never finished, and the conclusion module renders on load, so one accepted handoff froze Analysis for every reader. The closers are one character class, `[*"”’)]*`, which accepts exactly the same strings. Test `tests/unit/markdown.test.tsx` "a run of stars after a sentence's end costs one pass, not one per way to pair them" (80 stars: killed by a 45 s timeout before, 19 ms after).
- F419 — HIGH, the final sweep's review of what landed after review 5. A `<!-- table-id: -->` comment carrying anything beside its id (`<!-- table-id: cp1.debt_facility_register MATERIAL: amounts unaudited -->`) was drawn as the id alone in the Formatted view of a Report or Committee artifact, the view Sign and Freeze sit beside, though `render.py::_comment` puts the whole comment on the committee's page and its docstring names this exact loss ("bound and unseen"); Analysis consumed the same comment with its tag. `ds/markdown.ts::tableTag` reads a tag's id and whether the comment says nothing else, and only a bare tag is drawn as its id; any other comment is drawn as written, and Analysis keeps it as a note of the register the tag labels. The two copies of `TABLE_ID` are one. Test `tests/unit/markdown.test.tsx` "a caveat written beside a table's tag is shown, never consumed with the tag" (the caveat absent before).
- F420 — MEDIUM, the same review. Three patterns over model text cost a pass per split: `ModelMarkdown.tsx`'s `FIGURE` (two adjacent `\s*`; a table cell of 60,000 spaces took 3.6 s), `parts.ts`'s `REGISTER_ID` (`[0-9]+[A-Z]?` followed by `[0-9A-Z]*`; a heading of one letter and 40,000 digits took 6.5 s) and the `SHAPES` rules over a register's title (`[^;]+` per `::`), and the 500,000-character formatting ceiling held for a handoff but not for a Report or Committee artifact of up to 4 MiB, read again on every render. A cell longer than 64 characters is no figure; `REGISTER_ID` reads the same ids as `[A-Z][0-9][0-9A-Z]*`; a title past 300 characters names no shape; `ds/markdown.ts::FORMATTED_MAX` bounds every `<Markdown>`, read once per text (`useMemo`). Test `tests/unit/markdown.test.tsx` "model text of any length costs the page one pass per pattern" (3.6 s at its first check before).
- F421 — MEDIUM, the same review (backend, M5). A run whose pass was refused `ATTEMPT_UNSETTLED` (another session's call on its node not yet settled, F363) was released at its old place, and `claim_run` takes the oldest `requested_at` first, so it was the head of every claim: each worker took it, was refused and backed off, and no other actor's queued run was claimed until the other session let go, up to `CALL_HOLD_SECONDS`. `store.work.release(..., behind=True)` puts it after every run queued now, as `requeue_run` already does; a store fault keeps its place, since that fault is every run's. Test `tests/test_worker.py::test_an_unsettled_run_goes_behind_the_queue_rather_than_ahead_of_it` (the same run claimed again before).
- F422 — Refactor R3 of the final-sweep contract: the four baselined functions it required, on invariant paths, are at 15 or below and out of `complexipy-snapshot.json`, so `complexity_baselined` falls from 21 to 17. `render._list` (31 to 9; the AR-08 site) opens, closes and numbers a list level in `_open_level`, `_close_deeper` and `_ordinal`, and `verify_package.RENDERER_SHA256` moves with `render.py`'s bytes, as its comment says and F255 did; `handoff.stored_lineage` (30 to 8) reads each direct link in `_direct_link` and admits each ancestor in `_admit_link`, still inside the one `_or_refuse`; `pdf.walk_pages` (23 to 12) checks a page's ceilings in `_within_limits` and walks text boxes through `_text_boxes`, still lazily, page by page; `stream.case_tail` (22 to 9) drains a poll in `_poll`, a sub-generator that answers whether standing still holds. No behaviour moves: the parity goldens (`render` and `extraction` among them) are byte-identical, and the render, deliverable-package, lineage, PDF and stream suites pass unchanged. CLAUDE.md says 17.
- F423 — Refactor R6 of the final-sweep contract, the documents half (CF-100, CF-101). The spec's G14 still baselined "the 26 legacy functions" and §11's rows stopped at A33, though A34–A37 (D28, F73) are gates CLAUDE.md lists: both are corrected in place with a `(corrected: Fn)` pointer. Each is dropped where the rule it cited is stated beside it, or repointed (DESIGN.md "Rules with teeth", CLAUDE.md invariant 4, `@/ds/SurfaceState`, `@/wire/shared`); `frontend/src/wire/v1/schema.json` is regenerated from `python -m caos.api.wire`, since three of `wire.py`'s docstrings are its descriptions. `caos/store/schema.sql` keeps its own: it is migration `0001_legacy`, whose bytes an applied store records, so no comment in it may change. CI's `bundle` job gains a Postgres service and the step, pinned in `check_gate_config.CI_STEPS` with its one variable; CLAUDE.md lists it, and the two commands CI already ran that it did not (`scripts/document_register.py`, `vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py`), so neither side has a gate the other lacks (§7.4). - F425 — §7.2 and §7.3 of the final-sweep contract. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` section 8 named the stand-in's limits in one sentence, with no row per gap: it now lists each known gap (presigned downloads CF-095, OAuth M2M N7, 429s and output-token limits CF-009, Lakebase grants and the migration role CF-010/OD-5, `forward_user_access_token` CF-033, the proxy and the stream C42, the app's own model call N82, the platform's Python install B7, the Lakebase version D17, nested groups), what the stand-in does instead, and the row that shows the real answer, and names the shipped-set boot. `docs/rebuild/ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` cited D29–D45 and listed no open owner decision: it cites D29–D73, states the fork (r4, D47) and the widened second attempt (D41) behind "Afterwards 1", what is proven here and what only the workspace can prove, and the owner decisions still open (OD-10, OD-11, OD-5's remainder, OD-6).

F420 bounded a register's title for `parts.ts::shapeOf` but not its columns, and the same `SHAPES` rules (`:: [^;]+;`, `threshold; .*`, `\b(walk|bridge)\b(?=.* :: )`) ran unbounded over the head: one header cell of 120,000 characters of `:: x` took 3.7 s, 30,000 columns named `threshold` 11 s, and a 330,125-character handoff, under `FORMATTED_MAX`, 9.9 s through `moduleParts`, where Analysis builds a module's parts on load. ## GitNexus review resolutions, 2026-09-27 (baseline 9393940)

- F432 — R035: a pipe-delimited single-column QA table now reaches the vendor severity gate, including CRITICAL and MATERIAL caps. The shared CP-0 validator was changed and the package refreshed and re-pinned; `test_canonical_handoff.py` proves refusal at the host seam.
- F433 — R011: opening the case switcher re-reads the Directory so newly created or granted cases appear; the one-flight guard still coalesces overlapping opens. Evidence: `chrome.test.tsx`.
- F434 — R014: the PDF reader walks form XObject layout boxes and admits their visible text. Extractor identity v9 names `all_texts` and the changed token set; the page reader accepts v9 coordinates, while prior stored extractions retain their recorded identity until readmission. Evidence: `test_pdf_extraction.py`, `test_evidence_page_read.py`.
- F435 — R015: a nonrectangular fill has an unknown backdrop across its box, not a falsely known colour; a true rectangular fill keeps the near-background mark. This shares extractor identity v9. Evidence: `test_pdf_extraction.py`.
- F436 — R016: admission and withdrawal refreshes share one Upload generation; an older response cannot replace a newer source pack or a parent document. Evidence: `upload.test.tsx`.
- F437 — R031: the quote opener index uses a normalized first-word key, so verbatim evidence that begins with punctuation survives outer quotation marks without a quadratic index. The exact typography predicate remains unchanged. Evidence: `test_handoff_record.py`.
- F438 — R040: labelled analysis lists with nested items use the recursive Markdown view, retaining caveats instead of projecting their parent text alone. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx`.
- F439 — R047: Report draft state lives above the revision-keyed view and is scoped to the displayed case and run; words typed during a save survive the successful revision remount. Evidence: `report.test.tsx`.
- F440 — R050: retrying a qualification set verifies completed or blocked earlier runs from their stored pin, route, source set and bundle, records them without executing again, and resumes the stopped run. Evidence: `test_qualification_harness.py`.
- F441 — R053: a mounted qualification badge expires at the signed deadline, stops claiming success and re-reads the server; an already expired response never displays Qualified. Evidence: `qualification-strip.test.tsx`.
- F442 — R059: the static shell serves the client router's declared legacy forwards directly with or without a trailing slash, preserving the query and avoiding directory redirects. The server list is checked against `routes.json`. Evidence: `test_site.py`.
- F443 — V003: a sources-changed event invalidates and rechecks an open evidence page independently of its section refresh, so a failed Report read cannot leave withdrawn page text displayed. Evidence: `evidence-page.test.tsx`.
- F444 — CI's public quote-parser regression measured shared-runner wall time, so its one-second assertion failed while linear matching and all correctness checks passed. It now bounds token comparisons through the same public parser, using the existing counted-word probe. Restoring the quadratic scan fails the bound (1,444,576 comparisons against 29,056); application matching is unchanged. Evidence: `test_handoff_record.py::test_the_public_parser_answers_repeated_near_matches_in_linear_work`.
- F445 — The health-probe isolation regression used a 50 ms deadline and immediate rounds, so CI could report a healthy probe timed out through scheduling or a still-retiring thread. Delaying a worker thread's exit reproduced the latter even with the production deadline. The test now uses the production five-second deadline and joins completed healthy threads between its accelerated rounds, while the held identity probe must still time out and run exactly once. Application probe deadlines and intervals are unchanged. Evidence: `test_health.py::test_a_probe_that_never_returns_cannot_starve_the_others`.
- F446 — Quote matching checks a candidate's final edge before its first edge. A long repeated near match ending in a different word otherwise normalized the matching opening edge at every candidate before refusing at the end, repeatedly exceeding the existing one-second CI bound. Last-edge rejection avoids that work; region bounds, exact-match priority, ambiguity refusal and edge normalization rules stay unchanged. Evidence: `test_quote_normalisation.py::test_a_long_near_match_costs_no_more_than_the_page` and `test_the_linear_search_finds_what_every_start_found`.
- F447 — The Upload timestamp layout test follows the canonical demo case UUID. Splitting the interface changes left its updated URL in the later graph patch while the workflow patch already served the canonical case, so the test timed out in every browser. The URL correction now ships with the fixture change; the timestamp assertions remain unchanged. Evidence: `layout.spec.ts` ("timestamps never wrap inside themselves"), passing in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.
- F452 (2026-09-28) — AF-01: a one-column QA table with only a leading or trailing border is still a table. The authoritative vendor reader recognizes either border, so CRITICAL and MATERIAL cannot disappear from the severity gate. Shared validator copies, vendor integrity, host manifest and test pins are refreshed through the existing fork procedure; prompt output is unchanged. Evidence: `test_canonical_handoff.py`, vendor verification and current/legacy prompt parity.
- F453 (2026-09-28) — AF-02: a PDF Form XObject inherits the invoking text and graphics parameters. Its interpreter restores copies after native initialization and resets local text matrices, retaining font, fill and render mode without changing the parent. Evidence: inherited-state, nested-form and parent-isolation cases in `test_pdf_extraction.py`.
- F454 (2026-09-28) — AF-03: text outside an active form BBox is excluded under the existing whole-word crop policy. Native untransformed glyph boxes map directly into each form, avoiding an inflated world rectangle for rotated text. Nested, crossing, reflected and singular forms are covered; extraction identity v10 preserves old stored identities. Evidence: `test_pdf_extraction.py` and the vertical multibyte-font/TJ probe in the fix-resolution report.
- F455 (2026-09-28) — AF-04: eligible filled form glyphs enter the chronological page-cover ledger, so a later opaque page fill retains the painted-over warning. Clipped glyphs do not enter that ledger; stroked/Type3 text, uncertain form-internal fills and work limits retain their existing exclusions. Evidence: form/page cover controls in `test_pdf_extraction.py` and `test_hidden_text.py`.
- F456 (2026-09-28) — AF-05: every active event-stream open invalidates the independent evidence-page epoch as a delivered source-change event does. A replacement stream can miss the earlier withdrawal, and a refusing Report refresh must not prevent the drawer from re-reading the page. Evidence: `evidence-page.test.tsx` and fresh real-API withdrawal/recreated-stream replay on the combined frontend.
- F457 (2026-09-28) — AF-06: the case switcher keeps ready rows during each fresh read and reconciles native popup/item focus after replacement, retaining Base UI's roving index when rows are inserted or removed. Cold load and Home/Enter reopening now select the actual case; focus outside a closed popup remains alone. Evidence: `chrome.spec.ts` in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, and fresh Directory-response keyboard replay in both themes.
- D74 (2026-09-29) — The module workbench (owner decision, 2026-09-29: accepted, with the module index from the 1024px desk floor rather than the 1200px first proposed, so the 2026-09-23 critique's rule that every view stays in sight holds at every width D63 supports). It lands as a series of three changes, each within the PR size gate: this one (the layout, the header's facts and the Model chart, F458–F461), then the Analysis figures for the vendor tables (F462–F464, then F467). Every section's body is 1.2rem padded with 0.8rem gaps and the sidebar is 13rem (`module-workbench` on the shell, caos.css after the components layer). At 1024px and above a section's views are a vertical index beside the selected view, sticky under the header, arrows Up/Down, with the selected name and its review reason above the list; below 1024px, which only a zoomed reader reaches, they reflow into a native select (DESIGN.md's select moves from below 640px). At 1024px the view is 538px beside the open sidebar, with no horizontal scroll: the prose keeps its measure, the figures take one column, and the sidebar's icon rail (⌘/Ctrl-B) is the reader's way to widen it. The header's facts are compact status badges (green, amber, red by the bundle's vocabularies, the served word always beside the colour) and no longer print the route node. The Model chart draws one figure per value and unit with a line per case when the cases share a period schedule, else one figure per case. The Analysis figures gain the vendor tables' charts: CP-1C peer comparison (one chart per metric and currency; a percent or multiple is never on a currency axis), CP-1D bridge, CP-2D liquidity waterfall, CP-2E rate exposure, CP-2H trigger headroom, CP-3C maturity wall, CP-4A covenant headroom and CP-8 expected against realized; each partitioned so no chart mixes units, bounded like the rest (F327), and every mark's source is the row's own cells. Evidence: `chrome.test.tsx`, `model.test.tsx`, `analysis.test.tsx`, `tests/workbench/layout.spec.ts` (the index at 1024px, beside the view), and `figures.test.tsx` with the figures.
- F458 — Adversarial review of D74: the wide layout put the module's view below a blank column whenever a page banner (offline, not live, paused) stood between the index and the view, since the grid places the index in the first column and the view after it in document order. The index is now rendered after the banners, directly before its panel, and caos.css states that nothing may stand between them. Evidence: `layout.spec.ts` ("a banner above the module index leaves the view beside it, and each fact wears its hue": a refused tail's banner, the view's left edge right of the index and its top level with it, at three modules; it fails with the old order) and `workspace-live.test.tsx` (the DOM order).
- F459 — Removing the route node from the header's facts also removed the only place Analysis showed a module's artifact digest (a hover title). The Audit tab has a "This handoff" group, host-verified, with the route node and the artifact and record digests as `Digest` chips. `SectionTabs` and `AppShell` no longer carry Tailwind utilities that the workbench CSS overrides (sticky placement, tab height and padding, wrapping, body padding), and their comments say where the rules are. Evidence: `analysis.test.tsx` ("the three labelled parts render for every handoff, in their places"), `chrome.test.tsx`.
- F460 — The header's status colours cover the bundle's whole vocabularies, spelled exactly as its validator admits them: "Requires More Work" is amber, and "Insufficient Information", for a committee status and for a confidence band, is red, worse than "Low". Evidence: `layout.spec.ts` (the F458 test: each fact's computed colour against its token at three modules; it fails when a word's rule is removed).
- F461 — A Model chart carrying several cases was titled with its first case ("cash · Base"). `forecastSeries` returns the chart's title: the value alone when one chart carries two or more cases (the legend names them), the value and the case otherwise, since a one-line chart has no legend and a Downside-only forecast would read as a plain one. Evidence: `model.test.tsx`.
- F462 (2026-09-29) — F454 tested each form glyph's em box against the form's `/BBox` as written, so v10 dropped text every viewer draws: a form cropped to its ink (12 pt Helvetica's caps reach 8.6 pt, its em box 9.5 pt) and a `/BBox` naming its other pair of corners, which ISO 32000-1 7.9.5 allows, both extracted nothing, where v9 read them. A glyph is now inside a form when its box's centre is, against the normalised bounds; a run with any glyph outside is still dropped whole. Extraction identity v11; stored v1–v10 rows keep their identity. Evidence: `test_pdf_extraction.py::test_form_bounds_drop_whole_clipped_words` (ink-cropped and reversed-corner cases, red on v10; every earlier clip case unchanged), Poppler and Ghostscript renders of the same fixtures.
- F463 (2026-09-29) — F456 advanced the page epoch on every stream open, and the server ends each tail after `TAIL_DEADLINE` (300 s), so an open evidence drawer dropped its page to "Reading the page…" at least every five minutes, and a recheck that never reached the server replaced a good page with the offline state. The drawer keeps the page it is rechecking until the answer arrives, and an offline answer leaves it up; a withdrawn page still answers 404 and is replaced by its refusal. Evidence: `evidence-page.test.tsx` ("a recheck keeps the open page on screen…", red before), and the F443/F456 withdrawal tests unchanged.
- F464 (2026-09-29) — The Analysis review line (PR 6) took the first module whose severity was not SUCCESS and said it "was accepted with a review point", including a module QA blocked or failed, which the tab strip calls "Blocked or failed"; an earlier restricted module also hid a later blocked one. The worst module now leads (blocked or failed, then warning, then limitation), and a blocked or failed one reads "did not pass QA". Evidence: `selection.test.tsx` ("a module QA blocked leads, and is not called accepted", red before).
- F465 (2026-09-29) — `gitleaks git --no-banner` exited 1 with 43 `generic-api-key` findings in three files added by `9b6b61a58b` (a Codex worktree snapshot, `refs/codex/snapshots/30d7ff02…`, local only: not an ancestor of any branch and absent from origin, so CI never scanned it): `docs/rebuild/gitnexus-review/<baseline sha>/baseline.json` (15), `evidence/prepublication-integrity.json` (14), `evidence/final-integrity.json` (14). Each match is a `"<repo path>": "<64 hex>"` line of an integrity manifest; all 43 were recomputed and each hex is the SHA-256 of the named file at the baseline commit `93939406…` (43 of 43, none differ), so none is a credential. Action: one path-scoped entry in `.gitleaks.toml`'s `[allowlist] paths`, `^docs/rebuild/gitnexus-review/[0-9a-f]{40}/(?:baseline|evidence/(?:prepublication|final)-integrity)\.json$`; no pattern allowlist (F68 stands), no history rewrite, no other file in that directory exempt (a scratch-repo control with a `ghp_` token in `evidence/notes.json` under the same directory shape is still reported). Evidence: `gitleaks git --no-banner --report-format json --report-path gl.json` → 43 leaks before, under the local v8.30.1 and under the hook's pinned v8.24.3 alike; after: `gitleaks git --no-banner` → no leaks found, exit 0, under both. The hook `gitleaks (uncommitted tree)` failed locally on git-ignored paths only (280 findings in 93 files under `.claude/worktrees/`, `docs/rebuild/runs/` and `frontend/.impeccable/live/sessions/`, every one ignored by `.gitignore`); F466.
- F466 (2026-09-29) — `gitleaks dir` walks the disk and ignores `.gitignore`, so the `gitleaks (uncommitted tree)` hook (F172) scanned local state that can never reach a commit and exited 1 in a checkout that holds other worktrees, retained audit runs or live-editing logs (280 findings, 93 files, all git-ignored: content digests, `KeyError` lines, session UUIDs, and one planted `ghp_` string in `docs/rebuild/runs/audit-2026-09-23/evidence/DB-11/gl/leak.py` that fails GitHub's offline CRC32 token checksum). No path allowlist in `.gitleaks.toml`: that file also configures the history scan, and a path exemption there would hide a force-added secret. Action: the hook runs `bash scripts/gitleaks_tree.sh`, which copies `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` (tracked plus untracked, never ignored; a tracked file deleted on disk is skipped) into a scratch directory and runs the same `gitleaks dir --no-banner --redact -v .` there, so `.gitleaks.toml` and its `^`-anchored paths apply as before; CI, whose clean checkout holds no such state, scans the same set. What it no longer reads: an ignored file such as `.env`, which no commit can carry. `check_gate_config.py` pins the new entry (N46). Evidence: `tests/test_gitleaks_tree.py` (against a stand-in scanner: tracked and untracked files reach it, an ignored file and a tracked file deleted on disk do not, its exit code is the hook's; two mutants, `--exclude-standard` dropped and the scanner never run, each fail it), `test_the_gitleaks_dir_scan_of_the_uncommitted_tree_is_required`; `bash scripts/gitleaks_tree.sh` from the main checkout under the hook's pinned v8.24.3 → 27 MB scanned, no leaks found, exit 0 (the unmodified `gitleaks dir --no-banner --redact -v .` there: 280 leaks, 1.70 GB).

## Enterprise models, 2026-09-29 (owner: "what requires enterprise databricks knowledge and access needs to be documented and instructed clearly for the enterprise opus 5 implementer, including what it should not do; anything that does not require that should be remediated now"; and "App needs a model selector ... only the attached models approved")

- D75 (2026-09-29) — The model choice, its server half (the deploy scripts carry it in part 2 of this series, the Create run select in part 3). The deployment approves its models: `CAOS_MODEL_ENDPOINT` at `CAOS_MODEL_PRICE` is the configured one, and `CAOS_MODEL_CHOICES` lists the others as dated prices in the same form joined by `;`, at most 16 in all (`caos.pricing.model_choices`; a malformed entry, an endpoint priced two ways or one too many refuses every model `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`; an entry equal to the configured price is that one, and an empty entry is nothing). The Run section serves them as `model_choices` (`ModelChoice`: endpoint, per-token rates as exact decimal strings, date, `configured`), and `CreateRun.model` names one, or null for the configured one; a model the deployment does not name is refused `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` and starts nothing. The run is pinned to the model and its dated price at creation (F468), and the audit payload's `model` names the model pinned while the request digest stays the request's. Alternatives: a speed slider (the choices are discrete endpoints with no order the host can claim, and a slower model is not a deeper one); a reasoning-effort control (not sent to the endpoint, N2); a separate CP-DR model (no model has reached CP-DR live, so a choice for it rests on nothing); offering only qualified models (none has qualified on the gateway yet, so the select would be empty). The API reads the choices from `caos.pricing`, never `caos.models` (D4: no API module reaches a provider transport). Evidence: `tests/test_model_choice.py` (8 tests: the list and its refusals, the pin and its write-once trigger, `started_on`, the worker's pinned execution and the park on a dropped model; `tests/test_model_choice_api.py`, in part 2, drives the command, the audit and the Run section).
- F468 (2026-09-29) — A run did not pin its model. `runs` held no model and the worker built one provider for the process (`caos/graph/worker.py`), so after a redeploy that changed `model_endpoint` an attempt reserved before it was refused `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` (`bills_at`, `caos/methodology/canonical.py`) and every later node ran on the new model: one committee conclusion from two models, while `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` section 7 said runs "finish under" the old price. Action: migration `0043_run_price` adds the model and its dated price to `runs` (all four columns or none, readable as 0024's reservation price, written once by trigger as `supersedes_run_id` is); `start_run(price=...)` writes them; `create_run` pins the chosen or configured model (`started_on`), and a process configuring no model pins none, as every run was before; the worker builds each run's provider from its pin (`module_execution(provider_for, ...)`, `approved`) and executes it at the pinned price, or parks it `PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` before any attempt when the deployment no longer approves its model. The Run section and the case directory name each run's model. A run started before 0043 keeps null columns and runs on the configured model. Section 7 now says what happens. Evidence: `tests/test_model_choice.py::test_the_worker_executes_a_run_on_its_pinned_model_at_its_pinned_price`, `::test_a_run_on_a_model_the_deployment_dropped_parks_before_any_attempt`, `::test_a_run_keeps_the_price_it_was_started_on_and_it_is_written_once`.

## Enterprise models, part 2, 2026-09-29

- D75, addendum (2026-09-29) — The deploy half of the model choice. The bundle variable `model_choices` (default empty) reaches the app as `CAOS_MODEL_CHOICES`, composed `${var.model_price};${var.model_choices}`, the configured price first: the CLI resolves a variable that is empty to an env entry with no `value` at all (seen under the stand-in: `{'name': 'CAOS_MODEL_CHOICES'}`), which E2 would read as a mismatch and the platform may refuse, so the value is never empty. `scripts/preflight.py --choices` holds every approved model to the configured one's rules (a fit endpoint, one worst-case call under the run ceiling, no endpoint priced twice); E2 holds the resolved value to `<price>;<choices>`; the one command takes the choices as argument 8. The bundle grants `CAN_QUERY` on `model_endpoint` alone: an app'"'"'s `resources` is a fixed list, and a complex variable in its place did not merge with the targets'"'"' own `database` resource (tried under the stand-in: the resolved list came back as the unexpanded variable), so a workspace administrator grants it per choice (`docs/rebuild/ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` step 4.2; N119). The Create run form offers the choice in part 3. Evidence: `tests/test_model_choice_api.py` (the command'"'"'s pin, refusal and audit, and the Run section'"'"'s choices), `tests/test_workspace_stub.py::test_preflight_holds_every_approved_model_to_the_configured_one_s_rules`, `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py`, the four targets'"'"' validate, deploy and run under the stand-in and `tests/shipped_boot.py`.
- F469 (2026-09-29) — `scripts/enterprise_deploy.sh` defaulted arguments 5 and 6 to the bundle's `databricks-claude-opus-5` at the public list price, so a deploy given four arguments priced every budget at a price no enterprise contract states, or failed E1 on an endpoint the workspace does not serve (the owner's workspace names its endpoints without the `databricks-` prefix). Action: arguments 5 and 6 are required, refused with exit 2 before anything runs; the bundle's defaults stay for the stand-in gates. Evidence: `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py::test_the_endpoint_and_its_contract_price_are_never_defaulted`.
- F470 (2026-09-29) — E10 wrote "no model call answered" for every park, including `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` and citation refusals, where the gateway had answered and the host refused the answer: an implementer reading the row would chase a gateway fault. Action: a `PROVIDER_*` park keeps that wording; any other reads "the model answered and the host refused the answer". Evidence: `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py::test_e10_passes_only_on_a_call_the_gateway_answered` (the `HANDOFF_MALFORMED` case).
- F471 (2026-09-29) — `scripts/qualify.py`'s docstring named `OPENROUTER_*` as its provider; the script calls `caos.models.from_environment` (`ChatDatabricks` through the caller's CLI profile), and OpenRouter reaches it only through the test-only `tests/qualify_openrouter.py` wrapper. The enterprise path to qualify a model on the gateway was therefore undocumented. Action: the docstring says both, with a gateway example; the handoff's step 4.3 gives the command.
- F472 (2026-09-29) — The enterprise documentation did not say how to find the workspace's endpoint names, whether an endpoint sends documents outside the workspace, how to derive a per-token price for a DBU-billed or provisioned endpoint, that preflight refuses payload logging (it sat in section 7, not the prerequisites), that a green E1–E10 does not show the model can complete a run, how to qualify a model through the gateway, or what the implementer must not do. Action: `docs/rebuild/ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` rewritten around what only the workspace can do: a "Do not" list, the owner's decisions to obtain first, the values and where to find them, the steps (deploy, grant each other approved model, qualify on the gateway, the first governed run, record), the rows with what to do on each failure; `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` section 1 carries endpoint discovery, data egress, price derivation and the gateway refusals, and the one command's new signature.
