## The goal condition, 2026-09-29 (owner: "make sure the current goal prompt is relevant and up to date")

- D76 (2026-09-29) — `docs/rebuild/2026-09-22-goal.txt`, the file `docs/rebuild/launcher/launch.sh` reads as the newest `*-goal.txt` and runs as `/goal`, stated the greenfield build: read the spec, meet gates G1-G25 and A1-A33, commit to `rebuild/databricks`. The build is complete, so a launch from it would have rebuilt what exists. It now states the enterprise deployment: the values only the owner has, the ordered steps of `docs/rebuild/ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` (E1 to E10, the grant and smoke of each other approved endpoint, the gateway qualification, the first governed run, the record), what voids the result, and the evidence table that means done, in 3,816 of the 4,000 characters the launcher allows. A value left as a placeholder finishes blocked on an owner decision rather than asking, as an unattended `/goal` cannot be answered. The file keeps its name so the launcher still finds it, and no other `*-goal.txt` may be added while it is the newest; the build goal is at `9401e48`. Evidence: `wc -m` 3,816; every command and file it names exists (`scripts/enterprise_deploy.sh`, `scripts/gateway_smoke.py`, `scripts/qualify.py`, `qualification/ccl-fy2025-market-dislocation`).

- F473 (2026-09-29) — CI failed `tests/test_run_commands.py::test_a_successor_run_links_a_blocked_run_of_its_case` ("the audit payload binds the predecessor") on PR 20 and passed on 16 to 18: order-dependent. `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py`'s `main` writes `CAOS_MODEL_ENDPOINT`, `CAOS_MODEL_PRICE` and `CAOS_MODEL_CHOICES` into `os.environ` for the scripts it calls, and `tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py` calls it in process; since D75 `create_run` reads those names (it pins the configured model and audits it), so a `create_run` later in the same xdist worker pinned a model and its audit digest differed from the test's. Before D75 nothing on that path read them, so the leak was harmless. Action: an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py` restores the three names after every test (restored, not cleared first: the live-provider suites read them from the caller's environment). Reproduced without xdist: `pytest -p no:xdist tests/test_enterprise_deploy.py::test_a_recorded_cli_step_is_one_row_with_the_log_tail tests/test_run_commands.py::test_a_successor_run_links_a_blocked_run_of_its_case` failed before the fix and passes after.

## GitHub Copilot as the model, 2026-10-01 (owner: "Update them so I may initialise the build")

- D77 (2026-10-01) — The enterprise workspace has AI Gateway disabled (B12), so the spec's §3.3 path, `ChatDatabricks` on a serving endpoint, cannot answer a call there. Lakebase, Unity Catalog volumes and compute remain available. GitHub Copilot, which the firm already approves, becomes the model, and CAOS stays the orchestrator. A model named `copilot:<model>[@<effort>]` is answered through the GitHub Copilot SDK, and `copilot-cli:<model>[@<effort>]` through the Copilot CLI as a fallback chosen by name, never automatically. Both sit behind the one factory (`caos.models.chat_model` returns `ChatCopilot`), so the run's model pin, the attempt ledger, budgets, the canonical envelope and anchored citations hold unchanged. The worker runs on the analyst's PC, where the Copilot seat is, against Lakebase and the volume. The build is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-copilot-sdk-adapter.md` (PR 23): the SDK transport lands as "D77, addendum" (its Task 2), the CLI fallback as D78, the worker on Windows as D79. Initialising it amended spec §2 and §3.3, CLAUDE.md's Layer 0, `docs/rebuild/ENTERPRISE_HANDOFF.md` (status note; "No other provider") and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` (status note), and reissued the goal (D76, addendum). Alternatives: Copilot CLI as the orchestrator, with one skill per module and Delta over ODBC (run order, the exactly-once ledger, the envelope and citations would rest on instructions, not code; rejected); RAI, the firm's own gateway (needs use-case onboarding; a later provider behind the same factory); waiting for the gateway (no date given). Open: whether Databricks Apps may host the API and UI (the administrator's answer; the plan's N124), and Compliance's confirmation that issuer documents may be sent to Copilot models (owner).
- D76, addendum (2026-10-01) — `docs/rebuild/2026-09-22-goal.txt` now states the Copilot build (D77) instead of the enterprise deployment through the gateway, which cannot pass while AI Gateway is disabled (B12). It executes the plan inline on three stacked branches, holds every CLAUDE.md gate, and makes no paid or Copilot call unless its VALUES line authorises one. The default authorises none, so the plan's Task 1 spike and its Task 7 live run finish as the owner's steps on the analyst PC. It does not push or open a PR. The file keeps its name so the launcher still finds it; it uses 2,868 of the 4,000 characters (`wc -m`). The deployment goal is at `dfd64ad`.
- D77, addendum: dependency (2026-10-01) — `github-copilot-sdk==1.0.16` (MIT) is locked with the documentation, so the build starts from a lock `pip-audit` has cleared. Its dependencies, `python-dateutil`, `pydantic>=2.11` and `httpx>=0.24`, were already locked. No standard-library module or pinned dependency speaks the Copilot runtime's protocol. Evidence: `uv lock --check` clean; `uv run pip-audit --strict` → \"No known vulnerabilities found\" (after F474).
- F474 (2026-10-01) — `uv run pip-audit --strict` failed on the base lock: `pyjwt 2.14.0 GHSA-42vr-xj54-vc7v`, fixed in 2.15.0. `pyjwt` arrives through `mcp[crypto]`, `databricks-mcp` and `databricks-langchain`, not through the SDK; the advisory is new, so every PR's security job would have failed. Action: `uv lock --upgrade-package pyjwt` (2.14.0 → 2.15.1). Evidence: `uv run pip-audit --strict` → "No known vulnerabilities found".
- F475 (2026-10-02) — Answer keys checked as whole evidence lines. F235 made an accepted citation one whole evidence line (`WHOLE_LINE`), and the matrix scores a key by exact equality with an accepted quote (`caos/qualification/matrix.py` `_matches`), so a key that is a fragment of a line, or runs onto the next, can never be met. `tests/test_qualification_on_disk.py` checked only that a key was a unique run, which let thirteen such keys into the CZR sets (`czr-2026q2-earnings-update`, `czr-2026q2-liquidity`, `czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing`, `czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`). Action: each is restated as its whole source line, unique in its document; the committed-key test now also requires every key's words to equal exactly one evidence line of the document as admission writes it (each token line cut into its `token_groups` blocks). Every older committed set already passed. Evidence: the test fails on the previous keys and passes on the restated ones; the four set digests move. The assertion compares the exact string, as the matrix does.

## The synthetic test mandate, 2026-10-02 (owner decision of 2 October 2026)

- D80 (2026-10-02) — The CZR LITE portfolio set (`qualification/czr-2026q2-lite-portfolio/`) needs portfolio context, and no real portfolio is available: on `ccl-fy2025-portfolio` CP-0 refused CP-L10 for want of holdings and exposure, a mandate and limits, an eligible-security universe, current security-market evidence and the applicable executed governing security documents. The owner decided on 2 October 2026 to adopt the synthetic "Test CLO I" mandate from `REF_CP-6A_Portfolio_Debate_Inputs.xlsx`, adapted to carry a CZR position. It is admitted as owner-adopted test input, `TEST_CLO_I_Mandate_and_Exposures_2026-10-02.txt` (text SHA-256 `864ee90ab21372bc…`, raw workbook `05a32699b39cedce…`), labelled SYNTHETIC everywhere: in its own first lines, its register row's document and note, the set's `RESULT.md` and `DOCUMENTS.md`. It may never ground a real decision: no figure in it is a real holding, and a run over it qualifies the route's handling of a mandate, not a position. The register row `test-clo-i-mandate-2026-10-02` has `status: in_hand` and a note naming it synthetic; its `source` is a provenance string naming the workbook, not a URL. This does not contradict `ccl-portfolio-mandate-exposures`, which calls the same workbook "a methodology reference, not live portfolio evidence": it still is not live evidence, and that row stays `to_source`; D80 admits it only as labelled test input. The set also carries the notes' indenture as their governing document; no eligible-security universe exists in the workbook beyond its lien buckets (C-09, C-10), and none is invented. The adaptation proposes USD 5,000,000 par of the CZR 6.50% 2032 notes (CUSIP 12769GAC4) against the workbook's 29 May 2026 NAV of USD 630,928,502, 0.7925% of NAV under C-01's 2.5% single-name limit; the price is FINRA's real 1 October 2026 last trade. Alternatives: leave the set as an owner-gap refusal like `ccl-fy2025-portfolio` (it would measure the refusal again, not the ready path with a mandate); wait for a real portfolio extract (`ccl-portfolio-mandate-exposures` stays `to_source`; no date).

## Live-run fixes, 2026-10-02 (owner decision of 2 October 2026: "Apply your recommendation to all. Run only one module at a time, and patch the faults before moving on to the next.")

- F476 (2026-10-02) — Markdown emphasis around a body quote. The 2 October live runs wrote a cited line as `**For the period 2026**.`; the body-quote check carried the same line in quotation marks or a backtick (F148, F149) but refused it in emphasis, a host defect recorded as the model's answer. Action: `*` and `_` join `_QUOTATION` in `caos/methodology/handoff.py`, so `*`, `**`, `_` and `__` are edge typography exactly as the quotation marks are (unpaired and mismatched markers are accepted as unbalanced quote marks already are); only the two outer tokens may wear them. Evidence: `test_a_quote_the_body_wraps_in_markdown_emphasis_is_still_quoted` carries each spelling and refuses the same line with an inner word changed. Nothing widens what may be cited; `verify_citations` is untouched.
- D81 (2026-10-02) — Evidence presentation the model can quote from (option 1 of the 2 October root-cause analysis, adopted by the owner's decision above). Of the 41 citations refused on the 2 October live runs, 19 were one whole sentence taken from a longer evidence line (lines of 167 to 909 characters) and 17 named the wrong page, 14 of them off by exactly one: the model was never told what "one evidence line" is, and the `page:` header was printed once per run of up to 60 lines (a `.txt` page), often kilobytes above the line it covered. Decision: (1) `_evidence_section` repeats the run's header, unchanged (source_id, page and any `hidden` note), before every eighth line of a run counted from the run's first line (`caos/methodology/invocation.py` `_HEADER_EVERY`, `_evidence_blocks`), so a line's page is never more than seven lines up; `evidence_sizes` still counts every host byte (W3), a line's size now a function of its position in its run, and since positions count from the run's start the sizes of any leading lines of each page are the pin's own, which `selection.gate_view` cuts by. (2) `icm/shared/prompt/final_check.md` defines an evidence line once: all the text between two blank lines in the EVIDENCE section, a whole paragraph or a whole table row; `matched_text` copies the entire line, any leading bullet or footnote marker and any trailing `|` included, never only a sentence of it; `page` is the page in the nearest evidence header above the line; a body quote keeps the source's own wording ("we", "our", "us"), never rephrased into the third person. Alternatives: accept a whole sentence of a line as a citation (option 5; not applied, revisit only if the live loop still shows fragments); a header on every line (about eight times the cost below). Cost, measured offline with the host's own extraction (`prepare_pack`), `gate_view`, `_evidence_section` and JSON escaping over the committed CZR sets: +63,281 evidence-section bytes on `czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing` (2,440,779 to 2,504,060), the largest +69,853 on `czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment` (2,541,436 to 2,611,289); those deltas are measured, while the whole request for the largest set, about 2.8 MB against `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` (4,194,304), is an estimate, not a measurement. Evidence: `tests/test_evidence_selection.py::test_the_header_is_repeated_every_eight_lines_of_a_run`, `::test_each_lines_size_is_its_share_of_the_evidence_section`, `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py` (the final check's wording).
- F477 (2026-10-02) — D81 is a deliberate departure from the legacy `prompt` parity group: the final check's citation sentence changed in all 27 cases (+311 bytes each); no fixture run is longer than eight lines, so the header repeat moves no golden. Only the `prompt` group was regenerated, from the legacy snapshot's own code with the change mirrored (D34's procedure; `tests/parity/goldens.py` refuses a manifest written from `caos`, F57): a scratch clone of the snapshot at `b5a3932f` with `vendor/deploy-v` replaced by the fork, its prompt constants read from `icm/shared/prompt/`, and the host's source-preparation text, citation-register header, withheld-workbook note (D38), final-check arguments and RELATIVE_VALUE/LITE fixtures mirrored; that copy reproduced every committed `prompt` golden byte for byte before D81 was applied to it (the final-check block and the eight-line repeat in its `_evidence_section`), then `tests/parity/generate_goldens.py --package server --root <copy> --out tests/parity/golden --group prompt`. The 27 regenerated cases equal what `caos` renders. Host pins: `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `caos/methodology/host_pin.py` regenerated by `scripts/host_manifest.py`.
- D82 (2026-10-02) — Retry feedback that says what was wrong, and two guided retries per node (option 2 of the 2 October root-cause analysis, adopted by the owner's decision above; it amends N52's "never by text" and D30's one second attempt). Evidence: of the 41 citations refused on the 2 October live runs, 19 were a sentence of a longer evidence line and 17 named the wrong page, yet the retry was told only "citation N is not one evidence line of its cited page"; 4 of the 6 second attempts broke a different rule than the first, which with one retry stopped the node; and 7 of the 13 handoff refusals carried no guidance at all. Decision: (1) a citation refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` is placed by the host's own anchoring code (`caos/evidence/citations.py` `find_line`, `LineFinding`): `_page_run` (`ANY_RUN`) on the cited page, and a unique run inside one longer delivered shown line names that line; failing that, `verify_citations` under `WHOLE_LINE` on each other page of its source the node was given names those pages; a quote with no `ANY_RUN` run inside the delivered lines of any delivered page is absent; anything else (twice on a page, across two lines, part of a line of another page) is not placed and keeps the rule's wording -- never a guess. Only pages delivered to the node are read: a cited page it was not given is never probed, so what that page holds cannot change the line. The search is help, not a verdict: any refusal from it (`EVIDENCE_PACKING_MISMATCH`, say) leaves the citation unplaced rather than costing the retry, and only the first `MAX_FEEDBACK_CITATIONS` unlocated citations are searched. `handoff.anchoring_line` (with `LineHint`) then says, per citation and for at most `MAX_FEEDBACK_CITATIONS` of them: `citation N of T is part of a longer evidence line of its cited page, which begins "<first 12 words>"; quote the whole line`, the words (`HINT_WORDS`) the delivered block's own, whitespace-split and joined by one space, read from what the node was delivered (`canonical._line_hint`); `citation N of T is one whole evidence line of page P, not of its cited page`; or `citation N of T is in no evidence line of its source this node was given`. Ambiguous and not-delivered keep their wording. The line is held to `MAX_ANCHORING_CHARS` (four vendor lines' room, 2,048 characters), dropping placements from the last back, each citation keeping its number under the rule's wording, so a retry's prompt grows by a bounded amount. The search is scoped to the cited source: a quote of another delivered source is told absent from its own, which is true. These words reach only that one prompt, never a log, refusal or row, like every feedback line (D30). (2) `canonical._feedback_source` gives a node's next attempt feedback when its latest attempt was refused with a `SECOND_ATTEMPT_CODES` code and the node holds at most `GUIDED_RETRIES` (2) such refusals, so its 2nd and 3rd attempts are each told of the attempt just before them and every later attempt is ordinary; still read from the ledger, so a crash between a refusal and its retry gives the same lines, and the prospective (priced) prompt and the attempt's rebuilt one count the same attempts. `runtime._with_second_attempt` loops at most `1 + GUIDED_RETRIES` passes, the ledger deciding each retry; a retry the ceiling cannot pay for leaves the refusal before it standing. No prompt golden carries feedback (none has a `SECOND ATTEMPT` section), so no golden moved. The host block `icm/shared/prompt/validator_feedback.md` now reads "This is a retry of this same request", true of both retries (its marker names are unchanged; no golden carries the block); `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `caos/methodology/host_pin.py` regenerated by `scripts/host_manifest.py`, and the 27 stage contracts by `scripts/icm_stages.py --write` from `caos/icm.py`'s condition. Tests that counted one retry now count two (`tests/test_execution_billing.py`, `test_canonical_runtime.py`, `test_lite_cp1c_contract.py`, `test_lite_route_e2e_negative.py`, `test_lite_full_credit_screen_route.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_runtime.py`, `test_worker.py`). Evidence: `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_find_line_places_a_quote_the_whole_line_rule_refused`, `::test_a_placed_line_is_shown_by_its_first_words_as_delivered`, `::test_a_search_that_cannot_read_a_page_leaves_the_citation_unplaced`; `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_the_anchoring_line_places_each_unlocated_citation_it_can`, `::test_an_unanchored_citation_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_it` (case b, end to end), `::test_part_of_a_line_gets_the_second_attempt_naming_it` (case a), `::test_a_second_refusal_gets_a_retry_told_of_the_attempt_before_it`, `::test_a_third_refusal_stops_the_run_with_no_fourth_attempt`, `::test_two_guided_retries_per_node_whichever_codes_refused`, `::test_the_third_attempt_survives_a_crash_after_the_second_refusal` (the resumed 3rd prompt equals, byte for byte less each run's own identity, a sibling run's that never crashed; prospective and rebuilt request sizes equal).
- D83 (2026-10-02) — The call deadline and the upstream-section bound (option 3 of the 2 October root-cause analysis, adopted by the owner's decision above; it amends F89's 240 s, brief D5's "a lease outlives two call deadlines", F110's 52 KiB and D29's default run ceiling). Evidence: `complete` holds one deadline over the whole call (ST-9) and `_invoked` abandons the call at it, recorded `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`; default-tier `gpt-6-luna-pro` calls took 131–229 s and 5 of 19 ran past 240 s, priority-tier 84–175 s with none past it, and the 65,536-token reservation at about 155 tokens a second is about 420 s. CP-1's accepted answers ran to 50,489–85,559 bytes on the CZR sets (9 of 11 over 53,248) and 55,474–76,186 on CCL of 23 September (6 of 6 over), so the next node refused `UPSTREAM_SECTION_OVER_CEILING`. Decision: (1) `caos.provider.TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is 420 s; `MIN_RESEND_SECONDS` stays half of it (210 s), `WORKER_STALE_AFTER` stays the deadline plus a minute (480 s), and E10's `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` in `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py` waits 440 s; the test-only OpenRouter adapter and `chat_model` carry the same constant. (2) The lease is unchanged. `reserve` is a fenced write that renews the lease to 600 s immediately before the call unit (`LEASE_SECONDS`); the call ends by 420 s, leaving 180 s for the bill (unfenced, under `call_hold`), the answer's checks and the fenced `accept_attempt` (F485: a liveness budget, shared with the work before the call too, not a safety invariant), so the rule asserted in `tests/test_worker.py` and `tests/test_models.py` is now `LEASE_SECONDS - TIMEOUT_SECONDS >= 180` in place of D5's two deadlines, which 840 s would break. A lease lost past that point still pays nothing twice: the bill has committed, `CALL_HOLD_SECONDS` (1,200 s past the reservation) keeps a second worker off the node while the call may still bill, and `replay_billed` settles the charged answer. Each D82 guided retry is its own attempt and reservation, so it renews the lease again. (3) `MAX_UPSTREAM_HANDOFF_BYTES` is 98,304 (96 KiB). The widest node is CP-5 of FULL_CREDIT_32, 16 direct upstreams: at the bound beside its 169,493 bytes of delivered authority it encodes to 1,750,342 bytes, leaving 2,443,962 of the 4 MiB request for evidence and for the upstream citation register, which that figure excludes and which is unbounded (F486); the widest-node test in `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py` now asserts the 16-upstream node was measured, that it fits with at least half the request left, and that the largest observed CP-1 answer fits with 8 KiB of room, and its docstring names CP-5, not CP-3. (4) The bundle's default `run_ceiling` is 120.00 (owner, 3 October 2026), amending D29's 100.00: at the default price `databricks-claude-opus-5,0.000005,0.000025,2026-09-22` the widest profile at the section bounds costs 96.122820 and one worst-case call 22.609920, 118.732740 ≤ 120.00, which `tests/test_run_ceiling.py` asserts. Alternatives: streaming with lease renewal (N14, still unmeasured on the workspace); the priority tier alone (a run configuration, not a host bound); restructuring CP-1's duplicate tables in the bundle fork (not recommended in option 4). No parity golden encodes either number.
- F478 (2026-10-03) — The CZR conversions carried layout as text. The staging converter (`scratchpad/edgar/convert.py`, outside the repo) ended every table row with ` |`, kept a list bullet glued to its first word (`•Consolidated`), and glued a footnote marker set in its own padded span to its word (`(a)The`), so a model quoting the line had to reproduce a separator, a glyph or a missing space the page does not show. Action: a row now ends at its last cell; a leading `•`, `◦` or `▪` is dropped; one space follows a leading `(a)`/`(1)`-style marker glued to a word (not `(a)(i)`, not a figure such as `(2)%`). All eleven EDGAR conversions were re-run (3,038 row terminators, 177 bullets, 85 markers); the TRACE observations and the synthetic mandate (D80) are not conversions and keep their bytes, so the mandate's keys keep their row terminator. Every copy in the eleven `qualification/czr-2026q2*` sets is replaced byte for byte; 19 distinct keys (62 key rows) whose source line changed are restated as the new whole line (F475), same words and figures; register notes, `RESULT.md` provenance hashes and set digests, `COMMITTED_SET_DIGESTS` and `DOCUMENTS.md` move with them. No size crossed 500 KiB, so the large-file pins stand. Evidence: the converter run twice gives identical SHA-256s; each conversion's non-whitespace character stream (less `|`) equals the HTML's visible text less the dropped bullets, and every changed line is explained by the three edits alone; `tests/test_qualification_on_disk.py` passes on the restated keys.
- F479 (2026-10-03) — The test-only OpenRouter adapter sends a reasoning effort and pins a provider. The owner asked to probe cheaper models (`openai/gpt-6-luna` at effort high, `openai/gpt-6-luna-pro` on the `openai/flex` tier, `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` at low on a named host), and the adapter sent neither. Action: `tests/openrouter_adapter.py` reads `OPENROUTER_REASONING_EFFORT` (`minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh`, anything else a `RuntimeError`) and `OPENROUTER_PROVIDER` (comma-separated provider names or endpoint tags) at call time and sends `reasoning: {"effort": ...}` and `provider: {"order": [...], "allow_fallbacks": false}` through the `ChatOpenAI` `extra_body`; unset, the request is unchanged. The identity names what ran (AR-15) as one plain string: `openrouter/<model>/<effort|none>/<max tokens>`, or `openrouter/<model>@<order>/<effort|none>/<max tokens>` with a pin, so `--expect-identity` stays a string compare. Production is untouched: `CAOS_REASONING_EFFORT` stays refused (N2). Evidence: `tests/test_openrouter_adapter.py`.
- D84 (2026-10-03, owner-authorised: option 4 of 2 October, "you are authorised to edit vendor files to improve performance and reliability" of 3 October) — Vendor fork r5, the two clarity fixes (build `3715187a`, manifest `717915f3`; D31's procedure). **CP-1's audit sections:** `REF_CP-1_STEPS.md` Step 12 item 5 said "the single Audit Appendix ... holds ALL audit items as sub-sections", while `validate_handoff.py` refuses anything but the six H2s once each ("H2 headings must be exactly once and in canonical order: Audit Summary -> Analysis -> Evidence Trace -> Source Registry -> Gaps & Conflicts -> QA Validation"); N3's CP-1 attempts 3 and 4 wrote Evidence Trace and Source Registry as `####` and were refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED`. The item now says those four audit sections are canonical H2s, quotes the validator's rule, says a `###` or `####` one in place of the H2 fails (the validator checks only H2s), keeps the registers they draw on (T4.1, T4.11, T4.12, T4.13) in the analytical appendix, and puts limitation flags in the front matter; it no longer names Source Gate / Readiness or Limitation Flags as appendix registers, neither being a T4 register (Task 5 review, M2, M3). **Register binding:** `completeness_check.find_registers` binds a table to a heading only among the last four non-blank lines above it (`recent[-4:]`), which no module text stated; N4's CP-0 attempt 5 put five blockquote lines between `#### T6 — Evidence Trace` and its table and was refused `HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE` (`T6: required register missing`). The canon's analytical-appendix rules (`CANON_SHARED.md` § TABLE_DESIGN_STANDARDS, delivered to every module whose `SKILL.md` names the canon, which is every module with registers) now state it: the heading or caption is one of the four non-blank lines directly above the header row, every non-blank line counts (prose, blockquote, a `table-id` comment), notes go below the table. No checker changed. Re-pin: `python3 -B vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py --refresh` (71 vendor tests, 2 integration skips, 10 self-checks, PASS), `uv run python scripts/host_manifest.py` (`bundle_pin.py`; host integrity unchanged), the build and manifest pins in `test_bundle_pin.py`, `test_methodology_bundle.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_canonical_proof.py`, `test_qualification_matrix.py`, and `MEASURED` in `test_delivered_authority.py` (+519 bytes each: CP-0 152,446, CP-L10 206,375, CP-5 170,012). Parity: only the `prompt` group, all 27 cases (+513 characters each for the canon paragraph, +981 for the two CP-1 cases), regenerated by D34's procedure: the legacy snapshot clone at `b5a3932f` with `vendor/deploy-v` replaced by the fork, the host's prompt text and fixtures mirrored as F477 describes, which reproduced all 27 committed goldens byte for byte before this change; then `tests/parity/generate_goldens.py --package server --root <copy> --out <dir> --group prompt`. Evidence: `vendor/deploy-v/tests/test_regressions.py::ForkR5Tests` (`test_cp1_audit_sections_are_the_validators_h2s`, which demotes Evidence Trace and checks the validator's message is the quoted rule; `test_a_register_heading_binds_within_four_non_blank_lines`, three notes found and four missing).
- D85 (2026-10-03, owner decision of 3 October: "cut CP-1's duplicated tables", under the same day's authorisation to edit vendor files) — Single-table CP-1, vendor fork r5 continued (build `cc142a28`, manifest `62ec09a0`, then after the Task 5 review build `3fb5cf14`, manifest `f6704fd4`; D31's procedure). **Finding:** the one-table form was already the method's since fork r3 (`REF_CP-1_STEPS.md` § REF_CP-1_13: "write each under its register heading, then its table-id comment, then the one table"), and every reader already reads it; what was missing was a rule the model meets in `SKILL.md`, and a null spelling one table can carry. Of the 11 CP-1 answers stored on 2 October, 7 (N2's accepted artifact among them) were already single-table and 4 wrote T4.14-T4.19 untagged and again as a block of tagged copies; those 4 were the largest (56.8-84.3 KB), and N3 attempt 2's copy of the period register lost a cell (`table row width differs from its header`), voiding all seven interface tables. **Text:** CP-1's `SKILL.md` output profile says each of T4.14-T4.19 is one table written once (heading, `table-id` comment, table), never a second tagged copy, only blank lines between the comment and the table, an absent value `null` and not the canon's `—` (an explicit override of `CANON_SHARED.md`'s null rendering for these tables), and every such `null` listed in `## Gaps & Conflicts` with its table, row, column and reason; REF_CP-1_13 says the same and why `null`: an empty cell or `n/a` fails the register check (`critical_cell_values_casefold`) and `—` is not a CP-MODEL null (`validate_cp_model_inputs.NULL_TEXT`, `_number`, `_list`), while `null` is null to the register check, to `cp_tables.is_null` and to CP-MODEL. **Effect on the completeness check:** a critical `null` passes the critical-cell check, as the canon's `—` did before it; `n/a`, `tbd`, blanks and the rest of `critical_cell_values_casefold` are still refused, and that list is unchanged; the absence is carried as a gap in `## Gaps & Conflicts`, which the text requires and no checker enforces (Task 5 review, I1); the segment allocation, operating KPI schedule and readiness table, which have no T4 register, are each written once, tagged. **Readers, unchanged and why:** `completeness_check.find_registers` counts the tag line as one of the four lines its heading must sit within (D84), so heading, tag, table binds the register; `cp_tables.parse_tables` (the interface parser, one copy per skill) binds a tag to the next table across blank and comment lines only; CP-MODEL's `parse_stable_tables` skips blank lines only (`validate_cp_model_inputs.py:347`) and refuses a comment between a tag and its table with `ContractError`, so the prescribed form allows only blank lines there (Task 5 review, M1); the host reads tagged tables only through `parse_tables` (`caos.methodology.tables.handoff_tables`) and has no CP-1-specific handling; downstream texts (CP-MODEL, CP-1D, CP-2G) name `cp1.*` tables by id, never by position. No checker, parser or host code changed. **Old form:** still valid, unchanged readers. Replay of all 40 stored 2 October answers (`feedback_lines`: transport, quotes, front matter, missing IDs, `validate_handoff`, `completeness_check`, CP-0's T8) gives byte-identical check lines under build `994a1b3f` and `cc142a28`; the host's full replay verdict is unchanged for the 23 attempts with no accepted upstream, and the other 17 refuse `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED` because their upstream was accepted under the old build, the host's designed refusal on any re-pin (as at D31-D47), not a contract change. **Size:** N2's stored accepted CP-1 (`a96c77f6`, 57,322 bytes) was already single-table, so re-rendered it is 57,322 (0%); the four two-copy answers re-rendered (each tagged copy dropped, its tag set above the register table) shrink by 8.9-20.5 KB, 12-24% (N2 #2 56,809 to 47,239; N3 #2 61,750 to 49,941; N3 #3 84,326 to 63,866; N3 #4 74,095 to 65,169), each with the same check verdict; the host's CP-1B route fixture goes from 38,839 to 24,913 bytes. Single-table answers still run 48.8-60.9 KB, so D83's 96 KiB upstream bound stays needed. Re-pin: `verify_package.py --refresh` (72 vendor tests, PASS), `scripts/host_manifest.py`, the five build and manifest pins; `MEASURED` unchanged (no CP-0, CP-L10 or CP-5 file moved); the two CP-1 `prompt` goldens (`model_owner__CP-1`, `relative_value__CP-1`, +791 characters, then +475 after the review) regenerated by D84's procedure, the other 25 byte-identical each time. Evidence: `vendor/deploy-v/tests/test_regressions.py::ForkR5Tests::test_a_cp1_interface_register_is_one_tagged_table` (single and two-copy forms through the register check, `parse_tables` and CP-MODEL's reader; `null` off and `n/a`, `tbd`, blank on the critical-cell list; a comment after a tag refused by CP-MODEL; a tag below three notes leaves the register unbound but the interface table read), `tests/test_vendor_fork.py::test_cp1_writes_each_interface_register_once_as_one_tagged_table` (the CP-1B route fixture's CP-1 in both forms passes `validate_handoff`, the completeness check, `handoff_tables` with equal values, and CP-MODEL's `validate_cp_model_inputs.py` CLI against the fixture's CP-1B).
- D86 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V1, presentation only) — `citations` before `canonical_markdown`. Three of 36 refused answers on 2 and 3 October (P1 CP-1 attempt 2 among them, validator and completeness clean at 62 KB) wrote a 30-60 KB `canonical_markdown` string and never wrote `citations`, refused `HANDOFF_MALFORMED`. `icm/shared/prompt/instruction.md` shows `citations` first in the JSON shape and says "`citations` first, then `canonical_markdown`"; `final_check.md` says the same after the evidence. `strict_json` is order-blind, so an answer in either order is read as before. Host prompt text: `icm/HOST_INTEGRITY_v1.json` and `host_pin.py` regenerated (`scripts/host_manifest.py`); `prompt` goldens regenerated at the end of Task 5c (D88's re-pin). - F480 (2026-10-03, carried from Task 2's review) — The final check's evidence-line definition counted an evidence header as a line and left the section's last line, which the END EVIDENCE marker follows with no blank line, outside "between two blank lines". `final_check.md` now excludes "an evidence header (its `source_id:` and `page:` lines)" and says the section's last line is an evidence line too; it names the marker without its `---` so the prompt carries no second section marker. A deliberate departure from the legacy `prompt` parity group, as F477: every case's final check changed. - D88 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V2, owner-authorised under the same day's authorisation to edit vendor files; presentation only) — Vendor fork r6: the tagged-table readers tolerate what well-meaning answers wrote and refuse what they refused before. **Findings:** `cp_tables.parse_tables` raised for the whole document on one bad row, which `completeness_check.check` turned into every unconditional interface table "missing" (P1 CP-1 #1: one 14-cell row of the 15-column period register, ten lines of which eight were consequences; N3 CP-1 #2 nine lines); any line but a blank or comment between a tag and its table broke the bind silently, a `####` heading included (P1 CP-1 #3's readiness tag above `#### CP-MODEL Readiness`); a separator cell needed three hyphens; a row split at an escaped `\|`. **Change** (`skills/cp-0-source-readiness/scripts/cp_tables.py`, synced to its 22 copies, and CP-MODEL's `validate_cp_model_inputs.py`): `read_tables` returns the tables and, per malformed tagged table, its first fault -- a short or long row as "row N (first cell `…`) has K cells, header has M -- table row width differs from its header", plus how many more rows differ -- and reads every other table; `parse_tables` is `read_tables` refusing the whole document on any fault, as before, so the host's `handoff_tables` still serves no partial set (`TABLES_MALFORMED`) and the host's feedback ordering (`handoff._consequence`, which keys on "differs from its header") is unchanged; `completeness_check.check` appends each table's fault and reports "interface table missing" only for a table with no fault. A tag crosses heading lines (`###` to `######`) to its table, but only as a tolerance: a tag bound across a heading binds only a well-formed table and only an id no tag binds the old way and no second tag crosses to, and is otherwise dropped as before; prose, an H1 or H2, or another tag between still breaks the bind. A separator cell is one or more hyphens, optionally colon-aligned. A body row whose split at every `|` is not its header's width is split at the unescaped pipes, each `\|` read as `|`; a row that split to its header's width is read as before. CP-MODEL's `parse_stable_tables` skips heading lines after a marker as it skips blank lines, takes the same separator cells and splits a row the same way, so the readers still agree on every table CP-1's check accepts (a comment between tag and table is still refused there, D85). **Guarantee:** everything either reader accepted before is accepted and read the same (one exception, fix round 1's C2 below: a register row whose escaped `\|` had hidden a critical placeholder), held against fork r5's reader over 6,000 random documents; a malformed tagged table is still refused by the check, by `parse_tables` and by CP-MODEL. Replay of the review's answers under the new checkers: P1 CP-1 #1 10 to 4 lines, N3 CP-1 #2 9 to 3 (the third is a duplicate `cp1.cp_model_segment_allocation` tag, still refused), P1 CP-1 #3 3 to 2, N2 CP-1 #1 5 to 4, every other case unchanged. Evidence: `vendor/deploy-v/tests/test_regressions.py::ForkR6Tests` (`test_one_malformed_interface_table_is_named_alone`, `test_a_tag_crosses_a_heading_to_the_next_table_only`, `test_one_hyphen_separators_and_escaped_pipes_read_alike`, `test_every_document_read_before_reads_the_same`), `tests/test_vendor_fork.py::test_a_tag_above_its_register_heading_binds_for_every_reader` and `::test_a_short_interface_row_names_only_its_table` (the CP-1B route fixture's CP-1 through the check, `handoff_tables` and CP-MODEL's CLI). **Fix round 1** (adversarial review of Task 5c: three ways an answer r5 refused for a real defect passed under build `43d79461`): (C1) a tag crosses a heading only when the heading names no register ID (`cp_tables.REGISTER_ID_RE`, the locator's own pattern, now shared) and the tag cannot be the table above's -- a heading lies between that table and the tag, or that table was bound by a tag that could itself cross; with each T4.14-T4.19 tag written below its table, each tag had crossed the next register's heading and bound that register's table (the CP-1B fixture's CP-1: six interface tables missing under r5, none under `43d79461`, six again now), and `handoff_tables` served them under the wrong ids; (I1) a crossed read binds only an id tagged once in the document, and only across blank and heading lines, as CP-MODEL reads it -- a second tag before a malformed copy, prose or nothing had left it bound while CP-MODEL refused the duplicate; (C2) `completeness_check.find_registers` splits a row as the interface reader does (`cp_tables._row_cells`), where it had split at an escaped `\|` and truncated, so a blank, `n/a` or `TBD` in a CP-1 row with an escaped pipe in its locator slid out of its critical column and passed; this refuses an answer r5 accepted only where such a placeholder had been hidden; (M1) neither reader re-reads a row opening or closing on `||` at its unescaped pipes, since CP-MODEL strips every edge pipe and `cp_tables` one. CP-MODEL's `parse_stable_tables` takes the same crossing conditions. Agreement: over 20,000 random documents, each table the r6 reader newly binds across a heading is the table its tag was written for, every newly bound id is tagged once, CP-MODEL reads it alike and the register locator reads the same cells; over the reviewer's 200,000-document fuzz no document both readers accept is read differently, and every remaining divergence is CP-MODEL refusing a document in which `cp_tables`, as in r5, drops a tag silently (before prose, or crossed into a malformed table) and which a newly tolerated separator or row lets it accept. Unchanged from r5: a tag directly above the next table binds it, whichever table the writer meant. Cost: a tag directly below an untagged table, with no heading between, crosses nothing, so a readiness tag written that way is not tolerated across a heading; P1 CP-1 #3's is (the table above it was bound by a tag that crossed), and the replay counts are unchanged. Evidence, each failing on `0dd56a1`: `ForkR6Tests::test_a_tag_below_its_table_never_crosses_into_the_next`, `::test_a_crossing_tag_binds_only_when_its_id_is_tagged_once`, `::test_an_escaped_pipe_never_shifts_a_critical_cell`, `::test_a_row_opening_on_a_double_pipe_is_never_re_read`, `::test_the_three_readers_agree_on_what_the_tolerances_accept`; `tests/test_vendor_fork.py::test_a_tag_below_its_table_binds_no_other_table`, `::test_a_second_tag_of_a_crossed_id_unbinds_it`, `::test_an_escaped_pipe_never_hides_a_critical_placeholder` (three cases), and `::test_a_tag_above_a_heading_naming_no_register_binds_for_every_reader`, which replaces `::test_a_tag_above_its_register_heading_binds_for_every_reader` (a heading naming a register is no longer crossed, its own included). **Re-pin**, once for D86-D92 by D31's procedure: `python3 -B vendor/deploy-v/verify_package.py --refresh` (80 vendor tests, 2 integration skips, 10 self-checks, PASS), build `43d79461` (was `3fb5cf14`), manifest `bf3851ff` (was `f6704fd4`), and after fix round 1 (85 vendor tests) build `fa90657d`, manifest `0f1b7c9b`; `uv run python scripts/host_manifest.py` (`bundle_pin.py`; host integrity `23ca9822` for D86, D87 and F480); the build and manifest pins in `test_bundle_pin.py`, `test_methodology_bundle.py`, `test_loop_charges.py`, `test_canonical_proof.py`, `test_qualification_matrix.py`; `MEASURED` in `test_delivered_authority.py` +723 bytes each (CP-0 153,169, CP-L10 207,098, CP-5 170,735), +732 after fix round 1 (153,178; 207,107; 170,744). Parity: only the `prompt` group, all 27 cases (+1,001 characters each for the host text and the canon; +57 more for CP-0's T8 line, +1,346 more for CP-1's skeleton; fix round 1 +9 more each for the canon's binding paragraph and SEC2), regenerated by D34's mirror procedure -- the legacy snapshot clone with `vendor/deploy-v` synced to the fork and the host's prompt blocks and fixtures mirrored, which reproduced all 27 committed goldens byte for byte before this change -- then `tests/parity/generate_goldens.py --package server --root <copy> --out <dir> --group prompt`. Stored answers: the replay above is under the new checkers; the host's replay verdicts of answers whose upstream was accepted under `3fb5cf14` refuse `ORCHESTRATION_BUILD_MOVED`, the designed refusal on any re-pin.

- D93 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V7, owner decision of 3 October: "apply all") — The payload schemas are withheld from every module. The canonical adapter takes a Markdown handoff and never a JSON payload (`handoff.validate_markdown` calls no `check_payload`), yet every module was handed each `*.schema.txt` its manifest lists or its `SKILL.md` names: 54,133 bytes of every CP-L10 request (the five `CP-L*__payload.schema.txt` and the root `CP_DEPLOY_V_LITE_MODULE_PAYLOAD_BASE_v1.schema.txt`), 11,628 of CP-0's, 5,705 of CP-2A's, 4,140 of CP-2G's, 3,307 of CP-2's, and 343 to 1,485 bytes for CP-1A, CP-1C, CP-2D, CP-2E, CP-2H, CP-3D, CP-4C, CP-8 and CP-DR. Decision: `delivered_authority` keeps back every file ending `PAYLOAD_SCHEMA_SUFFIX` (`.schema.txt`), manifest or root, through the existing withheld-authority mechanism (D38): `DeliveredAuthority.withheld` names them and the authority section ends with its own host-owned note ("PAYLOAD SCHEMAS WITHHELD": the schema of a payload this host never takes -- JSON Schema, or the vendor's compact text form for CP-0's; the handoff is the canonical Markdown alone, held to the registers, columns and values the `SKILL.md` and its delivered references state; a payload schema the `SKILL.md` names is neither a gap nor a step). D38's sample-workbook note is unchanged and is said only of those workbooks. Not every value set is stated elsewhere: some appear only in a withheld schema -- CP-0's `extraction_confidence`, `usability_verdict` and gap `type`; CP-2A/2B's `event_type` and `horizon`; CP-2E/2F's "Material - Directional"/"Material - Quantified"; CP-8's "Premature"/"Mistimed" (fix round 1, M4). No reader the host runs (`validate_handoff`, `completeness_check`, the host's own table readers) checks those columns against those sets, so no verdict depends on them; a module now writes them from its `SKILL.md` and references alone. Where a value set is checked it is delivered: CP-2G's `cp2g.cp_model_forecast_drivers` columns and every enum of the schema's `forecast_driver` appear in its `SKILL.md`, `REF_CP-2G_STEPS.md` or `CP-2G_ForwardCreditModel.schema.md`, and the host's own readers (`forecast.validate_driver_mapping`, the test fixtures' `driver_schema`) read the schema through `verified_bytes`, not through delivery. `host_steps.md` already says the host validates with `validate_handoff.py` and `completeness_check.py` alone, so it is unchanged. Cost: the `delivered_authority_digest` of the 14 modules above moves, so a record accepted under build `fa90657d` before this change no longer matches `methodology_matches` for those modules (new records only; the build and every other digest are unchanged); no stored record is migrated. Measured: CP-0 153,178 to 141,550 delivered bytes, CP-L10 207,107 to 152,974, CP-5 unchanged (`MEASURED`). Prompt parity: 17 `prompt` cases (every case of a module above) regenerated from the legacy snapshot with the change mirrored (D34's procedure, verified first to reproduce the committed goldens byte for byte); stage contracts regenerated (`scripts/icm_stages.py --write`). Evidence: `tests/test_delivered_authority.py::test_no_module_is_delivered_a_payload_schema`, `::test_every_required_reference_byte_is_delivered`, `tests/test_handoff_invocation.py::test_section_markers_cannot_be_forged_by_evidence`.

- D94 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V14, owner decision of 3 October: "apply all") — A whole evidence line cited on the wrong page is anchored at its true page. The Task 5b review counted ten refusals among the 36 answers refused on 2 and 3 October that a line unique within its source's delivered evidence would have cleared: a line the node was given, copied whole, cited under the wrong page. Decision: under `WHOLE_LINE` (the rule an answer is accepted under), when a quote is no line of its cited page (`CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`), `verify_citations` searches every other page of its source that holds a block the node was given (`_delivered_pages`, one read per distinct delivery and only on this path; an undelivered page is never read) with the same whole-line search (`_shown_line_run`, ambiguity counted over each whole page); exactly one matching line across them, and that line delivered, anchors the citation there (`_true_page`). Never a tolerance: the stored `page` and rectangles are the true page's, so every anchored citation is still one whole evidence line of the page it names (invariant 11), and the body-quote check is unchanged. The page the module named is recorded beside it as `AnchoredCitation.cited_page` (None where the quote is where it was cited): written into the canonical record only when set (`record_bytes`), so every record stored before reads back byte for byte, and a stored `cited_page` that is null, not a page or the page itself refuses (`_anchored`). It is served on the Analysis and Book `CitationView` (`cited_page`, null otherwise; wire schema, frontend shape and fixtures updated) and travels with the record wherever the record goes (Report, deliverable). Two matching lines (on one page or two), a match on a line the node was not given, a page it was not given, and part of a line still refuse `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`, as before, so the D82 guided retry and its `find_line` hint are unchanged for them (`find_line` asks each page at that page alone, never re-anchoring). Every reader reaches the live verdict, each by its own check: `replay_billed` rebuilds the node's own delivery (its selection, or `gate_view` for CP-0) and re-runs `_answer` on the stored response, so it repeats the whole search; the proof and the deliverable only hold the run's captured blocks, which may carry copies on pages the node was never given, so they never search (fix round 1, C1): `verify_stored_citations` locates each citation at the page the record stores, by the record's rule, within the captured blocks, and keeps its `cited_page` only when that page holds no such line -- a record claiming any other re-anchoring no longer equals the record (`CITATION_MOVED`), and one storing a page the quote is not on refuses with the citation's own code (`CITATION_NOT_LOCATED`), as before D94. The uniqueness among the node's pages is judged at acceptance and at `replay_billed`, not re-judged by those two readers. Placing a quote for a guided retry (`find_line`) never re-anchors: one search per delivered page, as before (fix round 1, I1; 360 delivered pages, one quote on none: 0.08 s, where re-anchoring inside the placement took 3.45 s). The search reads the other delivered pages in one query (`TokenIndex.read_pages`), so a quote found on no line of its cited page costs two round trips more at acceptance (its delivered pages, then their tokens) however many pages were delivered (M1). Only `WHOLE_LINE` re-anchors: records accepted under `ANY_RUN` or `WHOLE_LINE_AS_STORED` are located as they always were, and no record accepted under `WHOLE_LINE` changes (each anchored where it was cited, which is tried first). No migration: citations live in the record blob. Evidence: `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_a_whole_line_cited_on_the_wrong_page_is_anchored_at_its_true_page` (true page and rectangles, `cited_page`; two pages, an undelivered line, an undelivered page never read, a fragment and the older rules refused), `::test_find_line_places_a_quote_the_whole_line_rule_refused`, `tests/test_second_attempt.py::test_a_wrong_page_citation_is_anchored_at_its_true_page` (end to end: accepted on the first attempt, the record's page and boxes the true page's), `tests/test_handoff_record.py::test_a_record_keeps_the_page_a_reanchored_citation_was_cited_on`, `tests/test_verification.py::test_refuse_maps_each_step_to_the_callers_code_or_lets_it_through`, `tests/test_analysis_section.py::test_a_reanchored_citation_is_served_with_the_page_it_was_cited_on`; fix round 1: `tests/test_reanchor_readers.py::test_live_replay_proof_and_deliverable_agree_on_a_reanchored_citation` (a node given pages 1-2 of a source whose page 3 repeats the line: live, `replay_billed`, the proof and the deliverable's reader agree), `tests/test_whole_line_citations.py::test_a_stored_citation_is_checked_where_it_is_stored`, `::test_placing_a_quote_reads_each_page_once_and_never_re_anchors`, `tests/test_handoff_record.py::test_a_record_keeps_the_page_a_reanchored_citation_was_cited_on` (a `cited_page` under any rule but `WHOLE_LINE` refuses, M2). The drawers say "Cited p.N, found p.M" for such a citation (`frontend/src/evidence/Reanchored.tsx`, M3; `evidence.test.tsx`, `evidence-page.test.tsx`).
- D95 (2026-10-03, Task 5b vendor review V5-option, V6 and V9, owner decision of 3 October: "apply all", under the same day's authorisation to edit vendor files; fix rounds 1 and 2 of the same day) — Vendor fork r7: the canon's dash a CP-MODEL null, only a value-bearing null a gap, and no consolidated copy of CP-1's statements (build `e58407e7`, manifest `7e92bd72`, authority bundle `90b6c5e2`; D31's procedure). **V5-option:** CP-MODEL's `validate_cp_model_inputs.NULL_TEXT` and the exporter's `cp_model_v3/domain.NULL_TEXT` gain `—` and `–`, and the validator's `_number`, `_list` and non-QUARTER `fiscal_quarter` checks read them (`DASHES`), so the canon's `—` in a tagged CP-1 table is null as `cp_tables.is_null` already read it, never "unknown component periods ['—']" or "FY must not set fiscal_quarter"; D85's `null` stays the prescribed spelling (the controller's ruling with the owner's confirmed critical null). **V6:** D85's "every `null` listed in `## Gaps & Conflicts`" now covers value-bearing columns only; a `null` in a reference column means none applies, is not a gap and is not listed: `conflict_refs`, `limitation_refs` (T4.15), `component_period_ids` on a directly reported period and `fiscal_quarter` on a row that is not a QUARTER (T4.14) (`SKILL.md` output profile, REF_CP-1_13; D90's skeleton keeps its two rows, whose nulls are all reference columns, and shows one value-bearing gap line, a T4.15 `value`). Readers checked for T4.7: no other module, script, catalog entry, qualification key, host reader (`tables.py`, the wire, the deliverable, the analysis readers) or frontend fixture reads CP-1's T4.7 by id (CP-1B, CP-1C and CP-4 have their own T4.7); CP-1B's Steps 5 and 6 now name CP-1's T4.4-T4.6 and its T4.9 and T4.10. **Binding:** `completeness_check.find_registers` let a prose line name a table's register when no heading near it named a listed one, so with T4.7 unlisted, N2's accepted CP-1 bound its `#### T4.7` table as T4.18 (the T4.6 note mentions T4.18) and failed T4.18's columns. Each module now lists the registers it retired beside `required_register_ids` (`retired_register_ids` in the appendix contract; CP-1: T4.7, no other module any), `load_contract` returns them as `retired_registers`, and `check()` and the host's `matrix.module_registers` pass them to `find_registers`: a heading led by one of them (`LEADING_ID_RE`: `#### T4.7 ...`, `### **T4.7** ...`) keeps its table, and no prose claims it. Any heading not led by the module's own retired ID now leaves the prose. - F485 (2026-10-03, Task 4 review M1) — The 180 s between the lease and the call deadline is a liveness budget, not a safety invariant. Comments in `caos/provider.py` and `caos/store/work.py`, D83(2) and the two tests' wording say so; the assertion `LEASE_SECONDS - TIMEOUT_SECONDS >= 180` is unchanged. A test (`tests/test_models.py`) now also asserts `scripts/enterprise_deploy.py`'s `MODEL_CALL_SECONDS` exceeds `TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (M2).

CP-1B was refused three times: each retry was told only which citations failed, rewrote its whole citation list, and traded the fixed quote for a new partial one (attempt 1: citations 6 and 7 of 8; attempt 2: 3 of 6; attempt 3: 8 of 10, each a sentence or cut row of a longer line). `handoff.anchoring_line` now ends, after the per-case clauses (D82's wording unchanged), with every citation that anchored and the rule: "keep citations 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 exactly as they were; any citation you add or change must be one entire evidence line of its cited page". Every kept number is listed (a counted "and 3 more" would not say which to keep); past `MAX_ANCHORING_CHARS` the kept list is dropped whole before any placement is, and the rule stays. - F492 (2026-10-03, owner decision of 3 October: "Split corpus by sentence") — The CZR conversions put a whole paragraph on one evidence line. In live run R2, `gpt-6-luna` was refused `CITATION_NOT_LOCATED` three times at CP-0: to meet the whole-line rule (F235, D81) it copied 700-800-character paragraph lines and slipped on a word or a character ("therein" for "of the Merger Agreement (as defined below),", an added "s"). Action: the staging converter (v3, `scratchpad/edgar/convert.py` and `sentences.py`, outside the repo; v2 kept as `convert-v2.py` and `text-v2/`) cuts every prose line into one sentence per line; a table row stays one line. The splitter is rule-based and stdlib-only: a boundary is the space after `.`, `!` or `?` (closing quotes and brackets may follow), at bracket and curly-quote depth 0, before a capitalised word; never after an abbreviation (`U.S.`, `Inc.`, `Corp.`, `Co.`, `Ltd.`, `No.`, `e.g.`, `i.e.`, `approx.`, `vs.`, `Mr.`, month abbreviations, `a.m.`/`p.m.`, `Sec.` and others, compared as written so `SEC.` still ends a sentence), an initial, an ellipsis, a leading list marker or section number, or a heading reference (`Item 1A.`, `Part II.`, a leading `SECTION 1.01.`); a citation mid-line (`as defined in Section 6.06. The`) does end one. A long sentence stays whole: nothing is cut mid-sentence. Across the eleven EDGAR conversions 1,253 prose lines were cut and 3,038 table rows kept, 9,314 lines became 12,021, lines over 400 characters fell from 1,910 to 1,576 (the longest, a 5,475-character credit-agreement definition, is one sentence), and no byte count moved (a space became a line feed). The TRACE observations and the synthetic mandate (D80) are not conversions and keep their bytes. Every copy in the eleven `qualification/czr-2026q2*` sets is replaced byte for byte (63 files); the 7 distinct keys (17 key rows) whose line was cut are restated as the one sentence that carries the key's figure or fact (F475), and where the old line carried a keyed fact in a second sentence, that sentence is keyed too, same module (5 key rows: CP-2E's `51%` and `5.87%`/`6.18%` rates in `czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment`, CP-4's senior unsecured guarantee there and in `czr-2026q2-portfolio` and `czr-2026q2-relative-value`, CP-2D's `$388` million letter-of-credit sub-facility in `czr-2026q2-liquidity`), and the press release's financing-sources sentence as CP-3C in `czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing`, whose `RESULT.md` already states it). The CP-3C revolver keys (`czr-2026q2-covenant-refinancing`, `czr-2026q2-lite-covenant-refinancing`, `czr-2026q2-lite-full-credit-screen`, `czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment`) keep the commitment sentence; the `$388` million letter-of-credit sub-facility and `$40` million reserves sentence is keyed only in `czr-2026q2-liquidity` (CP-2D), by choice; register notes, `RESULT.md` provenance hashes and set digests, `COMMITTED_SET_DIGESTS` and `DOCUMENTS.md` move with them. No size moved, so the large-file pins and the page-map threshold stand. Evidence: the converter run twice gives identical SHA-256s; `fidelity_v3.py` re-derives v2 from the HTML, and every v2 line equals its v3 sentences joined by one space, every table row is one unchanged line; on the largest set (`czr-2026q2-full-credit-assessment`, host code: `prepare_pack`, `gate_view`, `_evidence_section`) the pages rose from 168 to 213, a page's mean text fell from 14,600 to 11,503 characters (largest 46,709 to 32,251), the CP-0 evidence section rose from 2,560,242 to 2,584,090 bytes (+0.9%, the repeated D81 header and a second line feed per cut) and the encoded CP-0 request from 2,754,426 to 2,785,116 bytes, 66.4% of `MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`, with no source past the page-map bound. Limit: the R2 line is itself one 761-character sentence (the 10-Q's merger paragraph), so this split does not shorten it.

- D100 (2026-10-03, Task V2, live-loop run R1b CP-1B; owner approval of 3 October as D99) — Vendor fork r11, part 2: an interface register written without its table-id comment is told the comment is missing, not the table. The CP-1B contract maps no table-id to a register, so `completeness_check` pairs them by name: an interface table no tag binds is reported "`<!-- table-id: X -->` comment not found above the <register> table" when a heading led by a located register's ID has, as its title, the table-id's name part (letters and digits, casefolded, a trailing parenthetical dropped: `cp1b.model_comparator_register` and "T4.12 — Model Comparator Register", `cp1b.addback_validation_register` and "T4.14 — Add-Back Validation Register") and the comment for X appears nowhere in the answer. With no such heading, or with the comment written but bound to no table (prose between, or a tag above the register heading), the message stays "interface table missing". No reader's binding changes: `cp_tables.read_tables`, the host's `caos.methodology.tables.handoff_tables` and CP-MODEL's reader still bind a tag to the same table, and the pairing only chooses the message for a table none of them binds, so the three still agree; the refusal code is unchanged (`HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE`).
